## Dev Gotchas

### A genre model that imports `sidequest.dungeon.*` at module top closes an import CYCLE — `dungeon/__init__` eagerly loads materializer; a genre-tier model is loaded during `game.persistence` init (2026-07-10, 164-6)
- Task 10's `SiteArchetype` needs `ALGORITHMS` (∈ `sidequest.dungeon.interiors`) to validate `interior_algorithm`. Importing it at module top (`from sidequest.dungeon.interiors import ALGORITHMS`) passed a naive `python -c` check but **broke the full pytest collection** with `ImportError: cannot import name 'DatabaseError' from partially initialized module 'sidequest.game.persistence'`. The cycle: `game/__init__ → game.persistence → game.session → game.chassis → genre/__init__ → genre.models.pack` (which I made import `site_archetype`) `→ sidequest.dungeon.interiors → dungeon/__init__.py` (line 3: `from sidequest.dungeon.materializer import ...`) `→ materializer → dungeon.persistence → game.persistence` (mid-init). Genre-tier models are pulled in DURING `game.persistence` module init, so ANY genre model reaching into `sidequest.dungeon` at load time closes the loop. **Fix: lazy-import inside the validator** (`def _known_algorithm(cls, v): from sidequest.dungeon.interiors import ALGORITHMS; ...`) — a SiteArchetype is only built well after all modules load, so deferring the import breaks the genre→dungeon module-load edge with zero behavior change. **Rule: never import `sidequest.dungeon.*` (or anything whose package `__init__` pulls materializer) at the top of a `genre/models/*` file — lazy-import in the method that needs it. And `python -c "import X"` is NOT a cycle check: import order masks cycles; the real test is `uv run pytest --co -q` (collection).**
- **The materializer had a SECOND cycle contributor:** it imported `DEFAULT_SITE_ID` from `sidequest.game.pg.dungeon` (the concrete PG impl, which chains to `game.persistence`). Fix: relocated `DEFAULT_SITE_ID = "frontier"` to the **protocol** module `sidequest.game.repository` (a runtime leaf — its heavy imports are all under `TYPE_CHECKING`), re-exported from `pg.dungeon` for back-compat (`from ...repository import DEFAULT_SITE_ID` keeps `__all__` + the existing `test_dungeon_site_keying` import working). materializer already imported `game.repository`, so this removed the `materializer → game.pg` edge entirely. **Rule: a shared low-level constant belongs on the protocol/leaf module, not the concrete impl — importing a constant from `game.pg.*` drags the whole persistence chain and cycles.**

### `ROOMCORRIDOR_MIN_DIM = 25` — a SiteArchetype's `grid_width/grid_height` are the TACTICAL grid, NOT the interior-generation canvas; and the interior ALGORITHM comes from the theme, not the archetype (2026-07-10, 164-6)
- The plan (task 11) said "use `archetype.grid_width/grid_height` in place of `DEFAULT_INTERIOR_WIDTH/HEIGHT`." But a tavern archetype is `roomcorridor` at 15×20, and `materializer.ROOMCORRIDOR_MIN_DIM = 25` fails LOUD below 25 — threading the archetype dims into interior gen crashes materialize. Deeper: `_stage_fill` reads the interior algorithm from `palette.themes[node.theme].interior.algorithm` (the THEME's `InteriorSpec`), NOT from `archetype.interior_algorithm`. So the archetype's grid/algorithm fields describe the **tactical combat grid** (feet-per-cell display, Task 13/B4 rendering), a different concern from the maze-generation canvas (`DEFAULT_INTERIOR_*=49`). **Kept interior gen at the default; did NOT add `interior_width/height` to the request.** `bounded_site` drives materialize purely via the passed palette + `archetype.room_count_max` (burst). Logged as a deviation.
- **Bounded-site seed must fit signed BIGINT:** `campaign_seed` is `BIGINT` and the frontier bootstrap draws `secrets.randbits(63)`. A derived per-site seed `int.from_bytes(blake2b(...,digest_size=8).digest(),"big")` is up to 2⁶⁴ → `DatabaseError: bigint out of range`. Mask to 63 bits: `& ((1 << 63) - 1)`. Match the existing `_SEED_BITS=63` convention.
- **Bounded sites write NO frontier** (`lookahead_breadth=0`): gate the frontier derivation (`new_frontier = _new_frontier_edges(...) if request.lookahead_breadth > 0 else []`) — `_new_frontier_edges` otherwise makes one edge per new node regardless. This makes `load_frontier(site_id)==[]` hold and means `put_frontier`/frontier `site_id` threading is unreachable for sites (I reverted it — it only broke an existing `_boom_put_frontier` mock for zero gain). `open_thread` site_id was also reverted: the base `DungeonStore.open_thread` has no `site_id` param (only `PgDungeonTransaction` does), so threading it broke `TestStageAttach`; a bounded site's complication threads land under `DEFAULT_SITE_ID` (latent gap, non-failing — flagged as a delivery finding). Thread `site_id` ONLY into `commit_expansion` + `record_mutation` (which the real tx accepts) + the `materialize` introspection (`load_map`/`load_frontier`), and default `MaterializationRequest.site_id` to `DEFAULT_SITE_ID` so every frontier caller is unchanged.
### Per-region weather re-sample crashes the turn when the new zone lacks the bootstrap season; the Task-20 plan mis-tagged a village kirk as upland — verify plan content against the authored world (2026-07-10, 163-6)
- **Latent turn-crash (pre-review Critical).** `WeatherGenerator.generate(zone, season, seed)` raises `UnknownWeatherSeason` (a `KeyError` subclass, exported from `sidequest.game.weather` alongside `UnknownWeatherZone`) when the target zone doesn't define the requested season. Weather A2 caches ONE bootstrap season per session (`sd.weather_season`) and re-samples it against whatever zone the party enters on a region change — so a world whose zones don't all share that season crashes the whole turn (the region-change block is NOT inside the broadcast phase's `except Exception` guard; it propagates to `websocket.py`'s safety net → discards the turn's outbound queue → generic "Server error"). The task-18 validator (`_validate_weather_zones`) only checks the zone EXISTS, never that it shares the session season, so pack validation passes and it's a live-only crash. **Fix pattern: contain at the emit-helper boundary** — `try: regenerate_weather_for_region(...) except (UnknownWeatherZone, UnknownWeatherSeason): _watcher_publish("weather.zone_change_skipped", {reason}, component="location", severity="warning"); return`. Loud + observable (GM panel sees the skip), never a silent default, never a turn crash. glenross survives only by coincidence (both its zones define all four seasons).
- **The Track A plan's Task 20 content list mis-tagged `the_kirk_of_st_maelrubha` as `highland_pass` "(upland)".** The authored world says otherwise: summary "the parish kirk on its mound", description "a low mound at the village", `terrain: building`, adjacent ONLY to glen_floor village regions (never touches castle_ross / the_long_pass, the genuine uplands). Blindly annotating per the plan ships an incongruous blizzard on a pub→kirk stroll. **Rule (companion to the 163-5 "plans reference things that don't exist" entry): before applying a plan's content-annotation list, read each named entity's authored description/adjacency and reconcile — the plan's geography labels can be wrong.** Retagged glen_floor; highland_pass = castle_ross + the_long_pass only. Logged as a Dev deviation.
- **Threading a new value out of `load_world_grounding` (24-10):** extend the frozen `WorldGroundingBootstrap` dataclass with DEFAULTED fields (`weather_generator`/`weather_season` `= None`) and populate them in the one constructor call — existing 3-positional-arg / kwarg callers (`tests/genre/test_genre_flavor_world_tier.py`) stay green because the new fields default. Cleaner than reconstructing the generator in `connect.py`. Adding an optional `cartography: Any | None = None` kwarg is likewise backward-compatible.

### The mapping plans' UI reference impls style with CSS vars that DON'T EXIST in sidequest-ui — grep the token before transcribing, and substitute the sibling-component idiom (2026-07-10, 163-5)
- Track A task 13's RasterMap impl uses `var(--text-muted)` and `var(--surface-raised)` — neither is defined anywhere in the repo (repo-wide grep; theme vars are set at runtime by useGenreTheme, so a bogus var renders DEAD styling silently — no build error, no test failure since tests pin testids not tokens). Real idiom: `text-muted-foreground/60` (MapWidget empty-state), `var(--surface-2)`/`var(--surface-3)`/`var(--line)`/`var(--text)`/`var(--accent)` (CavernActionPanel). **Rule: for any plan-transcribed UI styling, grep each `var(--x)` for a definition or an existing consumer before shipping it; substitute the sibling component's tokens and log a minor deviation.** Same story: 163-5 GREEN was a clean plan transcription otherwise — TEA's ground-truth sweep (props/testids/guard placement all verified in RED) meant zero implementation surprises; the only two deviations were this token swap + stable route keys (`${from_id}-${to_id}-${name}`, closing TEA's lang-review #6 directive).
- **Baseline-tsc discipline paid off again (matches the 80-1 entry):** `tsc -b` on the branch showed 1 error in an UNTOUCHED Fate test file; `git stash -u` → checkout develop → `tsc -b` showed the SAME 1 error → pre-existing, logged as a finding, not owned. The enforced UI gate remains vitest + eslint; check `tsc` delta-vs-baseline, not absolute zero.

### 165-4 rework GREEN: the melee-sentinel range band, the ephemeral-vs-routed OTEL choice, a stash-pop lost to a Bash timeout, and a pre-existing broken full-suite (2026-07-09, 165-4)
- **The dice-result range echo's two fields are INDEPENDENT — wire them separately.** `range_band` comes from `resolve_weapon_range_band_from_beat_and_actor` (`combat_rules.py`), resolved at `dispatch/dice.py:851` BEFORE and regardless of the grid gate; `distance_cells` comes from the `_enforce_tactical_reach` verdict, which is None when the gate skips (no grid / unseated). Capture both into function-scoped vars (init to None before the reach `if`-block) and thread through `_compose_result_payload` (add two optional kwargs) onto the CHECK result at the compose call. A both-or-neither fix leaves every grid-less strike bandless.
- **`resolve_weapon_range_band_from_beat_and_actor` returns None as the MELEE SENTINEL, not "no data".** It returns the ranged weapon's SRD band string, or None for a melee/natural/unarmed strike (no catalog item with a `range_band`). `WithoutNumberRulesetModule.weapon_range_cells` treats `None == "melee"`. So to echo a band on EVERY combat strike (the story goal — the resolution card must show the math for melee too), default `_tactical_range_band = _resolved_band or "melee"`; pass the RAW `_resolved_band` (None) to the gate spec so gate behavior is unchanged. The TEA harness `_make_attacker` wields a synthetic melee blade NOT in the heavy_metal catalog → resolver returns None → the tests exercise the "melee" default; the ranged-band string path is wired but untested (logged as a Delivery Finding). Don't read a melee `range_band is not None` test as "the resolver produces a band" — it produces None and the DEFAULT produces the band.
- **A move-summary-style OTEL decision should emit via `_watcher_publish` (ephemeral watcher event), NOT a `Span.open`+`SPAN_ROUTES` route — pick by the SIBLINGS.** The two siblings in `_maybe_build_runtime_cavern_payload` (`tactical_grid.tactical_missing`, `.runtime_render_skipped`) call `_watcher_publish("tactical_grid.X", {...}, component=, severity=)` directly. Match them: `_watcher_publish("tactical_grid.move_summary", {capable, adjudication_count, …})`. Ephemeral events need NO `SPAN_ROUTES`/`FLAT_ONLY_SPANS` entry — `test_routing_completeness._all_span_constants()` only enumerates `SPAN_*` constants (Span.open names), so `tactical_grid.*` strings are invisible to it and can't trip it. (The carryover "new OTEL spans need a SPAN_ROUTES entry" applies to `Span.open` spans, NOT ephemeral `_watcher_publish` events.) Fire it on BOTH the engaged and the skip paths so the panel distinguishes them (`capable` bool + `adjudication_count`).
- **CRITICAL git gotcha — a Bash timeout can strand your working tree in a stash.** A one-liner that runs a long job then `git stash pop` at the end will SKIP the pop if the job hits the Bash tool's 2-min timeout (exit 143). I wrapped a full-suite baseline as `git stash push …; pytest …; BASELINE=$(pytest …); git stash pop` — the SECOND (command-substitution) pytest run timed out before `git stash pop`, leaving all my GREEN edits in `stash@{0}` and a CLEAN working tree. The next `git commit` said "nothing to commit"; nearly committed an empty GREEN. ALWAYS: (a) put `git stash pop` in a SEPARATE Bash call from any long-running work, (b) after any stash-wrapped baseline run, `git stash list` + grep a signature line in the changed file before trusting the tree, (c) recovery is just `git stash pop` (the changes aren't lost).
- **The full server suite is PRE-EXISTING broken on develop (~64 failures) — stash-compare to distinguish YOUR regressions from the debt.** All ~57-64 failures are one signature: `pydantic ValidationError` building `CartographyTreatmentWire(kind=<MagicMock>, …)` at `session_helpers.py:1695` (`_build_cartography_map_message` via `_maybe_emit_cartography_map`) — full-turn wiring tests whose MagicMock `world` doesn't satisfy a newer strict Track B cartography model. The team runs SCOPED tests (story + adjacent), so nobody runs the 14k-suite and this rots invisibly. To verify a HOT-path change (dice dispatch) added no regressions: run the full suite with your files STASHED (baseline count) vs restored — mine went 64→57 (fixed the 6 story REDs, added zero). A raw "57 failed" from testing-runner is NOT evidence you broke something.
- **testing-runner's failure COUNT + NAMES are trustworthy; its ROOT-CAUSE narrative is a guess.** My Engineering Console confidently diagnosed "the fail-loud wiring now validates cartography treatment objects" — but I never touched cartography; the 57 were pre-existing. (Same lesson as the TEA sidecar's "inferred failure REASONS are not evidence.") Verify causation yourself with a stash-compare.

### Registering a `sites/` resolver in `seams/registry.py` closes a seams↔sites import cycle — register LAZILY (2026-07-08, 164-2)
- The Track B site resolvers live in `sidequest/game/sites/{enter_site,exit_site}.py` and import `from sidequest.game.seams.base import SeamCrossingError, SeamCrossingResult`. A module-level `from sidequest.game.sites.enter_site import resolve_enter_site` in `seams/registry.py` closes a cycle: `registry → sites.enter_site → seams.base → seams/__init__ (imports registry) → registry` (partially-initialized `enter_site`). It bites in EVERY import order (sites-first via `sites/__init__`, seams-first via `registry`, or module-direct), because `seams/__init__.py` eagerly imports `registry`. Symptom: `ImportError: cannot import name 'resolve_enter_site' from partially initialized module`.
- **Fix: lazy-register.** Keep `_REGISTRY` with only the intra-`seams` resolvers (deep_descent) at module load; add `_ensure_site_resolvers()` that does the `from sidequest.game.sites... import ...` INSIDE the function and `_REGISTRY.update({...})` (atomic — never leave it half-populated), and call it at the top of `get_seam_resolver()`. The site kinds are dispatched BY KIND (not by a route `to_id`), so nothing on the module-load path needs them in `_REGISTRY` — deferring to first lookup (after both packages finish importing) is behavior-neutral. Verify with a 3-order import probe: `uv run python -c "import sidequest.game.sites; from sidequest.game.seams.registry import get_seam_resolver; from sidequest.game.sites.enter_site import resolve_enter_site; print(get_seam_resolver('enter_site') is resolve_enter_site)"` and the seams-first + module-direct variants. (This is the same lazy-import-to-dodge-a-cycle pattern as `subsystems/__init__._register_defaults` and the emitters function-local imports.)
- **A NEW `spans/<domain>.py` must be star-imported into `spans/__init__.py`** (`from .<domain> import *  # noqa: F401, F403`, alphabetical) or its `SPAN_*` constants are invisible to `tests/telemetry/test_routing_completeness.py` (which scans `vars(spans)`), so a future unrouted span in that module would NOT trip the routing-completeness lint. `spans/site.py` shipped without the line in the first RED→GREEN pass (caught in pre-review). `spans/site.py` imports only opentelemetry + `watcher_hub` + `._core`/`.span` (no `game.*`), so star-importing it into the package is cycle-free — same as `movement`.
