### Global-OTEL-provider test isolation: restore the PROCESSOR TUPLE, never the provider reference — and the shared provider must be installed session-wide, not leaked into existence (2026-07-08, 158-55)
- Root cause of a rotating, multi-subsystem OTEL-span-assertion flake (2-8 failures/run under `-n auto --dist load`): `otel_capture` (and the `_attach_exporter` pattern) call `get_tracer_provider().add_span_processor(...)` on the SHARED global provider and only `processor.shutdown()` on teardown — a shut-down processor is **never removed** from `SynchronousMultiSpanProcessor._span_processors`, so processors ACCUMULATE per xdist worker; a later OTEL test sees dozens of stale processors (+ a dead `BatchSpanProcessor`→:4317 whose `force_flush` hangs the 30 s budget). Whichever OTEL test shared a worker with the leaker failed → the victim set rotated.
- **The fix is a root-conftest autouse pair, and the FIRST cut was wrong in an instructive way:** resetting `trace._TRACER_PROVIDER` + the `_TRACER_PROVIDER_SET_ONCE` guard per test (the `tests/dungeon/conftest.py` swap-restore pattern) **broke the suite worse (1110 errors)** — `init_tracer()` has its OWN `_initialized` module-guard, so nulling only the OTEL once-guard made `init_tracer` early-return and never re-install → siblings that merely `assert isinstance(get_tracer_provider(), TracerProvider)` got a `ProxyTracerProvider`. The correct design: (1) a **session-scoped autouse** that calls `init_tracer()` once (provider present for all tests, no leak-ordering) and pops a stray `SIDEQUEST_OTLP_ENDPOINT` (dead collector → `force_flush` hang); (2) a **function-scoped autouse** that snapshots `_active_span_processor._span_processors` (an immutable tuple rebound on each add, so the bare ref is a valid snapshot) and reassigns it on teardown — stripping per-test additions while leaving the provider reference untouched. Module/session-scoped capture processors survive because the snapshot is taken AFTER higher-scope fixtures set up. Result: 6/8 randomized runs fully clean, rotating cast gone.
- **`SIDEQUEST_OTLP_ENDPOINT` set in a dev shell (local Jaeger) silently poisons the unit suite:** `init_tracer` installs a live OTLP `BatchSpanProcessor`, and with no collector on the port `force_flush` HANGS against `--timeout=30`. Neutralize it for the unit session (the OTLP-path tests set it themselves per-test via monkeypatch). If you see `Transient error StatusCode.UNAVAILABLE ... localhost:4317` + `force_flush` timeouts, check your shell env before blaming the code.

### A rotating suite flake can have MULTIPLE independent causes — fix the dominant one, then the residual is a DIFFERENT bug; don't assume one fix makes it fully green (2026-07-08, 158-55)
- After the OTEL-provider fix removed the wide rotating cast, ONE residual flake remained: `test_59_23_materialize_other::test_ship_combat_materialized_threat_resolves_on_hull` (~25%). It passes 10/10 isolated, passes its whole file, passes on serial-dispatch RETRY — i.e. **non-deterministic, not fixed-order pollution.** Signature points at `PYTHONHASHSEED`-dependent set/dict iteration in the `npcs_present=[]` opponent-seating fallback (`_npc_fallback_at_location` / roster reconciliation, `encounter_lifecycle.py`): the named threat is intermittently not seated as the Other (a crew member conscripted), cascading to the hull assertion. **Lesson: when a "make the suite deterministic" fix leaves a stable single residual, characterize whether it's (a) the same pollution (fix incomplete) or (b) a separate non-deterministic bug (different subsystem, own story). Bisect deterministically first (serial `-n0`, whole-file, N-predecessors) — if it passes serially on retry, it's a race/hash-order flake, not order pollution.**

### Track B mapping plan (2026-07-08) has a migration-revision-id bug + a fixture gotcha; and re-keying dungeon PKs forces FULL site_id threading, not partial (2026-07-08, 164-1)
- **Alembic revision ids are SHORT numeric, not descriptive.** The Track B plan's `0003` migration wrote `revision = "0003_dungeon_site_id"` / `down_revision = "0002_asset_ledger"`. But the actual files use `revision: str = "0001"`, `"0002"` — the descriptive part is only the FILENAME (`0002_asset_ledger.py`), the revision id is `"0002"`. Using `down_revision = "0002_asset_ledger"` makes alembic fail "Can't locate revision '0002_asset_ledger'". Correct: `revision = "0003"`, `down_revision = "0002"` (filename can stay `0003_dungeon_site_id.py`). Verify by grepping `^revision` in the parent file before writing any migration.
- **`Region` requires `summary` AND `description` (both `str`, no default) — `world.py:205-207`.** The plan's SiteRegistry test fixture built regions as `{"name": ..., "adjacent": [...]}` and `CartographyConfig.model_validate` rejected all of them ("Field required: summary/description"). Any cartography fixture must supply name+summary+description per region. The registry only reads `region.adjacent`, so a `_region(name, adjacent)` helper filling summary/description with the name keeps fixtures terse + valid.
- **Re-keying a table's PK forces coherent threading of EVERY writer/reader, not just the tested ones.** `0003` re-keys all six dungeon tables' PKs to include `site_id`. If you thread `site_id` only through `commit_expansion`/`load_map` (what the tests touch), `put_frontier` breaks (its `ON CONFLICT (session_id, frontier_edge_id)` no longer matches the new `(session_id, site_id, frontier_edge_id)` PK → "no unique constraint matching ON CONFLICT"), and `set_campaign_seed`'s per-site write-once check silently corrupts (a 2nd site can't seed). This is AC-10 ("all signatures updated consistently") + CLAUDE.md "no half-wired" — thread `site_id` through the whole `PgDungeonRepository`/`PgDungeonTransaction` surface (WHERE, INSERT cols, ON CONFLICT, and the seed get-check). The `dungeon.py` methods delegate repo→transaction, so pass `site_id=site_id` in the delegates too.
- **Verifying the DB path locally: Postgres is already running** (`pg_isready` → `/tmp:5432`), `sidequest_test` DB exists (owner `slabgorb`). Export `SIDEQUEST_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://slabgorb@localhost:5432/sidequest_test` and the `migrated_db` fixture spins throwaway DBs per run. Don't accept skipped DB tests as GREEN — wire the URL and make them actually run. For migration up/down symmetry (AC-5), drive `alembic.command.upgrade/downgrade` on a throwaway DB (mirror `tests/dungeon/conftest.py::migrated_db`).

### Track C plan (2026-07-08): the plan's `cells_reachable` IMPL and its own TEST disagree on cost-map semantics — the impl is under-baked; the test wins (2026-07-08, 165-1)
- Story 165-1 transcribed the Track C plan's Task-1/2/3 code verbatim → 18/19 green, but `test_reachable_difficult_terrain_doubles_cost` failed `KeyError: (3,1)`. Cause: the plan's `cells_reachable` gates `cost[n] = nc` on `nc <= budget` (so over-budget cells never enter `ReachResult.cost`), yet the plan's own test asserts `r.cost[(3,1)] == 3` for an over-budget cell while requiring `(3,1) not in r.reachable`. Plan impl ≠ plan test — a SECOND internal inconsistency in this plan (TEA already caught a mask bug in the same Task-2 test; this one is algorithmic, not mask, so it survived TEA's cell-by-cell pass).
- **Fix (minimal, test-wins):** split the guard — record `cost[n] = nc` on every Dijkstra relaxation (the immediate over-budget frontier lands in the cost map), append to the frontier only when `nc <= budget`, and filter `reachable = frozenset(c for c in cost if c != origin and cost[c] <= budget)`. Semantics the test pins: `cost` = full min-cost map incl. the boundary cell one step past budget; `reachable` = budget-filtered. Verified the other 3 `cells_reachable` tests still pass. **The plan doc (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-08-mapping-track-c-tactical-mechanics.md`, Task 2 ~lines 262–289) still carries the under-baked impl — siblings 165-2..165-5 reuse this library, so correct the doc or expect the same KeyError.**
- **Two ruff fixes the plan's C1 code needs** (this repo flags them as errors, so they fail the quality gate): `zip(path, path[1:])` → add `strict=False` (B905); `line_of_sight`'s `for c in ray[1:-1]: if not is_floor: return False / return True` → `return all(is_floor(rows, c) for c in ray[1:-1])` (SIM110). Both byte-identical behavior. Lint the touched files (`uv run ruff check sidequest/game/tactical/ tests/game/tactical/`) before committing — don't discover it at the gate.

### An understudy "perception fidelity" fix is a cross-repo UI-accessibility fix, NOT a detector-regex tweak — the detector reads the REAL aria tree, so the fix belongs where the aria is emitted (2026-07-07, 162-11)
- The `two_names_one_enemy` detector (understudy findings/detect.py) was "inert in production" not because its regexes were wrong but because the live sidequest-ui emitted no `region "Enemies"`, no `listitem` foes, and no `role="log"` narration — so Playwright's aria_snapshot never carried the tokens it greps for. **The load-bearing fix is in sidequest-ui (add the ARIA roles), reconciled with a small detector change for how Playwright NESTS text.** Two concrete aria_snapshot facts that bit:
  1. **`role="log"` with prose in a child `<p>` renders NESTED:** `- log:` (empty) then `- paragraph: <text>` on a deeper-indented line. A detector that only matched inline `log: <text>` got empty narration → returned None. Fix: read child lines of a bare `log:` opener as prose (see `_narration`/`_node_text` in detect.py). Keep the inline form working — both occur.
  2. **A `listitem` containing a portrait chip + name span renders as `- listitem: T Thief`** — the ActorChip fallback initial ("T") leaks into the accessible name and contaminates the foe label (would false-positive the consistent-naming case). Fix UI-side: `aria-hidden` the decorative portrait + add an `sr-only` clean name span, so aria_snapshot yields `- listitem: Thief`. (Don't sanitize the label in the detector — fix it at the source; the portrait genuinely IS decorative.)
- **Verify with real Playwright, not jsdom:** jsdom has no `aria_snapshot`. To see what the bot actually perceives, drive a throwaway `page.locator('body').aria_snapshot()` over the fixture (or the real app). The understudy wiring test (`run_table` over a file:// fixture) exercises the true perceive()→aria_snapshot→detector path — that's the GREEN proof, the component-level `getByRole` tests only prove the roles render.
- **Scope discipline that held:** the roster (`dial-scoreboard`) groups allies+opponents; the "Enemies" region must wrap the OPPONENT group ONLY (`group.every(a => a.side === "opponent")`) — a region labelled Enemies containing the player is both an a11y lie and breaks the detector's "exactly one foe" count.
- **REVIEW OVERTURNED my "leave the fixture emitting T Thief, it proves robustness" call (rework R1).** A "faithful reproduction" fixture that omits an a11y attribute the component SHIPS (here `aria-hidden` on the portrait) makes the wiring test pass for the WRONG reason and leaves the clean-name property untested cross-repo. **Rule: a fixture claiming to reproduce real DOM must carry the same a11y attributes the component emits — diff the fixture's aria_snapshot against the component's, don't rationalize the drift as "extra coverage."** The fix: add `aria-hidden` to the fixture (aria_snapshot then yields clean `listitem: Thief`) + a CONSISTENT-naming companion fixture whose wiring test false-forks (fails) if the fixture drifts back.
- **The nested-`log:` parser I added had a latent silent-fallback the review caught:** `_LOG = r"\blog\s*:\s*(.+)$"` — `(.+)` matches a whitespace-only remainder, so a `log:` opener with a trailing space matched the INLINE branch with an empty capture and dropped the nested prose → detector returned None on a valid fork (the exact bug the story fixes). Fix: `r"\blog\s*:\s*(\S.*)$"` (require a non-whitespace char) so the bare-opener regex reliably wins. **When two regexes race (inline-with-content vs bare-opener), make the content one require content (`\S`), or the greedy `.` will steal whitespace-only lines.** Also: `_node_text` must return `""` for a bare `- role:` node, else the role token ("paragraph:") leaks into the narration string.
- **role="listitem" needs a role="list" parent (WCAG 1.3.1).** Wrap the foe listitems in `<span role="list" className="flex items-center gap-1.5">` — same flex container, so layout is byte-identical; the detector's region-scan tolerates the intermediate `- list:` line. Mutation-verify the aria-hidden guard by deleting the attribute and re-running the one test (it must go red) — a guard you didn't watch fail is a guard you can't trust.

### Wiring a NEW optional pack attribute at the confrontation frame supplier breaks every duck-typed `_FakeGenrePack` — use `getattr(pack, "attr", None)`, not direct access (2026-07-07, 158-56)
- Adding `mutation_catalog=genre_pack.mutations` to the `build_confrontation_payload` call inside `_frame_for` (confrontation.py) regressed 4 previously-green tests: `test_confrontation_stakes_portrait_payload.py` + `test_wwn_cast_spell_wiring.py` both build a duck-typed `_FakeGenrePack` (NOT a real `GenrePack`) that has `.classes`/`.rules` but not `.mutations` → `AttributeError: '_FakeGenrePack' object has no attribute 'mutations'`. The adjacent `rules=genre_pack.rules` gets away with DIRECT access because those fakes DO model `.rules` (it's required/load-bearing). **For an OPTIONAL pack field (`.mutations`, None on non-AWN packs), use `getattr(genre_pack, "mutations", None)` — it's semantically correct (optional → maybe-absent) AND matches the sibling mutation-path accessors (`narration_apply.py:589`, `magic_working.py:130` both getattr). A real GenrePack always has the declared field, so production is byte-identical; the getattr default only ever fires for a test fake.** Not a silent fallback: no real config drift is masked (the field is pydantic-declared). Chose getattr over adding `mutations=None` to two fakes (less invasive, convention-matching).
- **The verification that caught it:** the full-suite run flagged the 4 as failures but a subagent CLAIMED "no regression in confrontation.py paths." NEVER trust that — my sidecar's own rule. The `git stash push <my files> && pytest && git stash pop` isolation proved it (23 pass without my diff, 4 fail with). WATCH the stash: the companion tests take ~66s serially, and my stash-check TIMED OUT mid-pop, leaving my changes stashed — always `git stash list` + grep for a signature string after any stash dance to confirm the pop landed.

### `confrontation-wiring.test.tsx` is a SOURCE-TEXT regex wiring test — widening `onBeatSelect`/`handleBeatSelect` breaks its `readFileSync`+`toMatch` patterns; widen the regex (2026-07-07, 158-56)
- Adding a `mutationId?` slot to the overlay/GameBoard/App `onBeatSelect` chain (the direct mirror of how `spell_id` was bolted on) broke 2 assertions in `src/__tests__/confrontation-wiring.test.tsx`: `/onBeatSelect\?\.\(beatId,\s*draft,\s*spellId\)/` (now `...spellId, mutationId)`) and the `handleBeatSelect` signature regex `(beatId: string, playerAction?: string, spellId?: string)`. These `readFileSync(GameBoard.tsx/App.tsx) + toMatch(/.../)` tests are the CLAUDE.md "No Source-Text Wiring Tests" anti-pattern — they pass on the literal, not the behavior, and break on any harmless signature refactor. Fix = widen the regex to allow the optional new param: `/...spellId(,\s*mutationId)?\)/`, `/...spellId\?:\s*string(,\s*mutationId\?:\s*string)?\s*\)/`. Flag them as a Delivery Finding for behavioral rewrite; the FUNCTIONAL wiring is already covered by the real GameBoard-prop-trap wiring test (`mutation-throw-wiring-158-56.test.tsx`), so these source-greps are pure debt.

### The mutation picker IS the spell picker, one economy over — mirror `spellcasting` at EVERY layer, invent nothing (2026-07-07, 158-56)
- Server: `ConfrontationPayload.mutation_economy: dict|None` (twin of `spellcasting`, extra="forbid" declared) ← `build_confrontation_payload` projects `{owned:[{id,name,strain_cost}]}` from `snapshot.mutation_state.characters[actor].positive_ids` joined via `pack.mutations.positive_by_id`, scoped to `recipient_actor_name`, None when no state/catalog/owned (never a fabricated empty economy). Wired at the per-recipient `_frame_for` supplier ONLY — the 3 union call sites (`recipient_pc=None` in dice.py×2 + websocket_session_handler) deliberately carry no per-recipient economy (the supplier re-projects per socket), exactly as `spellcasting` behaves. UI: `MutationPicker` (SpellPicker twin, `data-testid`/`data-mutation-id`/`data-strain-cost`), detect via `beat.mutation_resolution` boolean (already projected — beats `model_dump`'d wholesale at confrontation.py:323), defer-and-refuse on missing economy, `mutationId` in its OWN callback slot (spellId undefined), latched in `pendingMutationIdRef` and conditional-spread into DICE_THROW beside `spell_id` at App.tsx. Every layer had a `spell_id`/`spellcasting` precedent line to copy — grep the codebase for the spell twin before writing any new picker line.

### "Adopt `resolve_roster_npc`" ≠ call it everywhere — the mention path is richer, the pool is a different type; reuse its PRIMITIVE (`normalize_name`) instead (2026-07-06, 162-10)
- **`resolve_roster_npc(snapshot.npcs, name)` (game/origin.py) matches canonical + alias + invented_from with ONE `normalize_name` (fold_to_ascii + casefold + whitespace-collapse). But it does NOT do comma-inversion or leading-"The" folding** — the `_apply_npc_mentions` seam (narration_apply.py) DOES (via `_npc_name_match_keys`). So you cannot swap the mention legs for `resolve_roster_npc` wholesale — you'd lose the article/comma tests (test_npc_article_fold_match, test_npc_comma_inversion_match). The correct adoption: (a) build `_npc_name_match_keys` through `normalize_name` instead of bare `.casefold()` (adds diacritic folding, identical for ASCII single-space names), and (b) add an explicit alias leg (`any(_npc_name_match_keys(alias) & mention_keys for alias in npc.aliases)`) — the mention path never checked `npc.aliases` before. That reuses the resolver's normalization primitive without a parallel resolver ("Don't Reinvent").
- **The seeder pool-member legs can't call `resolve_roster_npc` at all** — pool members are `NpcPoolMember`, not `Npc`; the fn signature is `Sequence[Npc]`. Cut `m.name == actor.name` over to `normalize_name(m.name) == normalize_name(actor.name)` (the shared primitive) — both the Fate seeder (`_seed_fate_opponents`) and hp_depletion seeder (`_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs`) have this leg; fix BOTH (No Half-Wired).
- **Honest telemetry when you add a fold:** a diacritic-only reconciliation was mislabeling as `comma_normalized` in `_reconciliation_form` (the fallback). Add a `diacritic_normalized` branch (`fold_to_ascii(a).casefold() == fold_to_ascii(b).casefold()`) — the GM panel is the lie-detector; a fold you introduce must be labeled for what it is.

### When you canonicalize a SEAT on one resolution branch, grep for EVERY sibling branch that resolves-then-seats and apply it there too — the pool-promotion/fallback branch is the one you'll miss (2026-07-06, 162-10 — caused a review REJECT)
- I adopted `resolve_roster_npc`/`normalize_name` at the seeders and canonicalized `actor.name = npc.core.name` on the ROSTER-hit branch (`npc is not None`) — but the seeders have a SECOND seating branch, pool-promotion (`npc is None` → promote an `NpcPoolMember`), which also resolves-then-seats and which I left un-canonicalized. `normalize_name` let a variant `actor.name` match a canonically-named pool member and promote it, but `find_creature_core` is exact-match, so the seat was unreachable → brick. The reviewer rejected on exactly this. **Fix pattern:** after EVERY `snapshot.npcs.append(npc)` in a seating function, `if npc.core.name != actor.name: actor.name = npc.core.name`. Before shipping a seat-canonicalization, `grep -n "snapshot.npcs.append" <file>` and confirm each append that seats an opponent rewrites the seat.
- **Test smell that would have caught it earlier:** the RED test asserted the right entity was promoted (`not ephemeral`, right `core.name`) but never `find_creature_core(actor.name) is not None`. Identity-correct ≠ reachable. Demand a reachability assertion on any seating test.

### "Pre-rename telemetry" fixes belong at actor-BUILD time, not just in the seeder — one canonicalization covers the span AND the exact-match presence lookup (2026-07-06, 162-10)
- The seeder (`_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs`, L425) canonicalizes `actor.name` — but `participant.joined` + the init span's `combatant_names` fire EARLIER in `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` (the seeder runs ~250 lines later at ~L2498). So the GM panel saw the alias join and the canonical name fight. Fix: canonicalize the seat name where the `EncounterActor` is BUILT (the `for npc in npcs_present:` loop), via `resolve_roster_npc` (rewrite only when it returns non-None and the name differs — PC names resolve to None and are left alone). Bonus: the presence-stamp block right after uses `_npc_by_name = {n.core.name: n}` EXACT-match on `actor.name` — canonicalizing at build time fixes that silent-miss too. NOT a double `identity.resolved` span: after canonicalization the name is canonical, so the seeder's later `resolve_roster_npc` hits the silent canonical leg.

### `dataclasses.replace` for a "rebind one field, keep the rest" on a dataclass — manual field-copy silently drops fields added later (2026-07-06, 162-10)
- The head-check rebind built a fresh `NpcMention(name=canonical, pronouns=..., role=..., appearance=..., side=...)` — dropping `is_new/is_creature/disengaged/is_place` (added post-hoc). `replace(materialized_threat, name=known.core.name)` preserves everything. `from dataclasses import replace`. Reach for `replace` on ANY "change one field of a frozen-ish struct" — the manual constructor is a forward landmine every time a field is added to the dataclass.

### Turning a fabrication fallback into fail-loud exposes SANCTIONED consumers of the fallback — hunt for them by SCALE, not by test file (2026-07-05, 162-3)
- **The stub-mint wasn't only debt — two by-design paths rode it:** (1) the ADR-153 §6 sealed-letter frame-default seat (a dogfight's Other IS the def frame; the mint was its instantiation mechanism), and (2) space_opera `ship_combat` — a NON-sealed-letter hp_depletion combat whose `opponent_default_stats` is a calibrated SHIP HULL (hp 30). The 162-3 generics rung silently seated a humanoid `void_drifter` (hp 4) as that hull — caught only by `test_59_23_materialize_other`'s hull pin. Nothing machine-readable distinguished vehicle-scale from entity-scale; fix = new typed `ConfrontationDef.opponent_source: Literal["bestiary","frame"] = "bestiary"` + `opponent_source: frame` on ship_combat, with sealed-letter implicitly frame. When you kill a fallback, grep for defs whose frame stats are NOT entity-scale (hull-sized hp is the tell) before assuming every consumer is degenerate test debt.
- **Restore-on-refusal at the entrypoint:** `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` sets `snapshot.encounter = enc` BEFORE the hp_depletion seeder runs (the 158-31 exception-residue trap), so the new raise needed `except ValueError: snapshot.encounter = current; raise` at the call site — `current` (the entry-time encounter) is still correctly bound there. Without it the refusal half-seats and the TEA contract (`snap.encounter is None` after raise) fails.
- **The flip-set sweep was 132 failures → 4 funnels:** `_wn_round_102_4.seat_wn_combat` (one line fixed ~75), `tests/_helpers/trigger_encounter.py` (the helper I missed on round 1 — it lacked the `genre_slug=`-line shape my insertion script keyed on because it was never in the FILES list; ALWAYS grep helpers/ before scripting a sweep), the two space_opera `_seated_combat` copies, and per-file direct calls. Script keyed on "insert after the `genre_slug=` line inside an `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger(` block" worked cleanly for 30 call sites; `tests/game/test_materialize_armor_class.py` surfaced only in the FULL suite (it lives in tests/game/, outside every "seating" directory I ran) — the full-suite pass is not optional on a seam this wide.
- **`test_pregen_bestiary_90_1[evropi]` xdist worker crash reproduced once in the full run** — identical signature to the OPEN 162-1 finding (gw crash, passes in isolation, mechanism unidentified). Don't burn time re-diagnosing it per-story; cite the 162-1 finding and prove isolation-pass.
- **Pre-existing repo lint debt:** `ruff check tests/` fails on `tests/dungeon/conftest.py` E402 ×3 at HEAD (untouched by the story). Scope lint gates to your changed files or you'll chase ghosts.

### Adding a NEW ATTRIBUTE to an EXISTING routed span = TWO edits, not one (158-53)
- A span attribute lives in TWO independent projections: the emit helper's `attributes` dict (raw span) AND the `SPAN_ROUTES[SPAN_*].extract` lambda (the typed GM-panel feed). Adding the attr to only the emit dict leaves it in raw spans but SILENTLY ABSENT from the typed Subsystems feed — the exact half-wired failure the OTEL doctrine is meant to catch. For 158-53's `casts_before` on `wwn.spell.cast`: `telemetry/spans/wwn.py` needed BOTH `wwn_spell_cast_span`'s attributes dict AND `SPAN_ROUTES[SPAN_WWN_SPELL_CAST]`'s extract updated.
- When the attr is load-bearing (not optional cosmetic), make the helper param **required keyword-only** (no default) — grep callers first (`grep -rn "<helper>(" sidequest/`; 158-53 had exactly 2, both in `wwn.py`). A default would be a silent fallback: a future caller that forgets it emits a misleading `0`. Pyright catches every missed call site the instant the param is required.

### Changing a ConfrontationDef field's SHAPE: grep every `.interaction_table` consumer — the plan's file list missed one (2026-07-02, 158-40)
- Moving the dogfight from single `interaction_table` to the `interaction_tables` registry: Plan 4 listed models/loader/sealed_letter/encounter_lifecycle/narration_apply/validate, but `sidequest/game/beat_filter.py:383` ALSO reads `.interaction_table` (sealed-letter maneuver-menu synthesis) — registry-only content would have raised PackError at menu build. `grep -rn '\.interaction_table\b' sidequest/` before and after; add a consumer fallback or the feature is half-wired.
- Identity matters for auto-registration back-compat: TEA's test asserts `d.interaction_tables["merge"] is d.interaction_table` — do it in a `model_validator(mode="after")` on the model (`self.interaction_tables = {t.starting_state: t}`), NOT in the loader (loader-side duplication validates two separate instances and `is` fails). Model-level also covers direct constructions in tests/fixtures.
- Parallel content subagents + live pack loads RACE: a `load_genre_pack` run mid-authoring caught `interactions_beam.yaml` with scalar `tags:` (agent self-fixed seconds later). Re-verify once all agents report; don't chase phantom failures from a half-written file.
- `ConfrontationDef` minimal-arrange chain for combat: hp_depletion + category=combat requires `opponent_default_stats` with `hp` AND `armor_class` AND `dexterity` (three separate validators fire in sequence); dial_threshold (the default) requires both metrics. Mirror a live def instead of guessing.

### testing-runner CAN edit test files during green verification — review its diff before committing (2026-07-02, 158-40)
- The green-verify runner patched `test_dogfight_content_loading.py`'s `dogfight_table` fixture (singular `interaction_table` → registry read) on its own initiative because live content had moved shapes. The fix was correct and well-written, but it landed in MY working tree unannounced — `git status` before `git add -A` is not optional. If a runner's edit had been wrong, blind-committing would attribute it to Dev.

### Any helper wired into `execute_intent_router_pre_narrator_pass` MUST tolerate duck-typed / MagicMock packs (2026-06-27, 158-29)
- The pre-narrator pass runs on FAKE packs in many existing tests (`_FakePack`/`_FakeConfrontationDef` in `test_intent_router_confrontation_classified.py`, `MagicMock` packs in `test_intent_router_degrade_decompose_span.py`, etc.). `_confrontation_verb_hits` already `getattr`-walks (`getattr(cdef, "intent_verbs", None)`) for exactly this reason. When I wired `force_dispatch_dogfight_on_verb_miss` into the `confrontation_verb_unrouted` miss branch, it called `_resolve_dogfight_type(pack)` which did direct attribute access (`cdef.resolution_mode`, `pack.rules.confrontations`) → `AttributeError: '_FakeConfrontationDef' object has no attribute 'resolution_mode'` → **5 previously-green pre-pass tests went red** (`test_158_2_literary_attack_routing` ×4, `test_intent_router_confrontation_classified` ×1). Fix: harden the resolver to a `getattr`-walk. Real packs carry the required field, so live behavior is unchanged — only fakes resolve to None. **Lesson:** before wiring any new injector/helper into the pre-pass, audit EVERY attribute access it (and everything it transitively calls) makes for duck-typed-pack safety; mirror the existing getattr idiom.
- **The force-seat confidence must be ≥ the bank's per-subsystem gate (0.6).** A force-injected `SubsystemDispatch` with sub-threshold `confidence` is silently degraded to a narrator hint by `run_dispatch_bank` and SEATS NOTHING. For a deliberate force-seat use `confidence=1.0`. Also supply `idempotency_key` — both are REQUIRED on `SubsystemDispatch` (no defaults). And `_normalize_per_player_ids` runs at `intent_router_pass.py:866` (BEFORE the classification block), mapping `player_id → seat_id` only when `player_seats` is bound — so a post-decompose injector that targets the submitter slot must match `pd.player_id in (player_name, seat_id)` with a single-submitter / create-slot fallback.
- **Verify a "pre-existing" failure by NEUTRALIZING your change, not by assuming.** The 2 `test_intent_router_degrade_decompose_span` failures looked like they might be mine (same pass). I temporarily replaced my injection call with `pass` and re-ran: still 2 failed → proven pre-existing (story 71-29 `MagicMock` pack × story 158-33 `effective_bestiary` unpack `ValueError`). `git stash`/`checkout --` are banned, so neutralize-in-place (edit your own just-added lines) is the cheap non-destructive isolation move; restore after.

### Migrating a confrontation def dial_threshold→hp_depletion has THREE ripples beyond the def block (2026-06-27, 158-31)
Changing a `ConfrontationDef`'s `win_condition` from `dial_threshold` to `hp_depletion` (ADR-153 §2 dogfight firewall) is NOT a one-block content edit. Three non-obvious consumers break:
- **The hp_depletion combat-seeding block newly fires.** `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` gates `_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs` + `_roll_and_persist_initiative` on `cdef.win_condition == WinCondition.hp_depletion and cdef.category=='combat' and not is_fate` (encounter_lifecycle.py ~2204). A def that was `dial_threshold` never reached this; flipping it to hp_depletion pulls it in. `_roll_and_persist_initiative` (SWN P4 1d8+DEX turn order) **fails loud** (`player actor '<name>' not found among snapshot.characters — cannot resolve DEX`) for any snapshot whose PC isn't a full `Character` with a DEX stat — which is most minimal test snapshots. For a **sealed-letter dogfight** (simultaneous-commit, resolved by `resolve_dogfight_shots` geometry + caller-injected d20s) there IS no WN turn order, so the fix is to EXEMPT `resolution_mode == sealed_letter_lookup` from `_roll_and_persist_initiative` (keep `_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs` — the opponent core is needed for hp_depletion). 10 tests regressed on the DEX error; the exemption fixed 9, the 10th was a superseded reject test.
- **Class `encounter_beat_choices` is a SECOND consumer of the def's `beats:`.** Deleting the dogfight beats orphaned `straight/bank/loop/kill_rotation` in the `Pilot` class `encounter_beat_choices` → `PackError: class 'Pilot' encounter_beat_choices references beat id(s) … not in pool` at LOAD time. The plan's "confirm the maneuver menu doesn't come from beats" check only covered the sealed-letter interaction-table menu, not the class beat-choice surface. Grep `encounter_beat_choices` + the deleted beat ids across `classes.yaml` before deleting beats. (Also a stale `bank` reference lived in the Pilot ability prose — not validated, but dead.)
- **The synthetic fixture pack must migrate in lockstep with the live pack.** `tests/fixtures/packs/swn_test_pack/` carried the SAME dogfight def; server tests load it (no live content in pytest). The opponent-core minting that `test_dogfight_default_opponent` asserts (`find_creature_core(name).hp.max==8`) only fires under hp_depletion, so the fixture MUST be migrated too (and its Pilot class fixed the same way) — otherwise it diverges from the live pack and fails.
**Verify order:** after the content+fixture edits, FIRST `load_genre_pack(path)` both packs (catches PackError fast), THEN run the dogfight suite serially (`-n0`). A full blast-radius sweep also caught a calibration test (`test_sealed_letter_thresholds_unchanged`) and two dispatch reject tests that pinned the pre-firewall dial/reject contract — all superseded by §6/§2, updated to the new contract per Keith's ruling. See [[story_158_31_dogfight_firewall]] and the TEA dogfight gotcha.

### In-tree hatchling multi-package: adding a new import package needs `uv sync` to make it resolve (2026-06-26, 159-4)
- sidequest-understudy is ONE distribution wheel exposing MULTIPLE import packages via `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["src/understudy", "src/seat_core"]`. Adding a sibling package (159-4's `src/companion`) is two steps: (1) create `src/companion/` with an `__init__.py`, (2) append `"src/companion"` to that `packages` list. But `import companion` will NOT resolve until you re-run `uv sync` — hatchling's editable install builds a per-package finder/mapping at install time, so a brand-new package under `src/` is invisible to the existing editable install until re-synced. After `uv sync`, `uv run python -c "import companion"` works. The new package needs NO new runtime deps if it only imports stdlib + pydantic + the in-tree `seat_core` (companion's manifest/brain/dice/protocol/persona/intent/actuation do); `websockets`/`typer` only arrive with 159-5's ws_transport+CLI.
- The companion modules pasted from Plan C verbatim and went 47/47 green with one micro-change: `brain.decide` used `except (TimeoutError, ModelError, Exception)` in the plan — a redundant tuple (Exception covers the others; TimeoutError/ModelError are subclasses). Simplified to `except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001`. Same behavior (any failure → YIELD), drops the now-unused `ModelError` import. `except Exception` correctly does NOT swallow `asyncio.CancelledError` (a BaseException) — cancellation must propagate; only model/timeout failures degrade to YIELD.
- Tests live in `tests/companion/` (NOT `tests/`) to dodge a pytest package-import-mode basename collision with understudy's own `test_manifest.py`/`test_persona.py`/`test_actuation.py`. The wheel-packages change is regression-safe for the other packages (seat_core suite stayed 34/34 green) but ALWAYS re-run a sibling suite after touching the shared pyproject — a packaging typo there breaks every package's editable resolution at once.

### World `npcs.yaml` MUST use top-level `npcs:` + the `AuthoredNpc` schema — `authored_npcs:` silently loaded as [] (2026-06-26, 158-32)
- The world loader (`sidequest/genre/loader.py`, `_load_single_world`) reads `npcs_raw.get("npcs", [])` and validates each item as `AuthoredNpc` (`extra="forbid"`). `heavy_metal/{barsoom,evropi}/npcs.yaml` authored the roster under `authored_npcs:` with non-schema fields (`culture`/`location`/`disposition:<string>`/`goals`/`timeline`) — so `.get("npcs")` returned `[]` and the WHOLE canon roster silently vanished. Symptom in play: canon NPCs (Salensus Oll, Kantos Vah) never preloaded → narrator mentions fell through `_apply_npc_mentions` Step-3 and got shuffled into phantom minted names. **Field map for conversion:** `location`→`location_tags: [..]`, `disposition:<word>`→`initial_disposition:<int -100..100>` (ADR-020), `goals`+`timeline`→`history_seeds: [..]`, `culture`→fold into `role` prose (no culture field). Mirror a working world: `wry_whimsy/wonderland/npcs.yaml`. Loader now FAILS LOUD (`GenreLoadError`) when a present `npcs.yaml` has no `npcs:` key; explicit `npcs: []` is allowed.
- **Blast-radius rule for loader fail-loud changes:** scan BOTH content AND `sidequest-server/tests/fixtures/**` for the malformed shape — a copy of the bad barsoom file lived at `tests/fixtures/packs/wwn_test_pack/worlds/test_world/npcs.yaml` and broke `test_wwn_heavy_metal_chargen` + `test_120_4_world_equipment_tables_override` once the loader started raising. ALSO note scenario-tier `worlds/<w>/scenarios/<s>/npcs.yaml` is a DIFFERENT loader (`ScenarioNpc`), not touched by the world-tier `world_path / "npcs.yaml"` block — don't "fix" those.
- **The testing-runner (haiku) helper mislabeled a real regression as "pre-existing."** It called the heavy_metal chargen/equipment failures pre-existing; a stash-diff proved they were MINE (baseline passed). LESSON: for any full-suite failure in a subsystem your change *could* touch (loads your pack, hits your loader), don't trust a "pre-existing" verdict — confirm by `git stash` (BOTH repos) + re-run the specific tests. Also `test_pregen_bestiary_90_1[evropi]` is genuinely flaky (encountergen runs UNSEEDED — `_generate_encounter` never threads `rng`), so it intermittently empties the pool under xdist; verify determinism by re-running before blaming your change.

### Single-command Typer app COLLAPSES the subcommand — `companion play <x>` needs an `@app.callback()` (2026-06-26, 159-5)
- Plan C's `cli.py` sample registered ONE command (`app.command(name="play")(play_cmd)`) with no callback. A Typer app with exactly one command runs that command DIRECTLY — the subcommand name is dropped — so `runner.invoke(app, ["play", str(path)])` parses `"play"` as the `definition` argument and `path` as an EXTRA → Click error "Got unexpected extra argument(s)", exit 2 for the wrong reason. 3 CLI tests failed (64/67) on this, NOT on logic.
- Fix (mirrors understudy's own `src/understudy/cli.py`): add a no-op `@app.callback()\ndef main() -> None: ...` right after `app = typer.Typer(...)`. A callback forces Typer to keep the app as a subcommand GROUP, so `companion play <def>` (the spec's intended UX) parses correctly. Logged as a `minor` deviation (Plan C's sample omitted it). websockets 16.0 (not 12) resolved by `uv sync`; `websockets.ConnectionClosed(None, None)` ctor and `websockets.connect` (now `websockets.asyncio.client.connect`) both work — TEA's RED test ctor was version-robust.
- Scope discipline that held: a repo-wide `ruff check .` flags 2 pre-existing `E402` errors in `tests/test_reconnect.py` (present on `origin/develop`, commit `1584329`, NOT touched by 159-5). My five 159-5 files are ruff-clean. Left the pre-existing debt untouched (flagged as a non-blocking Delivery Finding) rather than scope-creeping a fix into an unrelated test file. The `git diff HEAD -- <file>` empty + `git show origin/develop:<file> | ruff` reproducing the same errors is the proof-it-isn't-mine check.

### Adding a module-level helper to `emitters.py`? Imports go FUNCTION-LOCAL (cycle avoidance), annotations go TYPE_CHECKING (2026-06-25, 159-3)
- `sidequest/server/emitters.py` deliberately imports `MessageEnvelope`, `_watcher_publish`, and `json` **inside functions**, not at module scope — there's an `emitters` ↔ `session_handler` ↔ `game.projection` import cycle, and `emitters` is imported very early. So a NEW module-level helper (here `expand_visibility_for_companions`) must follow suit: `import json` / `from sidequest.game.projection.envelope import MessageEnvelope as _Envelope` / `from sidequest.server.session_handler import _watcher_publish` / `from sidequest.game.projection.invariants import VISIBILITY_GATED_KINDS` all go in the function body. A bare module-level `_watcher_publish(...)` (as Plan B's sample code wrote it) would NameError — `session_handler.py:51` re-exports it specifically for emitters' function-local imports.
- For the helper's SIGNATURE annotations, add the types to the existing `if TYPE_CHECKING:` block (e.g. `from sidequest.server.session_room import SessionRoom`, `from sidequest.game.projection.envelope import MessageEnvelope`). TYPE_CHECKING imports never execute at runtime → no cycle, and with `from __future__ import annotations` the annotations are strings so pyright resolves them while runtime stays clean.
- This also satisfies TEA's monkeypatch contract for the OTEL span test: a function-local `from session_handler import _watcher_publish` re-resolves each call, so `monkeypatch.setattr("sidequest.server.session_handler._watcher_publish", ...)` intercepts it. (`connect.py` differs — it imports `_watcher_publish` at module level (line 69), so spans there are patched at `connect._watcher_publish`.)

### Companion pet-perception is widen-before-projection, NOT a firewall change (2026-06-25, 159-3)
- The seam that lets a bonded PET share its owner's private NARRATION_SEGMENT/SECRET_NOTE does NOT touch `CoreInvariantStage` (the security firewall in `game/projection/invariants.py`). Instead, `emit_event` calls `expand_visibility_for_companions(envelope, room)` right after building the `MessageEnvelope` (before `_project_frames`), which only *adds* the pet's `player_id` to `_visibility.visible_to`. The existing visibility gate then includes the pet by ordinary membership, and the per-recipient `invariant.secret_routed` span already covers it. Lesson for any "let X also see Y" perception task: grow the authorized recipient set upstream of the firewall; never weaken the firewall's exclusion logic. Bonds key on owner *identity* (ADR-119 Cf-Access email / dev Host), resolved to live `player_id`s via `SessionRoom.pets_of` at fan-out — a companion can't know the owner's server-minted id.

### New-repo GREEN: the branch-protection hook blocks `main` — commit on a feature branch even when the plan says "work on main" (2026-06-25, 159-1)
- Story 159-1 created the new local repo `sidequest-seat-core` and the plan/SM/TEA all said "work directly on `main` (no feature branch)". TEA's RED commit landed on `main` fine — but the Dev GREEN commit to `main` was HARD-BLOCKED by the `pf hooks dispatch PreToolUse` guard: "Cannot commit to protected branch 'main'. Create a feature branch first." The hook treats `main` as protected regardless of whether the repo is in `.pennyfarthing/repos.yaml`. (Why RED slipped through and GREEN didn't in the same repo on the same branch is unexplained — don't rely on `main` being writable; assume it's protected.)
- Resolution that works: `git checkout -b feat/<story>-<slug>` as its OWN Bash call (the hook keys on the cwd's current branch, so switch first), THEN `git add/commit` in a separate call. The plan's "work on main" ideal is operationally overridden by the hook; log it as a `minor` deviation. For a local-only repo a feature branch is harmless and gives a clean impl-only review diff (`git diff main..feat` = just the implementation files, since RED tests are already on `main`). Note the transient: `main` holds the failing RED tests until finish fast-forwards `feat → main`.
- The new repo has NO git remote (`git init`, no `origin`) → the workflow's `git push` step and SM-finish PR creation have no target. File a non-blocking delivery finding: the repo needs a GitHub remote + a `seat-core` entry in `.pennyfarthing/repos.yaml` before finish can open/merge a PR. Don't fabricate a push.
- `uv sync` for `requires-python = ">=3.12"` pulls the NEWEST interpreter available (got 3.14). Fine for `StrEnum`/`match`/`X | None`. When transcribing a plan verbatim, do NOT "fix" things that look odd but are valid — e.g. `f"...{data!r:.300}"` (precision spec applied to a repr string truncates to 300 chars) is legal Python; leave it. Minimalist discipline = the plan's impl, nothing added.

### 106-4 Part C item-use beat-scan — LANDED, rides the WN sealed-round engine (2026-06-14, 106-4)
- Confrontation beat menus scan the acting PC's `core.inventory.items` and surface transient `use_item:<slug>` "Drink <potion>" heal beats. Server PR #847 + UI PR #387, squash-merged to develop.
- Design forks (Keith): item-use COSTS the Main Action (opponent still acts on its WN slot); AUTO-SUCCESS, no d20 (heal magnitude still rolls `1d6+2` via Part-A `_apply_consumable_heal`); surfacing gated to `hp_depletion` combat only.
- Wiring seam: `beat_filter.beats_available_for(inventory_items=…)` appends transient beats (helpers `item_slug`/`item_use_beat_id`/`is_item_use_beat`, prefix `use_item:`). `dispatch/confrontation` threads inventory and SKIPS DC-authoring for item beats (absent `difficulty` = UI no-roll signal). `dispatch/dice._dispatch_item_use` seals an auto-success WN commit → `run_wn_round` applies heal+consume at the PC's slot. Shared `dispatch/item_use.py` (`resolve_item_use`/`apply_item_use`); new `confrontation.item_used` OTEL span. UI: `lib/beatDispatch.isItemUseBeat`, `App.handleBeatSelect` commits via DICE_THROW with no dice tray, overlay skips the optimistic seal-wait lock.
- Still open in epic 106 (Keith said confirm first): 106-3 (WWN-gate native bleed), 106-5 (death-state coherence), 106-6 (narration-truth guardrail). See project_without_number_module_wiring_checklist.

### 158-8 MP pronoun — Facet 1 already fixed by 153-29; Facet 2 fix is in `_apply_pov_swap`, not the localizer (2026-06-23, 158-8)
- Facet 1 (agreement break "You... his... him", incl. solo reconnect/replay) ALREADY FIXED by 153-29 (commit `8dec6bcd`). The driver's solo repro was a stale-tree artifact (on `d19afd32`). Facet 2 (per-recipient re-anchor — a non-anchor recipient reads their own PC in 3rd person) is the real, unimplemented part.
- Facet-2 fix lives in `sidequest/server/emitters.py::_apply_pov_swap` (~258). The guard `if recipient_pc_name != anchor_pc: return payload_dict` (~279) means only the recipient whose PC == the card's single `anchor_pc` gets swapped. Fix = target the RECIPIENT's OWN PC (`recipient_pc_name`), not gate on equaling `anchor_pc`. Localizer `swap_to_second_person` needs NO change (already re-anchors any PC with full agreement). OTEL `narration.second_person_swap` span in `swap_to_second_person` (pov_swap.py ~904).
- REVIEW-REJECTED then fixed (RT2, commit `42035ed9`): the Facet-2 widening (anchor-only → any-recipient/every-card) exposed that `swap_to_second_person` raises `ValueError` for pronouns outside `{he/him,she/her,they/them}` (pov_swap.py:877), but chargen allows freeform (`pronouns_allow_freeform=True`). Non-canonical pronouns ("she/they","xe/xem") reached the swap and raised INSIDE `repo.transaction()` (emitters.py:518) → rolled back the NARRATION event → table-wide narration loss. Fix = guard `if pronouns not in _PRONOUN_FORMS:` → fail-open to canonical + emit `narration.pov_swap_skipped` span; import `_PRONOUN_FORMS` from pov_swap (single source of truth).
- GENERAL PATTERN: a fail-loud localizer called UNCONDITIONALLY inside `emit_event`'s transaction becomes a table-wide turn-aborter the moment any recipient feeds it bad input — guard + fail-open + skip-span at the CALL SITE, don't weaken the localizer. Real root: `Character.pronouns: str` (character.py:106) unconstrained vs the localizer's 3-value enum.

### Epic 92 local-classifier rung is MERGED but DISABLED — NO-GO on all gates (2026-06-06, 92-1/92-2)
- Local-rung config seam is merged (server #715, `LOCAL_CLASSIFIER_MODEL=qwen2.5:7b-instruct`, default `anthropic`) but MUST STAY DISABLED — the 92-1 operator A/B eval recorded NO-GO on all three gates: 26.7% dispatch agreement (vs ≥95%), 86% schema validity (vs ≥95%), p95 24.1s (vs ≤5s). qwen3-coder:30b aborted (slower than 7B, worse schema modes).
- Evidence: `sidequest-server/artifacts/router_ab_92_2_gate_decision.md` (#717). 10MB corpora gitignored, re-derivable (`artifacts/mine_router_prompts.py`, ~$1.50).
- Caveat controlling next step: the eval ran on PRE-82-10 fat prompts (p50 35KB); 82-10 (#716) cut them to p50 12KB. Fair re-test = re-mine → re-capture → re-run both models on slimmed prompts (~30 min). Until a GO is recorded, don't enable `ollama` classification routing and don't treat the merged seam as evidence it works.

### A spend spike is a hypothesis, not a security incident — 05-21→23 ~$38.9K was self-inflicted stress testing (2026-05-25, from Keith)
- CORRECTED: the 2026-05-21→23 Anthropic spend spike (org Cost API: 05-21 $5,657, 05-22 $17,198, 05-23 $14,434; ~$38.9K over 7 days) was NOT a key compromise. Keith confirmed it was self-inflicted — he deliberately spawned multiple sidequest-servers and spammed headless tests with EXPLICIT CACHE BUSTS to stress-test cache economics. The duplicate-stack footgun intentionally maximized.
- Original 2026-05-23 reading ("suspected compromise — $20k unauthorized limit raise + ~$300 burn") was wrong on both magnitude and cause; I escalated to "security-first, contact Anthropic for refund" off a stale note before Keith corrected me.
- How to apply: get the org Cost API numbers (`scripts/anthropic_usage.py`, admin key) + per-key/model attribution FIRST, then ask the user what they were running. Massive cache_read/cache_write volume on dates the user was testing cache = expected. Key rotation (`sidequest-2`) was precautionary, not proof of compromise.

### SessionRoom.close_store must null `_store`, `_snapshot`, `_session` together — they're one binding (2026-05-24, 61-followup-C)
- When `close_store` was first wired into last-disconnect (commit 921694a), it nulled `_store` but left `_snapshot` set. `bind_world`'s idempotency check is `if self._snapshot is not None: return`, so the next reconnect early-returned WITHOUT re-attaching a store; every reconnecting `_SessionData` was built with `store=None` and crashed at the first `sd.store.recent_narrative(...)` call. Symptom: `'NoneType' object has no attribute 'recent_narrative'` after last-disconnect → reconnect.
- Load-bearing fact: `close_store` is a SLUG-RECYCLE SEAM — it must produce a room that behaves as if never bound, so the next `bind_world` re-runs the full attachment path. Half-teardown leaves a zombie room that early-returns on stale fields.
- How to apply: any fix touching `SessionRoom._snapshot`/`_store`/`_session` or `bind_world`'s idempotency must keep all three in sync — null all if you null one, check all (or refactor to a single `_bound` flag). Fixed in commit 6dcc179 (nulls all three); regression test `test_close_store_unbinds_snapshot_so_next_bind_reattaches_store` in `tests/server/test_session_room.py`. The per-site null guard at the call would have buried the bomb — the right fix was upstream at the teardown seam.

### Surprise SDK charge = duplicate dev stacks racing, not a real game (2026-05-21)
- Signature: huge tokens-in (41.6M) vs tiny tokens-out (24.5k) over ~45 min (~1700:1 ratio, vs normal ~20:1) = enormous prompts fired repeatedly for near-empty responses (turns dying mid-flight), NOT real narration.
- Root cause: multiple `just up` stacks across oq-1/oq-2 clones, TWO uvicorn processes bound to port 8765 racing every WebSocket connection → uvicorn `--reload` crash/restart loop (35 restarts, 1,241 tracebacks). Each restart = cold Anthropic prompt cache → next narrator turn re-sends full prompt at full input price.
- Apply: on a surprise SDK charge, FIRST run `ps aux | grep -iE "uvicorn sidequest|just up"` and `lsof -nP -iTCP:8765 -sTCP:LISTEN`. >1 server or >1 `just up` = the cause. Kill all (`pkill -f "just up"; pkill -f "uvicorn sidequest.server"; pkill -f "cloudflared tunnel run sidequest"`), bring up exactly ONE stack from ONE clone. Both clones tee to the SAME `/tmp/sidequest-*.log` so the healthy stack's log looks clean while money burns in clobbered logs. The cloudflared tunnel is NOT an exposure vector — Cloudflare Zero Trust / Access email-gated.

### Fate confrontations reuse the native StructuredEncounter; gate/seed each native seam (2026-06-19)
- A Fate pack (`rules.ruleset == "fate"`, ADR-144) seats a confrontation on the SAME `snapshot.encounter` StructuredEncounter the native/WN engine uses (by design — `build_fate_state_payload` reads enc.actors/situation_aspects/pending_compels; Fate surface is `FATE_STATE` via `_maybe_emit_fate_state`, ruleset-gated). Bug class: native code paths touching the encounter are NOT ruleset-gated, so they call Fate's d20-surface methods (compute_dc/offer_difficulty/apply_beat/attack_params) or hit the "opponent needs a FateSheet" guard — all fail loud by design (`_NO_D20_SURFACE`, `fate_opponent.py` ValueError). The guards are correct; the seam is the bug. Fixing one seam moves the failure one stage downstream.
- Fixed seams (playtest 150-2): Projection (#964, `e0632d0b`) — `should_emit_native_confrontation(rules)` gate (False for fate) in `dispatch/confrontation.py`; Opponent-seating (#966, `d5408dd0`) — `_seed_fate_opponents` in encounter_lifecycle + `FateRulesetModule.seed_opponent_fate_sheet` (seeds the Other from cfg.skills — peer adversary, no invented balance) + `fate.opponent.seeded` span.
- Doctrine: gate/seed the native seam on `ruleset != "fate"` (or seed the Fate facet); NEVER weaken the Fate fail-loud guard. Latent follow-ups: narrator still emits native `beat_selections` under Fate (inert, wasteful); opponent resolution can pick the wrong creature (flavor bug); Fate stunts absent from `FATE_STATE` wire model (`FateCharacterEntry` has no `stunts` field) — UI can't render picked stunts (126-19 family).

### 126-8 Fate DEFEND barrier hangs — server parks on FATE_DEFEND_REQUEST the UI can't answer (2026-06-19)
- Story 126-8 ([FATE] player-defense 4dF DEFEND barrier) was marked done/approved with `repos: server,ui` but the UI half NEVER shipped (`git -C sidequest-ui log -i --grep=defend` across all branches = nothing). Server broadcasts `FATE_DEFEND_REQUEST` and block-and-waits with no auto-roll (`sidequest/handlers/fate_throw.py:161-173`), but UI has no FATE_DEFEND_REQUEST handler, no "defend" in the action union (`payloads.ts:614`), no defend tray. The existing `concede()` (`FateConflictSurface.tsx:193`) is the pre-roll whole-conflict FATE_ACTION concede — a different mechanic.
- Consequence: the first time an NPC attacks a PC in a Fate conflict, the exchange parks forever (hangs); this path was never run end-to-end. Resolution: 126-14 re-scoped server-only (wire concede field/handler/OTEL — engine branches exist: `dispatch_fate_defense(conceded=...)`, fate_conflict.py:612/1231, `FatePendingDefense.conceded`); new story 126-17 (repos ui) builds the defend-throw UI (FATE_DEFEND_REQUEST → defend tray reusing FateDiceTray → FATE_THROW(action="defend")).
- General gotcha: a story's `repos:` tag + done/approved status does NOT prove every listed repo shipped (126-8 deferred its UI half to a spec "§8 follow-up" never filed). When auditing whether a feature is wired, check the per-repo git log, not the repos tag.

### A new Fate display-only-beat resolution_mode needs a per-mode narrator zone; the dial leak keys on win_condition NOT resolution_mode (2026-06-20)
- Adding a Fate `resolution_mode` whose beats are display-only stubs (`kind=None`, `stat_check=None`) — like contest, and conflict (153-3) — has TWO seams keyed on DIFFERENT things. (1) NARRATOR (real per-mode trap, keyed on resolution_mode): `agents/narrator.py` `build_encounter_context` gave `== contest` a dedicated Fate-live zone; EVERY other mode fell into the `elif` rendering `f"... kind={b.kind.value} ..."` → AttributeError on a kind=None stub. A new display-only mode MUST be added here (153-3 widened to `resolution_mode in (contest, conflict)`).
- (2) DISPATCH dial leak — keyed on win_condition, NOT resolution_mode, usually a NON-issue. `server/narration_apply.py` drops stray narrator beats via the 126-37 branch `if enc.win_condition == "fate_conflict":` (~5974) which runs BEFORE the resolution_mode ladder. Seating stamps `win_condition="fate_conflict"` for EVERY Fate non-contest/non-sealed mode (`encounter_lifecycle.py` ~1701/1757, `seat_as_fate_conflict`, un-gated by category). So a seated `conflict` is ALREADY dial-protected — do NOT add a `resolution_mode==conflict` drop branch (dead code, duplicates the 126-37 guard). `contest` is the special case (not seated as fate_conflict → keeps its own `== contest` drop branch + `_gate_applies_to_encounter` exclusion ~861).
- RT0 reviewer error to avoid: tracing the resolution_mode ladder to the `else` and concluding "conflict leaks the dial" skips the win_condition branch above it — MEASURE (probe a seated conflict + stray beat). Latent footgun: a non-Fate pack can author `resolution_mode: conflict`/`contest` (no schema guard) → is_fate=False → the dispatch else DOES apply_beat on a kind=None stub → crash. Seating/loader tests miss the narrator crash (never BUILD the narrator prompt).

### Fate found-item promotion (#945) — world-tier gear unwired + Phase-2 narrator promotion dormant (2026-06-19)
- Story #945 (`fate_item_promotion.py`) promotes a gained item → invokable Fate aspect by matching it against `_fate_cfg.gear_catalog` (`narration_apply.py:4967`). Two wiring traps that look live but aren't: (1) `gear_catalog` is GENRE-tier ONLY — `loader._load_gear` (loader.py:2130) reads `<pack>/gear.yaml` → `rules.fate.gear_catalog`; world-tier `worlds/<world>/gear.yaml` is NOT merged, so world-flavor found-items (Oz silver shoes) can't promote until a server merge lands. (2) Phase-2 narrator promotion is DORMANT — `narration_apply.py:4974` consumes `entry.get('grants_aspect')` to mint a capped aspect, but NO narrator tool-contract/prompt instructs the narrator it may set that field (zero refs outside narration_apply.py).
- Decision (Keith, GM 126-21): world-specific artifacts stay at the WORLD tier ("Crunch in the Genre, Flavor in the World"); genre `gear.yaml` is the generic shared chassis. Authored `worlds/oz/gear.yaml` (oz_silver_shoes, oz_golden_cap) — validates via `loader._load_gear`, INERT until the server merge. Blocker filed = 126-25 ([SERVER] merge world-tier Fate gear into gear_catalog + the dormant Phase-2 gap). Realistic Fate worlds (spaghetti_western/tea_and_murder/pulp_noir) have no magical artifacts → their significant items belong to Phase-2 narrator promotion, not authored permission-gear.

### Monster-manual inject cap silently drops PLACED authored NPCs beyond 3 (2026-06-24, 158-11)
- `monster_manual_inject._npc_patches_for_available_humans` did `available_at_location(...)[:_AVAILABLE_NPC_INJECT_LIMIT]` (=3); the list is placed-first then unplaced, so a location with >3 `location_tags`-anchored authored NPCs lost its tail every turn. Dropped NPCs never get `_human_patch(manual_origin=True, location=…)`, so they stay at `preload_authored_npcs` defaults `manual_origin=False`/`location=None` (`game/world_materialization.py:824` — preload never sets the origin marker; the per-turn inject patch upgrades it). Fix: surface ALL placed uncapped, cap only unplaced walk-ons. Same class as oz road "4 companions → 3". Owning test `test_inject_span_reports_placed_dropped_by_cap` PINNED the bug and had to be rewritten.
- Technique — measure state-seeding bugs from the live Postgres save, don't trace seed code. Real DB = `SIDEQUEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://$USER@localhost:5432/sidequest` (NOT `_test`). Find a session whose snapshot names the entity, then json.load `game_state.snapshot_json` and inspect `npcs[].{manual_origin,location,disposition}`: `psql -d sidequest -t -A -c "SELECT s.session_id,s.session_slug FROM sessions s JOIN game_state g ON g.session_id=s.session_id WHERE s.world_slug='<world>' AND g.snapshot_json LIKE '%<Name>%' ORDER BY s.session_id DESC LIMIT 10;"`. Ground truth (all npcs manual_origin=True except one) localizes inject-vs-chargen instantly.

### MAP_UPDATE name reused — old ADR-019 wire deleted, name revived for ADR-055 room-graph (2026-05-19)
- On 2026-04-28 the cartography image tier and the original ADR-019 MAP_UPDATE wire were removed across all four repos; ADR-019 flipped to superseded (by ADR-082). By 2026-05-19 the `MAP_UPDATE` message name was REVIVED with a NEW payload shape for the ADR-055 room-graph live map (`protocol/enums.py`, `messages.py`, `server/session_handler.py`, ui `App.tsx` + `MapWidget.tsx`).
- Treat any current `MAP_UPDATE` reference as the new ADR-055 room-graph shape, NOT the dead cartography one. Protocol-file comments "do NOT revive MAP_UPDATE" refer to the legacy PAYLOAD, not the constant name.
- Stayed deleted for good: `CartographyMetadata/Region/Route`, `ExploredLocation`, `FogBounds`, `RoomExitInfo`, `dispatch/map_update.py`, the `cartography` render tier, `emit_map_update_for_cartography`. Live now: new `MessageType.MAP_UPDATE` + model, `MapOverlay/MapWidget/Automapper/DungeonMapRenderer/TacticalGridRenderer.tsx`, `world.cartography.yaml` (always ADR-055). When auditing docs that say "MAP_UPDATE is dead", check which shape they mean before deleting.

### Combat-mutation features must wire BOTH resolution paths (2026-05-26, ADR-114 Task 11)
- Any feature mutating engine state on a confrontation beat (HP damage, edge, resource debits) must wire into TWO paths or it silently no-ops in real play: (1) simple-DC `dispatch_dice_throw` (`server/dispatch/dice.py`, applies beat inline ~dice.py:497); (2) opposed_check — `dispatch_dice_throw` DEFERS (sets `opposed_pending`, stashes player d20), beat applied later in `narration_apply.py::_resolve_opposed_check_branch` → `apply_beat(...)` (~narration_apply:2768), once per side.
- The trap: `space_opera`'s `combat` (Firefight) ConfrontationDef uses `resolution_mode: opposed_check`. Wiring a mutation only into the dispatch path passes a synthetic non-opposed e2e but deals ZERO effect in real space_opera combat (the phantom-combat failure ADR-078 chased). Fix injected damage at BOTH `apply_beat` sites via the `damage_resolver` param; dice-roll/broadcast helpers extracted to `dispatch/damage_roll.py` so both callers share them.
- How to apply: grep `resolution_mode` on the target pack's combat def; if `opposed_check`, drive the e2e through `narration_apply`'s opposed branch against the REAL pack, not a synthetic fixture.

### Region-mode combat abandoned same-turn because the abandon guard keys off the drifting scene-title STRING (2026-06-06, Perseus MP playtest)
- SWN combat looked "mechanically hollow" (`beat_selections=0 confrontation=None active=False`, narrator free-hands hit/miss) but the confrontation DOES instantiate (initiative rolls) — it is deactivated the SAME turn by `narration_apply.py` `encounter.deactivated_on_location_change` (emit ~line 3455, `else` at 3451; abandon block gated ~3298 `if old_loc != result.location`). Cause: the narrator re-titles the scene every turn in region-mode worlds (`narrator.location_drift_repaired` fires every turn), and the abandon guard keys off the raw location STRING, not the cartography REGION. Combat is `category=combat` → not `_encounter_is_mobile` → abandoned. Code at 3218–3244 ALREADY detects same-region sub-location (`region.entry_skipped_sub_location`) but the abandon block ignores it.
- Fix lane: server Python, narration_apply.py — don't abandon when `snapshot.current_region` is unchanged (mirror the mobile-continue branch at 3418). Blocks epic 90-3 for any region-mode ruleset world. A static code-trace wrongly blamed the ADR-113 confidence gate; the live log disproved it (measure-don't-assert win).

### "Slimming risks the 4096-token Haiku cache floor" was a category error (2026-06-06, server #716)
- Epic 91/92 context docs repeatedly claimed story 82-10 (router state_summary slimming) was boxed in by Haiku's 4,096-token cacheable floor. FALSE: the floor applies only to the cached system+tools PREFIX; the state_summary rides in the per-turn USER message, which was never cached. Slimming it cannot break caching. The myth deferred a 66% prompt cut (p50 35KB→12KB, landed server #716) for weeks while the Haiku bill ran.
- How to apply: when a doc claims a prompt change risks a cache floor, check WHICH zone the bytes live in (cached prefix vs per-turn user message) before accepting the constraint. Residual slimming targets in `sprint/archive/82-10-session.md` (scenario_state clue defs, whole in-scene NPC entries, PC inventories — ADR-110 deferred positive-allowlist follow-up).

### 2026-05-23 narrator runaway — Valley/Recency blocks uncached AND unslimmed (2026-05-23)
- Narrator burned ~$313 in 2 days (all sonnet-4-6), a 1490:1 input/output runaway. Bug A (primary): `agents/orchestrator.py:3437-3441` caches only `system_blocks[0]` (stable); Valley + Recency ride `cache=False`, paying full input on every one of up to 8 tool-loop iters/turn. AND the Valley payload is `snapshot.model_dump(...)` (`server/session_helpers.py:559-579`), but `_PHASE_B_DROP_FIELDS` (session_helpers.py:64) drops only `active_tropes, axis_values, genie_wishes, achievement_tracker` — growing fields `room_states, npcs, journal, known_facts, footnotes, belief_state, location_descriptions` flow unslimmed. Trigger commit `c8d2b54` shipped a token gesture as if ADR-110 were satisfied (still "deferred").
- Bug B (secondary): stable block writes 5m TTL not the env-configured 1h. `SIDEQUEST_ANTHROPIC_CACHE_TTL` defaults "1h" (`anthropic_sdk_client.py:80-90`) but logs show `5m=…, 1h=0` every iter=1 — the `ttl:"1h"` isn't carrying onto the system-block `cache_control`. Re-pays cache_write at 1.25× every turn.
- Fix (Dev story): (a) extend `_PHASE_B_DROP_FIELDS` to growing fields OR slim Valley to a bounded projection; (b) `cache=True` on Valley/Recency OR delete Valley and move truly-stable bytes into the stable block; (c) thread the `ttl` kwarg onto the system-block cache_control. Add an output-token-floor/input-bloat alarm. Acquittal list (NOT the bug): `running_messages` (fresh per turn), pytest live leak (none), background-task firings (none), `ab_eval_harness`, the aside Haiku path. Stop using `--reload` while iterating narrator-prompt code (each save invalidates the cache → full cache_write bill).

### SDK/subscription backend zeroes metered API cost but caps router at max_turns=2 (2026-06-16, from Keith)
- Running the server with `ANTHROPIC_DISABLED_API_KEY` (not `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) makes the `anthropic_sdk` backend auth narrator + intent-router via the Claude Agent SDK / Claude Code subscription seat (flat-rate) → per-token API billing ~$0. Log tell: "Claude Code returned an error result", `backend=claude-cli`/anthropic_sdk.
- Same path pins the intent router at mandatory `max_turns=2` (llm_factory.py:274-276; max_turns=1 fails closed); narrator runs higher so its tool rounds fit.
- When the router fails `error_max_turns` ("Reached maximum number of turns (2)"), fix ON the SDK path (more turn headroom / trim prompt) — do NOT re-enable the metered key (re-introduces per-token cost). A clean restart does NOT clear it. Masked in normal play by `SIDEQUEST_INTENT_ROUTER_DEGRADE_ON_FAIL=1`.

### "Sealed-letter" names three unrelated systems — do not conflate (2026-05-09)
- The codebase uses "sealed-letter" for three distinct concepts; conflating them produces false playtest bug reports.
- (1) Submit-and-wait barrier (turn coordination) — LIVE, `SessionRoom.dispatch_lock`; the wait mechanism, all submit then narrator resolves once.
- (2) Sealed visibility model (hidden peer submissions) — NOT implemented; reserved for PvP. ADR-036's 2026-05-03 amendment made peer action text VISIBLE by default (collaborative). A "peer text leaks" report is the design, not a bug.
- (3) Sealed-letter resolution mechanic — LIVE, `sidequest/server/dispatch/sealed_letter.py`; cross-product lookup table for dogfight (ADR-077) + magic confrontation outcomes. Shares only the name.
- Naming going forward: "submit-and-wait barrier" for the mechanism, "collaborative/sealed visibility" for visibility modes, "sealed-letter resolution" for the dogfight/magic table. Canonical source: ADR-036 amendments (2026-05-03 + 2026-05-09).

### A Without-Number standard-six attribute sweep must also sweep WORLD-tier archetypes.yaml (2026-06-05, 88-2)
- When binding a pack to swn/cwn/wwn/awn and adopting the standard six (STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA), the sweep MUST include world-tier `worlds/<world>/archetypes.yaml` `stat_ranges` keys, not just genre-tier.
- Why: `effective_archetypes` (genre/models/pack.py) makes a world's archetypes REPLACE the genre ones; NPCs roll stats from those `stat_ranges` (encountergen.py). The swn `_stat` lookup (game/ruleset/swn.py) raises KeyError with NO neutral-10 fallback — a retired flavor key (e.g. `Instinct`/`Wits`) → NPC stat block the standard-six attribute_map can't resolve → combat crashes in the only playable world. Green unit/wiring tests miss it (they hand-seed standard-six opponents, never world-archetype NPCs).
- Gate: add a world-tier sweep test globbing every `worlds/*/archetypes.yaml`, asserting standard-six `stat_ranges` keys (fail-loud if none found). World `creatures.yaml` uses HD-style SWN stat lines, not ability-score keys — not a flavor-stat surface.

- Faithful SWN `RulesetModule` built as a multi-plan epic (specs: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-26-{pluggable-srd-ruleset-modules,swn-module}-design.md`).

P6 follow-ups: `swn.py attack_params` silently rolls vs AC 10 when target_core None (should fail loud); aureate_span is `draft:true`; ship weapon/AC/HP values are feel-calibrated placeholders; dogfight smoke test pre-existing fail.

### sidequest-understudy built+merged; live-run gotchas and follow-ups (2026-06-11)
- Naive simulated-player client built from the 13-task plan, squash-merged to develop as PR #1 (`bbaa04f`); orchestrator wired at `b3f952fc` (`.pennyfarthing/repos.yaml` — NOT a top-level repos.yaml, plan was wrong — + `just understudy <manifest>` recipe + .gitignore). 50 tests green incl. full-loop wiring test.
- Live-run fix (PR #3 `b92c01e`): every seat defaulted to Game-mode Solo → N seats = N private solo sessions; persona system prompt now carries "tonight's table" social contract (party size, shared-multiplayer intent when seats>1, agreed world, humanized slugs) via required kwargs world/genre/party_size on build_system_prompt/SeatRunner. Verified live in two_seat_mp_shakeout (PR #4).

### Content-only WN combat de-nativize is blocked by the server model (2026-06-14, 108-3)
- Story 108-3 (strip `beats:`/`resolution_mode:beat_selection` from the WWN COMBAT confrontation def so combat resolves through `wn_round.py` only, ADR-143) CANNOT be done as a content-only change against the current server. Verified on origin/develop (not a stale checkout).
- Two hard loader/model blocks: (1) `genre/models/rules.py` `ConfrontationDef._validate` ends with an unconditional `if not self.beats: raise(...)` (develop:590) — stripping the beats list → pack fails to load. (2) `genre/loader.py:~701` validates every `allowed_classes` class's `encounter_beat_choices` against the combat def's beats pool — all three WWN packs have classes referencing strike/brace/committed_blow/etc, so stripping → PackError for every class.
- 108-1 cut the resolution engine (`wn_round.py` routes by `isinstance(WithoutNumberRulesetModule)`, not by resolution_mode) but left the loader/model still REQUIRING native beats. `resolution_mode` defaults to `beat_selection` (rules.py:436) — deleting the line falls through to the native default, does NOT de-nativize.
- Required first (net-new server story, none exists): make `beats` optional (allow zero) for WN/hp_depletion combat defs when the ruleset owns combat, AND relax the `encounter_beat_choices`→combat-beats-pool validation for WN packs. Land that, THEN the 108-3 content strip can proceed.

### WN worlds do magic via core.spellcasting/core.effort, NOT the ADR-126 magic_state plugin (2026-06-10, from Keith)
- Keith directive: "whenever we get hit by this, take the Without Number rules, check epic 102." WN ruleset-module worlds (WWN: heavy_metal/elemental_harmony/barsoom; SWN: space_opera; CWN: neon_dystopia/road_warrior; AWN: mutant_wasteland) own magic through the WN RulesetModule, NOT the ADR-126 plugin framework.
- Casting state lives on the character: `core.spellcasting` (prepared/casts_remaining) + `core.effort` (source-keyed Effort). `WwnRulesetModule.resolve_spellcast` (game/ruleset/wwn.py:466) reads these, spends casts/Effort/System-Strain, emits `wwn.spell.cast`. `grep magic_state game/ruleset/*.py` is EMPTY. The cast spine is already done in epic 102 (102-1 PC-death downed seam, 102-2 in-combat cast, 102-3 free-play cast→magic_working, 102-4 turn model).
- Red herrings to AVOID: the `magic_init` AND-gate (server/magic_init.py:204, dup :322) and `magic_state: null` on a WWN world are NOT blockers — they govern the separate ADR-126 framework WWN worlds don't use; do NOT relax the gate or reconcile schema as a fix. `long_foundry/magic.yaml` (78KB, ADR-126 schema) is orphaned draft content — quarantine/retire it; its misleading `magic.init_skipped` span wastes investigation time.
- Real remaining gaps: combat reachability (fight-seeker can't reach a WWN combat in a social opening); caster statting (headless `strategy: auto` chargen ignores scenario `class:` and rolls a martial PC); live re-verify the spine fires. Decision doc: `2026-06-10-wn-worlds-own-magic-not-magic_state.md`.

### Bestiary moved genre→world tier; half-wiring broke 4 ruleset packs (2026-06-06, server #723/#724)
- The genre/world repoint (content b3bc387/#378) moved creature rosters from `{genre}/bestiary.yaml` to `worlds/<slug>/bestiary.yaml` but the server wasn't taught to read the world tier → for every non-native pack whose genre bestiary was deleted (heavy_metal/wwn, elemental_harmony/wwn, space_opera/swn, road_warrior/cwn) `pack.bestiary` became None → encountergen failed loud across ALL worlds → Monster Manual broken, 21 authored world bestiaries dead.
- Fix (server PR #724): mirror `effective_cultures`/`effective_archetypes`. `World` gains a `bestiary` field (loader `_load_yaml_optional(..., Bestiary)`); `GenrePack.effective_bestiary(world) -> (Bestiary|None, source)` resolves world-over-genre with REPLACE semantics; encountergen's `pack.bestiary` reads (~360, ~801) now call it. REPLACE isolates canonical rosters (barsoom draws only Plant-Man of Dor / Red Martian Panthan — Keith's "keep barsoom canonical").
- Two creature schemas coexist (engine-imposed): world `creatures.yaml` (`regions.{r}.creatures`, simple threat_level/hp/ac/damage/morale-string, `creature_to_enemy_block`) is checked FIRST in encountergen main() and returns early, shadowing; world/genre `bestiary.yaml` (`entries:`, WWN-faithful level/hp/armor_class/attack_bonus/skill/save/morale#, `generate_enemy_from_bestiary`) is the ruleset path. flickering_reach has both; creatures.yaml wins.
- Companion bug (PR #381): the same repoint deleted `elemental_harmony/tropes.yaml` holding 9 universal abstract base tropes that world tropes `extends:` → `GenreMissingParentError` → unloadable pack; restored. Abstract base tropes are rulebook structure (genre tier), not flavor — over-deleted.

### Anthropic cost/usage API gotchas (2026-05-25)
- `scripts/anthropic_usage.py` (oq-2 root) pulls org Usage & Cost via `ANTHROPIC_ADMIN_KEY` (an `sk-ant-admin…` key in `~/.zshrc` line 53). The Bash-tool shell does NOT inherit it — re-export from `~/.zshrc` before running.
- `cost_report` `amount` is a STRING in CENTS, not dollars, despite `"currency":"USD"`. Divide by 100 (verified against console: 17198.23758 → $171.98). Summing as float-dollars produced a fake "$38,927 crisis" that was really $389.
- `cost_report` needs day-aligned (midnight UTC) bounds; a sub-day window 400s with "ending date must be after starting date" — the script floors to midnight, spans ≥1 full day.
- The Usage/Cost API has NO turn/request count, only token aggregates. Cost-PER-TURN is computed server-side: `sidequest-server` `anthropic_cost.cost_band(cost_usd)` bands per-turn `narration.turn.total_cost_usd` (<$0.05 all_systems_go / [$0.05,$0.12] needs_work / >$0.12 stop_everything) and emits `narration.turn.cost_band`.
- Always reconcile a derived $ figure against the console before announcing it — the numbers were real but the wrong UNIT was asserted as conclusion.

### ADR-113 confidence-gating is NOT operational — every dispatch fires (2026-06-06)
- Per sidequest-server CLAUDE.md (Intent Router): the ADR-113 mechanical-engagement spine is "structurally live but operationally under validation — per-dispatch confidence scoring and threshold-gating are NOT implemented (every dispatch in the package fires)."
- The `_threshold_for` / `DEFAULT_DISPATCH_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD=0.6` / `decision=degraded_to_hint` code in `agents/subsystems/__init__.py` exists but is NOT the live gating path.
- So a "confrontation/dispatch didn't fire because confidence < threshold" hypothesis is wrong by default — verify against the log before routing it. Burned a code-trace agent on this.

### gh CLI 401s — invalid GITHUB_TOKEN shadows keyring login (2026-05-19)
- `gh` commands fail with `HTTP 401: Bad credentials` / "The token in GITHUB_TOKEN is invalid" even though `gh auth status` shows a valid keyring login for `slabgorb` (ssh, `repo` scope). Cause: an invalid `GITHUB_TOKEN` env var is exported and `gh` prefers the env token over the keyring.
- Fix: prefix every `gh` invocation with `env -u GITHUB_TOKEN` (e.g. `env -u GITHUB_TOKEN gh pr create --base develop ...`). Apply pre-emptively at every SM finish-phase PR create/merge — don't re-diagnose. The keyring token's `repo` scope suffices.
- cwd persists across Bash calls, so run each repo's `gh pr merge` from that repo dir or pass `--repo`.

### PD notation sourcing for sidequest-composer — IMSLP rights trap, OMR, soundfonts (2026-06-14)
- sidequest-composer renders PD *notation* → rights-free OGG; it does NOT ingest recordings (those go to assets/audio/jazz_pd/ as direct OGG transcodes). Catalog files at `sidequest-content/genre_packs/assets/audio/{ragtime_pd,classical_pd,...}/catalog.yaml` ARE composer manifests; `scripts/render_pd_audio.py` renders+uploads. Per-entry `backend`/`soundfont` survive the render (`{**entry}`); top-level manifest fields are dropped.
- IMSLP rights trap: "on IMSLP" ≠ public domain (it hosts modern copyrighted works). Clean born-digital PDFs skew modern/©; genuinely-PD 1920s ragtime skews old scans. Vet BOTH composition PD (first published ≤ year−96; ≤1930 in 2026) AND the IMSLP per-file license (CC-BY ok w/ attribution; CC-BY-NC and all-rights-reserved OFF-LIMITS — treat the game as commercial). Mutopia has only Joplin among ragtime composers.
- OMR (PDF→MusicXML) feasible: `oemer` (pip, py3.12 only; needs np.int/np.float patches; unmaintained) made render-breaking errors even on clean engraving (key-sig sign flip, ~30% measures dropped). Use **Audiveris** (Java/JDK) for engraved scores. Spec parked: composer `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-14-omr-pdf-transcribe-stage.md`. PDF→PNG via `pdftoppm` (poppler).
- Composer soundfont reality: `mscore` CLI uses MS Basic (GM) soundfont, NOT premium Muse Sounds (CLI export can't reach MuseSampler). `fluidsynth` 2.5.5 installed; for brass-forward material wire FluidSynth + a permissive orchestral .sf2 via per-entry `soundfont:`. Piano ragtime is fine on MS Basic.

### Running R2 syncs/deletes — boto3 is in no venv; sync script unreliable (2026-05-29)
