- R2 ops against bucket `sidequest` (creds in shell env: `R2_S3_ENDPOINT` / `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`; boto3 client `region_name="auto"`). Keys mirror local path 1:1, e.g. `genre_packs/<g>/worlds/<w>/assets/{poi,portraits}/<slug>.png`.
- boto3 is NOT installed in any project venv (server/daemon/root). Run via ephemeral env: `uv run --with boto3 python - <<'PY' ... PY` from orchestrator root.
- `scripts/r2_sync_packs.py` is unreliable for scoped bulk uploads — `python -m scripts.r2_sync_packs --files <many>` failed opaquely (empty log, bare paths to stdout, no PUT/DONE) despite correct path-resolution logic. Don't trust its exit; verify against R2 with a `list_objects_v2` probe. For one-off reorgs an inline boto3 uploader (head_object ETag==md5 skip → put_object with `ContentType=image/png`, `CacheControl="public, max-age=31536000, immutable"`) is more observable. Deletes need `delete_objects` (max 1000/batch) — the sync script is upload-only.
- A reorg is NOT live until R2 sync runs (server serves from R2/CDN; PNGs are gitignored so git shows nothing).

### Movement/seam tests flaking with `'NonRecordingSpan' object has no attribute 'attributes'` → the telemetry MIRROR crashes the dispatch (2026-06-23, 158-7)
- **The trap:** `telemetry/spans/movement.py::_mirror_movement_span_to_sink` (called on `__exit__` of `movement_resolved_span`/`movement_unresolved_span`/`movement_region_mode_span`) reads `span.attributes` via `route.extract(span)` to mirror movement events into the Postgres sink. When NO recording `TracerProvider` is installed (a test that doesn't use a tracer fixture), `Span.open` yields a `NonRecordingSpan` which has NO `.attributes` → `AttributeError` propagates OUT of `run_movement_dispatch`, the bank catches it as a dispatch failure, and ANY test driving a movement/seam crossing without `otel_capture`/`capture_spans`/`watcher_setup` fails. This is order-independent (fails in isolation) — it's not "pollution," it's a telemetry side-channel that raises into the engine it wraps.
- **Fix:** guard the mirror — `if not hasattr(span, "attributes"): return`. A non-recording span carries nothing to mirror; production always has a recording provider (`init_tracer` at startup) so the mirror fires unchanged there. **Rule: a telemetry helper must NEVER raise into the instrumented code path.** When you add an inline span-attribute read on `__exit__`, guard it for `NonRecordingSpan`.
- A second bug hid behind this one: with the mirror fixed, `test_move_toward_uncommitted_edge_sync_materializes` then hit `'_FakeHandle' object has no attribute 'drain'` — the `_FakeHandle` double in `test_movement_dispatch.py` lagged the real `LookaheadWorkerHandle.drain()` (the affordance-race fix at movement.py). Test doubles drift from the real interface; when production adds a method the handler calls, the double needs it too.

### Full-suite-only flakes with a leaked `session_slug` / `_telemetry_sink` → watcher_hub PROCESS-GLOBAL fallback survives the test (2026-06-23, 158-7)
- **The trap:** `watcher_hub.bind_session_slug(slug)` / `bind_event_store(sink)` set BOTH a per-task ContextVar AND a **process-global** fallback (`_process_session_slug` / `_telemetry_sink`). The ContextVar resets when the test's asyncio task ends; the **process-global does NOT**. So a test that binds a slug (e.g. `test_resources_wired_on_session_create` → `resume-light-current-preserved`) leaks it to the next test in the same xdist worker — `test_publish_event_shape` then saw `ev["session_slug"] == 'resume-light-current-preserved'` instead of `None`. Passes in isolation, fails in the full `-n auto` run; the failing SET shifts run-to-run by worker assignment.
- **Fix:** the autouse `_watcher_hub_event_store_isolation` guard (`tests/server/conftest.py`) now save/restores BOTH `_telemetry_sink` AND `_process_session_slug`. CRUCIAL: `tests/integration/` is a SIBLING of `tests/server/` and does NOT inherit that conftest — re-export the guard's NAME in `tests/integration/conftest.py` (autouse flag travels with the import — the documented tripod pattern) or the integration leaker stays unguarded.
- **Diagnosing this class:** when a test "passes alone, fails in full suite," get the REAL assertion (the bank/runner truncates it) by driving the path bank-less via `uv run python -c`, or `pytest <test> --tb=long`. The culprit is almost always a watcher_hub/tracer PROCESS-GLOBAL (not the ContextVar). Reproduce serially with `pytest <suspected-polluter-dir>/ <victim> -n0` rather than bisecting 14k tests.
- **Pre-existing combat-engine full-suite flakes (FLAG, don't chase):** the WWN cast-spell (ADR-143) + AWN mutation (ADR-102) + ship-combat/dogfight integration tests fail non-deterministically in the full `-n auto` suite (different members each run) AND assert behavior the in-flight WN-combat engine intentionally drops (`wn_combat_beat_dropped_engine_owns_round`). These are epic-108 territory — do NOT bundle them into an unrelated story. They pass in isolation/their own directory; the full-suite failure is pre-existing pollution + unfinished combat wiring, not your regression.

### Re-introducing a RETIRED behavior? Grep existing tests for assertions that pin the OLD contract — TEA's RED won't catch the conflict (2026-06-22, 153-29)
- **The trap:** Story 153-29 RE-introduced the POV pronoun passes (his→your, he→you, him→you) that were RETIRED 2026-05-23. TEA wrote 13 new RED tests for the new contract and updated a few siblings — but THREE pre-existing tests still asserted the *retired* behavior (possessive SURVIVES: `test_adverb_between_subject_and_verb_conjugates` "his hands", `test_interrupter_conjugation_they_them_parity` "their hands", `test_possessive_his_after_comma_not_de_pluralized` "his copper face"). Those three currently PASS against pre-impl code (the passes don't exist yet), so TEA's RED verification is GREEN-on-them and the conflict is **invisible until Dev implements**. The new AC and the stale tests cannot coexist (identical grammar — no regex distinguishes "his hands" from "his shield").
- **Detection before coding:** when a story REVERSES previously-retired behavior, `grep -n 'assert.*\b\(his\|her\|their\|him\|them\)\b' <test_file>` and read every hit. A docstring that says "under the retired-X contract …" is a dead giveaway the assertion is now stale. Catch these in the analysis phase, not after the GREEN run breaks them.
- **Resolution (Dev CAN edit tests here, but log it loud):** Spec Authority Hierarchy — story scope (the AC) outranks a stale test. Update the stale assertion to the new contract while PRESERVING the test's load-bearing purpose (here: the verb-conjugation / #708 de-pluralization guards stayed; only the incidental possessive-survives line changed). File a Design Deviation + a non-blocking Delivery Finding so Reviewer/TEA can scrutinize. Do NOT special-case the implementation to keep a stale test green — "make `his hands` survive but `his shield` convert" is impossible and a worse violation.

### pov_swap pronoun agreement is CLAUSE-local — split the engine-sentence on `;`, don't use a whole-sentence flag (2026-06-22, 153-29)
- The engine "sentence" (`_split_into_sentences`) breaks only on `.!?`, so ONE sentence can hold an NPC clause after a `;` ("Carl plants a boot; the moth shudders against him"). The 2026-05-23 NPC-bleed bug lived exactly there. The gate that makes re-introduced pronoun passes safe must be CLAUSE-local: `_rewrite_sentence` now `re.split(r"(;)", …)` (separators preserved for verbatim rejoin) and dispatches each clause to `_rewrite_clause`, which carries a per-clause `name_swap_occurred` flag (armed by Pass 1 possessive-name OR Pass 2/3 subject-name) gating the possessive/subject/object passes. A clause that never names the PC is never armed → its pronouns survive (AC 4).
- **Side effect to know:** processing per-clause also makes the pre-existing `had_subject_swap`-gated passes (reflexive, "and"-verb, comma-verb) clause-local — previously a subject swap in clause 1 could conjugate a coordinated verb in a post-`;` clause 2. No test covered it; the narrowing is strictly safer. Any future pass added to `_rewrite_clause` is automatically clause-scoped.
- **she/her "her" is both possessive AND object** — disambiguate by lookahead: possessive `\bher\b(?=\s+\w)` (governs a following noun → "your"), object `\bher\b(?!\s+\w)` (clause-final / pre-punctuation → "you"). he/him ("his"/"him") and they/them ("their"/"them") have distinct surface forms, so no lookahead needed — drive the pattern off `_PRONOUN_FORMS[pronouns]`, converting ONLY the PC's own set (an unrelated NPC pronoun set in the clause is left alone). Run possessive→subject→object; subject pronoun ("she strikes") also conjugates its following verb like Pass 2.
- **Capitalization across `;` (CORRECTED in review rework r2 — the round-1 fix was incomplete):** adding a `clause_is_sentence_start` param to `_is_sentence_start_in` is NOT enough by itself. `re.split(r"(;)", s)` leaves the space that followed the `;` on the next clause, so a clause-initial name sits at idx **1**, not 0 — the `if idx == 0` branch never fires. The real path is the whitespace walk-back at the bottom: `while text[j].isspace(): j -= 1; if j < 0: return ...`. That `j < 0` line must ALSO return `clause_is_sentence_start` (it returned a hardcoded `True`, which capitalized "…; You step" mid-sentence and made `_is_proper_noun_fragment` read a post-`;` name fragment as a sentence opener, re-opening 153-14 as "…; Vah you bow"). Lesson: when you split text into sub-units, EVERY position-context helper (`_is_sentence_start_in`, `_is_proper_noun_fragment`) must be threaded the boundary AND you must trace which internal branch actually executes for the real (leading-space) input — a green suite with no boundary test will not catch it. Probe with `uv run python -c` on `;`-containing inputs, names in non-first clauses, and NPC-name collisions after `;`.

### The SWN/space_opera chargen "recap" is CONTENT prose, NOT chargen_summary.py (2026-06-21, 153-15)
- The space_opera **confirmation-scene narration** ("This Is Who You Are" / "Aboard the Kestrel" / "This Is Who the Cloud Made") IS the chargen "recap". Its prose lives in CONTENT — `genre_packs/space_opera/worlds/<world>/char_creation.yaml`, the `confirmation` scene's `narration` — with `{name}`/`{race}`/`{class}` placeholders. `server/dispatch/chargen_summary.py::render_confirmation_summary` renders the FIELD-based preview CARD (`Race: …`, `Class: …`) + a humanized backstory line; it does NOT own the recap PROSE. So a "recap grammar/prose" finding (e.g. SWN-CHARGEN-RECAP-GRAMMAR) is a CONTENT fix even when the story is tagged `repos: server` and setup pointed at chargen_summary.py. (153-15's fix touched 3 content files + 1 server test, NOT chargen_summary.py.)
- Placeholder substitution is `game/builder.py::interpolate_scene_narration` (server): `{race}` → `acc.race_hint` (the mechanical demonym/slug, BY DESIGN — comment at builder.py ~1686: the prose slot keeps the hint; the summary FIELD uses race_label); `{class}` → `acc.class_label or acc.class_hint`; `{name}` → scene name or lobby name. `substitute_token_with_article` only fixes a/an agreement (definite "The" is untouched). `{background}` is NOT a supported placeholder — unknown tokens get stripped and emit `chargen.scene_narration_unrecognized_placeholder`.
- In space_opera the `origins` scene sets `race_hint` to a demonym (Coreworlder/Colonial/Spacer/Uplifted) and `background` to an adjective (Core-educated/Frontier-raised/Void-born/Constructed). So "The {class} from {race} space" rendered "The Pilot from Spacer space" — redundant. 153-15 fix = reorder to "The {race} {class}" → "The Spacer Pilot" across the 3 worlds (aureate_span, coyote_star, perseus_cloud).
- **sidequest-content has NO Python test harness of its own** — content-prose regressions go in the SERVER suite (`tests/genre/`) because the genre loader reads the sibling content checkout. Pattern: `GenreLoader().load("space_opera")` → `pack.worlds[slug].char_creation` (keyed by world slug) → find `confirmation` scene → assert on `.narration`; plus a render-wiring test driving `CharacterBuilder.interpolate_scene_narration` through a space_opera-shaped accumulator. See `tests/genre/test_153_15_swn_recap_race_class_noun.py`.

### encountergen enemy blocks carry NO creature_id — name is the only join back to the bestiary; and a new `pack.<method>` read breaks SimpleNamespace loader-stubs (2026-06-20, 157-2)
- **The bestiary join key is the NAME.** `encountergen.generate_enemy_from_bestiary` (`sidequest/cli/encountergen/encountergen.py`) builds the EnemyBlock with `class_="creature"` (a literal) and `name=entry.name` (verbatim) — it does NOT carry `entry.id`/`creature_id`. So any code that needs to map a seeded encounter's enemies back to their `BestiaryEntry` (e.g. epic-157's `pregen._encounter_factions` unioning faction tags) MUST join by `name` (case-insensitive), not id. `MonsterManual.find_enemy_by_name` already does this for the same reason. If you ever need a robust id join, you'd have to add `creature_id=entry.id` to the EnemyBlock first.
- **Adding a `pack.effective_X()` call breaks the test loader-stubs.** `pregen.seed_manual` does `pack = load_genre_pack(dir)`; tests monkeypatch `load_genre_pack` to a `SimpleNamespace` (`test_pregen.py::_stub_pack`) that hand-mimics ONLY the pack methods seed_manual reads (`effective_cultures`, `effective_archetypes`). Add a new read (`pack.effective_bestiary(world)`) and every stub-based test `AttributeError`s. Fix = add the method to the stub (`pack.effective_bestiary = lambda _world: (None, "stub")`) — a real `GenrePack` always has it, so this is matching the stub to the real type, NOT weakening production. Don't reach for a getattr-default in production to paper over the stub (that's a silent fallback; a real pack never lacks the method, and `pack is not None` already guards load failure).
- **The server suite has a KNOWN pre-existing failure baseline (~88–96, flaky by xdist/infra) — see the baseline note below.** When verifying a change, an ADR-145 "genre catalog must be mode=verbatim" inventory-validation failure at *pack load* (e.g. `test_pregen_fail_loud_90_5`, `test_encountergen_bestiary_90_1`, items in `tests/fixtures/packs/test_genre/inventory.yaml`) is part of that baseline, NOT your regression — confirm by (a) `git status` showing your diff touches no inventory/validator/fixture file and (b) `git log -1 --format=%ci -- <validator> <fixture>` predating your branch base. A throwaway `git worktree` of develop can't run server tests (the venv lacks `claude_agent_sdk`, provisioned out-of-band in the main `.venv`), so causal-trace + timestamps is the practical proof.

### De-nativizing a Fate confrontation SEAT: the marker is `win_condition`, NOT `enc.contest` (2026-06-20, 126-30)
- Story 126-30 (Keith ruling 2026-06-19): under `ruleset=='fate'` a standoff/conflict must seat as a pure Fate conflict (4dF + ablative stress → taken-out), with the native `opponent_metric.tension` dial REMOVED — the upstream half of #964 (#964 only gated the native overlay PAYLOAD off via `should_emit_native_confrontation`; seating in `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` still stamped `win_condition='dial_threshold'` + tension metrics).
- **The trap:** TEA's HOW-agnostic RED accepts EITHER `enc.contest is not None` OR `win_condition != 'dial_threshold'`. Do NOT reach for `enc.contest` — `dispatch_fate_action`/`dispatch_fate_defense` (`server/dispatch/fate_conflict.py`) select the Contest engine (first-to-N victories) vs the Conflict engine (stress→taken-out) on **`encounter.contest is not None`**. A stress-based conflict MUST keep `contest=None`, so stamping a Contest would route a standoff to the WRONG engine. The de-nativization marker therefore has to move `win_condition` off `dial_threshold`.
- **The fix (3 seams in `encounter_lifecycle.py::instantiate_encounter_from_trigger`):** `is_fate = pack.rules.ruleset=='fate'`; `seat_as_fate_conflict = is_fate and resolution_mode not in (contest, sealed_letter_lookup)`. When true: (1) synthesize inert `fate_stress` placeholder metrics (the native dial REMOVED — same belt-and-suspenders 1e6-threshold pattern as `hp_depletion`); (2) stamp `win_condition='fate_conflict'`; (3) emit `fate.conflict.seeded` (new OTEL lie-detector, sibling to `fate.contest.seeded`). Separately gate the WHOLE native combat-seeding block on `not is_fate` (`if cdef.category=="combat" and not is_fate:`) — TEA's Improvement: it leaks `_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs`/`_publish_combat_edge_to_npcs` AND `_roll_and_persist_initiative` CRASHES under Fate (FateConfig has no DEXTERITY map). The opponent FateSheet (the real win track) is already seeded by `_seed_fate_opponents` (runs for every Fate confrontation).
- **`win_condition` Literal vs `WinCondition` enum are DELIBERATELY decoupled.** `StructuredEncounter.win_condition` is a `Literal[...]` string (NOT the `genre.models.rules.WinCondition` enum) to avoid a `game`→`genre.models` import cycle (see the field docstring). So an ENGINE-ONLY runtime value like `fate_conflict` — which content never authors — goes in the **Literal only**, never the content enum. Safe because every native reader of `enc.win_condition` is an open `== "dial_threshold"`/`== "hp_depletion"` check (no exhaustive switch exists — audit with `grep 'enc\.win_condition\|encounter\.win_condition'`), and `dial_threshold_outcome()` already returns None for any non-`dial_threshold` value. The native overlay readers (`build_confrontation_payload`, `beat_filter`) are gated off for Fate anyway.
- **Verification (matches the existing 88-failure baseline note below):** full server suite failure count is FLAKY run-to-run (saw 89→92→96 — xdist worker assignment + infra-dependent integration/e2e). Don't trust counts; capture sorted `FAILED|ERROR` with-changes vs `git stash`-baseline and `comm` the two — they were IDENTICAL except this story's RED→GREEN flip. A non-Fate pack change here is a behavioral NO-OP (`is_fate=False` → identical control flow), so any SWN/WWN-suite diff is flakiness — confirm by running it `-n0` in isolation (the `test_firefight…hp_depletion` "regression" passed 3/3 solo).

### NarratorDirectives must render through ONE player-safe boundary — raw `[{kind}]` tokens leak machinery into player prose (2026-06-19, playtest must_not_narrate leak)
- Symptom (annees_folles playtest): the narrator printed its own machinery to the PLAYER — *"The must_not_narrate constraint is clear … I cannot narrate Roy picking up … an envelope that doesn't exist."* Root cause was a ONE-LINE render in `orchestrator.py` (group-C directive block): `block = "\n".join(f"- [{d.kind}] {d.payload}" …)`. The raw `NarratorDirectiveKind` token (`must_not_narrate`, `must_narrate`, `distinctive_detail_for_referent`, `canonical_only_do_not_reveal_to_others`) went into the prompt as a visible label with NO framing that these are silent stage directions → the model parroted the token AND broke frame to explain the constraint.
- Fix pattern: a single render boundary `sidequest/agents/narrator_directives.py::render_narrator_directives(directives)` maps each kind → an in-fiction imperative carrying NO machinery vocabulary ("Keep out of the fiction", "Bring into the scene", …), wrapped in `<stage-directions>` framing that says obey silently + show absence IN-WORLD (hand finds only what's there) rather than break frame. Import-time `assert set(map)==set(get_args(NarratorDirectiveKind))` fails loud if a new kind is added unmapped (No Silent Fallbacks).
- Reusable lessons: (1) any enum/token that reaches an LLM prompt as a label is a leak risk — the model will echo it; route ALL narrator-facing machinery through one mapping that emits prose, never the token. (2) `get_args(SomeLiteral)` + a module-load completeness assert is the clean fail-loud guard for "every kind must be handled". (3) Tests that assert `"must_not_narrate" in prompt` were codifying the LEAK — three prompt tests (`test_lethality_directives_in_prompt`, `test_group_c_e2e`, `test_group_c_wiring`) asserted the raw token; the fix flips them to assert the imperative + keeps the payload assertions. The strict no-token regression lives on the pure render fn, not the whole-prompt (avoids fragility if another section legitimately names the concept).
- Baseline caveat (reconfirmed): oq-1 `develop` carries ~92 pre-existing full-suite failures (WN combat / epic-108 in-flight, chargen-e2e, dice/spell-cast, content). Prove a change is clean by DIFFING the failure SET before/after (stash -u + re-run), not by the count — and re-run a lone "new" failure with `-n0` to rule out xdist flakiness (one did exactly this here).

### claude-agent-sdk defaults extended thinking ON — the narrator silently got it at the 119-3 SDK port, 3x latency (2026-06-17, 126-9 root cause)
- The `anthropic` Messages SDK has extended thinking OFF unless you pass a budget; the **claude-agent-sdk `query()` loop defaults thinking ON ("adaptive")**. PR #908 (`f970091e`, 119-3) ported the narrator Messages-SDK→agent-sdk and thereby flipped narrator thinking off→on with NO code saying so. Result: sonnet-4.6 runs a thinking pass before EACH of up to 8 tool-loop iterations (`orchestrator.py:4089` max_iterations=8) → `agent_duration_ms` 16s→50-57s. Same-world proof: wry_whimsy/oz 15.9s→56.7s.
- The ONLY auto-disable is `anthropic_sdk_client.py:262-263`: `if output_format is not None and thinking is None: thinking={"type":"disabled"}` — fires for STRUCTURED calls only. The narrator tool-loop (`complete_with_tools`, builds options ~`:438`) passes neither `thinking` nor `output_format`, so the disable never fires and the CLI default (ON) wins. PR #919's own message says the narrator/aside loops are "untouched". Fix = pass `thinking={"type":"disabled"}` on the narrator call too (behavior-restore, NOT a quality cut — narrator never had thinking pre-Jun-16). Do NOT reach for `SIDEQUEST_NARRATOR_ITERATION_CAP` (caps narration depth/quality; conflates two things).
- General lesson: porting an LLM call across SDKs silently changes defaults (thinking, max_tokens, stop conditions). When swapping `anthropic` ↔ claude-agent-sdk, re-pin `thinking` explicitly on EVERY call site.

### Per-turn latency lives in Postgres `turn_telemetry`, NOT logs/SQLite — and the phase keys aren't what you'd guess (2026-06-17, 126-9)
- Source of truth for turn timing: Postgres db `sidequest`, table `turn_telemetry`, rows `component='validator' AND event_type='turn_complete'`. `payload_json` carries `total_duration_ms`, `phase_durations_ms{}`, `phase_call_counts{}`, `agent_duration_ms` (the narrator), `_unaccounted_ms`. SQLite saves are legacy/archive only (ADR-115). Server logs have NO line timestamps — use telemetry, not the log, for wall-clock.
- Phase-key vocabulary (people guess wrong, incl. me this session): router phase = **`intent_router_pass`** (wraps router + dispatch_bank); narrator = **`agent_duration_ms`**. There is NO `preprocess_llm` and NO `narrator_subprocess` key. Full set: turn_context_build, intent_router_pass, dispatch_bank, lethality_arbiter, entity_retrieval, lore_retrieval, prompt_build, narrator_extraction, state_apply, persistence, broadcast, dispatch_post, mp_barrier_wait.
- `phase_call_counts.intent_router_pass > 1` = the router-thrash signature. Across 339k rows it is ALWAYS 1 — no thrash (the historical 8×/turn storm is fixed by `suppress_intent_router`). The Timing tab (`#/dashboard` tab 4, story 124-3) renders all this live and IS functional.

### Cross-repo content↔server work has a MERGE-ORDER coupling — content must land on develop before server (2026-06-17, fate-contest-binding)
- The server suite reads genre packs from `../sidequest-content` at runtime. When a change spans both repos (e.g. converting `opposed_check`→`contest` defs in content + the engine/tests in server), the server's converted-pack tests assert the NEW content shape. If server CI pins `sidequest-content@develop`, those tests stay RED on develop until the **content** PR merges. So: merge the **content PR → develop FIRST** (or atomically), then server. Flag this in both PR bodies. The same coupling is why you can't "verify" a content conversion by running the server full suite mid-flight — the on-disk content branch masks/unmasks content-induced failures (the true baseline requires BOTH repos on the same ref).
- Companion: a full-suite "N failed" baseline can also carry pre-existing **ERRORS** (not just FAILED) that a `grep FAILED`-built baseline file won't capture — e.g. `_FakeClaudeClient has no attribute send_stateless` fake-drift in WN-narrator tests. Confirm "pre-existing" by reproducing on detached `origin/develop` (both repos), not by trusting the count alone. (See the 114-12 hermeticity gotcha below for the same lesson.)

### The per-session cost OTEL surfaces are split across TWO modules — adding a field to "all cost events" means editing both (119-4 green, 2026-06-16)
- ADR-134 cost telemetry lives in two files, and which module emits a given event is non-obvious: `narrator.sdk.usage` and `session.cost_running_total` fire from `anthropic_sdk_client.py` (the narrator client's own methods); `session.cost_ceiling_exceeded` and `cost_runaway_suspected` fire from `cost_safety.py` (`SessionCostLedger.update_cumulative` / `check_and_emit_runaway`, which the client *delegates* to — story 91-4 moved the ledger out of the client). So a task like "label every cost figure `cost_basis='notional'`" requires edits in BOTH modules; touching only `anthropic_sdk_client.py` silently misses the ceiling/runaway events. The tell: `grep -rn '_watcher_publish_event(' sidequest/agents/anthropic_sdk_client.py sidequest/agents/cost_safety.py` enumerates every emit site before you start.
- Constant-sharing caveat: `cost_safety` is imported BY `anthropic_sdk_client` at module top, so you CANNOT import a label constant the other way (cycle). Use a module-local constant in the client and the bare literal in `cost_safety` (or vice-versa) — don't reach across.

### When wrapping an `async for x in query(...)` loop to map raised transport errors to a typed error, re-raise the already-typed error FIRST (119-4 green, 2026-06-16)
- Pattern for mapping a raised SDK/transport exception (absent/expired login surfaces as a raised `query()` exception, OQ-5) to a typed domain error without double-wrapping: order the handlers `except AgentSdkAuthUnavailable: raise` BEFORE `except Exception as exc: ...raise AgentSdkAuthUnavailable(...) from exc`. The broad handler must use `from exc` (preserve the cause) and emit the GM-panel event BEFORE the raise (No Silent Fallbacks — the failure must be *visible*, not just loud). Re-indenting the loop body under the new `try:` is an unavoidable but mechanical diff; ruff-format it after and the only real change is the wrapper.

### A stored-but-unread field (and any docstring/comment claiming behavior the code doesn't do) is a HONESTY rejection even when the feature works green end-to-end (2026-06-16, 118-5 green-rework)
- 118-5 was functionally correct, fully wired, Fate suite green — and Reviewer REJECTED it. Not a functional rejection: a **quality/honesty** one. AC-1 said "store the offered fate point delta", so Dev added `PendingCompel.offered_delta` (default 1) — but `fate_projection` dropped it and the UI button hardcoded `Accept (+1 FP)`. The field was **stored but never projected or read** (No Stubbing / dead-code violation) and its docstring claimed "the fate point the player GAINS by accepting" — a lie, because nothing read it. A hardcoded display literal shadowing a stored field is also a **divergence vector** the OTEL lie-detector doctrine forbids (engine could grant X while UI shows a stale literal — the El Dorado lie).
- **Rule — when an AC says "store X", wire X end-to-end: store → project → display.** The displayed value must READ the real datum (`+{c.offered_delta}`), never a hardcoded literal that can drift. If a field is genuinely forward-only/unused, that contradicts the AC — escalate; don't leave it dead. Adding the field is half the work; reading it is the other half.
- **Reconcile honesty with bind-don't-balance:** keep the engine economy SRD-fixed (accept = +1 via `earn_fate_point`; don't make the grant read a variable field — that converts an SRD constant into a homebrew tunable, ADR-144). Instead PIN the field's default to the SRD value at its single creation site (`add_pending_compel` exposes no override) so display / stored field / grant agree **by construction** and the field cannot lie. The display reads the field (anti-drift); the engine stays SRD.
- **Wiring-test docstrings must claim ONLY the path the test renders.** `GameBoard-fate-compel-wiring.test.tsx` rendered `<GameBoard>` directly (injecting `onFateAction` as a prop) yet its docstring claimed it proved the `App → GameBoard` production path (`App.tsx:2727 → GameBoard.tsx:644`). It never mounts `<App>`, so that link is untested — comment-analyzer flags this HIGH and the reviewer **cannot dismiss** it (same as the 107-1 precedent). Trim the claim to the GameBoard→Surface path actually exercised and name the App→GameBoard seam as the remaining untested link (or mount `<App>`). Also delete stale "FAIL today / (RED)" lines once the test is green.
- **Negative tests on shared fail-loud paths:** even when accept and refuse share one `find_pending_compel is None → raise`, the reviewer wants BOTH legs tested independently (phantom-accept AND phantom-refuse) so a future routing divergence can't slip a never-offered verb through. Same for guard branches (`not encounter.resolved`): pin the resolved branch (span fires, nothing persists) so the guard can't be silently deleted.

### A scary `git diff develop..branch` (huge churn, file MOVES) does NOT predict a painful rebase — the conflict set is the INTERSECTION of branch-touched and develop-touched files (2026-06-16, 114-12 reconciliation)
- A stale 114-12 branch showed `+1491/-3278` across 68 files vs develop, including `{server => foundation}` honest-layering moves — looked like a nightmare rebase. But the branch's REAL changeset (`merge-base..branch`) was only 5 files, and `comm -12` of (branch-touched-files) ∩ (develop-touched-files-since-merge-base) was **empty**. The rebase replayed with **0 conflicts**. The giant diff was all develop's drift on files the branch never touched.
- **Rule before any scary rebase:** compute `MB=$(git merge-base develop $BR)`, then `comm -12 <(git diff --name-only $MB..$BR|sort) <(git diff --name-only $MB..develop|sort)`. Empty → conflict-free rebase regardless of net-diff size. After rebasing, the authoritative no-regression check is `git diff --name-only develop..HEAD` — it must equal exactly your intended changeset; anything extra means you clobbered develop's work.
- **Direction matters on reparents:** ADR-147 honest-layering moves pure logic DOWN (`sidequest/server/* → sidequest/foundation/*`), not up. Don't assume the direction — `git ls-tree -r develop --name-only | grep <file>` to see where develop actually keeps it before calling a file "resurrected."

### A full-suite "N failures" after a rebase/checkout can be PRE-EXISTING develop debt — verify on untouched develop before attributing it to your change (2026-06-16, 114-12)
- testing-runner reported 272 full-suite failures on the rebased 114-12 branch and (wrongly) claimed they "pass on main" (there is no `main` in this gitflow repo — it hallucinated the baseline). Reality: `git switch --detach origin/develop` + run a representative failing file → **same 10/10 failures on untouched develop**. The failures were a hermeticity regression already on develop (new narrator paths construct the real Anthropic SDK in tests lacking the conftest fake), not from the 5-file rebase.
- **Rule:** never accept a subagent's "pre-existing / not your change" claim on faith, and never attribute mass failures to your diff without proof. Checkout untouched `origin/develop` (detached) and run one representative failing test. If it fails there too, it's develop's debt — log it as a delivery finding (loudly: real-SDK-in-tests can bill live API calls) and scope your green-check to your actual diff + its own tests.

### A test that reads `worlds/<world>/inventory.yaml` directly sees the WORLD tier ONLY — the genre-tier verbatim items it merges with are invisible, and dict `.get(id, {})` hides the drop (2026-06-15, 120-5)
- `test_starting_mounted_weapons_fit_in_starting_rig_slots` built its catalog from `yaml.safe_load(.../the_circuit/inventory.yaml)["item_catalog"]` — the WORLD tier alone (26 items). The class kits, though, reference genre-tier `cwn_*` verbatim gear (`cwn_medkit`, `cwn_binoculars`, ...) that only lives in the merged catalog (92 items). With a raw-dict catalog, `catalog.get(id, {}).get("tags")` returned `{}` → `[]` for every genre-tier id, so those items **silently vanished** from the check and the test passed against a catalog that was NOT the real chargen path.
- **Rule:** when a test needs the catalog a player actually gets at chargen, resolve it through `resolve_inventory(pack, world_slug)` (re-exported at `sidequest.server.dispatch.inventory_resolve`), NOT by reading the world `inventory.yaml`. The resolver unions genre baseline + world per ADR-145 D3 (`merge_inventory_catalog`) and applies world-replaces-genre for `starting_equipment`. That merged `InventoryConfig` is the truth.
- **Typed vs dict:** `resolve_inventory` returns typed `CatalogItem`s (`.id`/`.tags`/`.category`/`.damage`), but `parse_vessel_tags(item: dict)` wants a dict — bridge with `item.model_dump()`. And kill the silent `.get(id, {})`: assert the id is in the merged catalog (`assert not missing`) so a genuine dangling id fails loud (No Silent Fallbacks) instead of evaporating.

### Reconciling a COMPETING already-merged implementation: conflict markers are only half the job — the competitor's net-new files ride in SILENTLY (2026-06-13, 106-1 dup-impl)
- Story 106-1 was implemented twice in parallel; develop already had a competing impl (#836) that this branch had to SUPERSEDE per Keith's ruling. `git merge origin/develop` flagged the two overlapping files (`chargen_loadout.py`, `chargen_mixin.py`) as `UU` conflicts — easy to spot. But #836 ALSO added a brand-new span file section (`telemetry/spans/chargen.py` armor spans) and a whole new test file (`tests/server/test_106_1_chargen_armor_equip.py`) that my branch never touched. Those are **additions on `theirs` only → no conflict marker → they merge in clean and silently**, leaving orphaned dead spans + a test asserting the OLD span vocabulary that would either fail or rot.
- **Rule:** after resolving the `UU` files, enumerate what the competitor PR added that you're replacing: `git show <their-sha> --stat`. For each net-new file/section the loser added, decide explicitly. If your branch never touched that file and only the loser modified it on develop (`git log <base>..origin/develop -- <file>` shows only their commit AND `git diff <base> HEAD -- <file>` is empty), then `git checkout HEAD -- <file>` restores your (base) version, cleanly dropping their addition. For loser-only new files, `git rm -f <file>`. Then grep the whole tree for the loser's symbol names to confirm zero orphans before committing the merge.
- **Watch for accidental-agreement collisions:** both impls independently defined `SPAN_CHARGEN_ARMOR_EQUIPPED = "chargen.armor_equipped"` (mine in `inventory.py`, theirs in `chargen.py`). Restoring base `chargen.py` left exactly one definition — but if I'd taken theirs I'd have had a duplicate constant + double `SPAN_ROUTES` registration. Always grep the span-name STRING, not just the Python constant name.

### A `git merge origin/develop` can surface develop's PRE-EXISTING lint debt onto your branch (2026-06-13, 106-1)
- After the merge, `ruff check .` failed with `I001` on `tests/game/pg/test_telemetry_sink_missing_session.py` — a file I never touched, committed un-sorted by develop (700e7c82). develop HEAD itself was red on ruff; the merge just inherited it, and it would have failed my PR's lint gate. Fixed the one-line import sort in-passing (zero behavior) and logged it as a delivery finding rather than silently absorbing it. **Rule:** when a merge makes your branch fail a quality gate on a file outside your story, first check whether develop HEAD already fails it (`git log -1 origin/develop -- <file>`); if so it's inherited debt — fix the minimal thing to stay green and SURFACE it as a finding, don't let it look like your change.

### Don't trust the briefing's "server runs --reload / PID X" — verify `ps -o lstart` against the commit time (2026-06-13, beneath_sunden playtest)
- Briefing said "oq-1 uvicorn IS running --reload" and a later pingpong note claimed a FIXER bounce gave fresh PIDs. Reality: `ps -o pid,lstart,command` showed the live uvicorn was started `00:35`, had **no `--reload` flag**, and the fix commit was `00:55` — so the process was running **stale code 20 min older than the fix**, even though the working tree had it. I almost reported "fix is live" off the source grep alone.
- **Fix/rule:** to claim a server-side fix is LIVE, check the PROCESS, not the tree: `ps -o pid,lstart,command | grep uvicorn.*8765`. Compare `lstart` to `git show -s --format=%ci <commit>`. If the process predates the commit AND has no `--reload`, the fix is NOT running — a bounce is required. Even with `--reload`, a module that was already imported before the edit may not re-import cleanly; a clean bounce is the only certain path. Never infer runtime state from `grep <symbol> source.py`.
- A bounce drops the in-memory session — if a DRIVER/operator is live-playing, it's THEIR call to bounce, not yours. Surface the precise state and let them.

### Resolve the player's words; don't ban the narrator's vocabulary to match a dumb engine (2026-06-13, movement bearings)
- The "fix" I started toward was forbidding the narrator from saying "north/ahead/left" because the movement resolver couldn't resolve them (it tied 4 forward edges on "the corridor ahead"). Keith: "that's BULLSHIT… we can't say 'oh, we can't understand players saying I go north' even after we say 'to the north there is an exit'." That's the **Zork Problem in reverse** — narrowing the player's natural language instead of resolving it. SOUL violation.
- **Root cause was missing DATA, not loose prose:** the procedural region graph is an abstract topology (nodes + `(a,b,kind)` edges, never embedded in a plane), so no edge ever carried a direction — there was no "north" anywhere to resolve against. The honest fix (commit `1d21c71d`, Keith-authored) is `assign_bearings(graph, region_id)`: derive a **stable, distinct** bearing per exit from the graph via blake2b (compass for passages, up/down for shaft/chute/stairs by depth) — no persistence, identical across loads, works on the existing live save. One source, four consumers that now agree: `project_region.RegionExit.bearing`, the narrator EXIT VOCABULARY (now ENDORSES bearings — names each way out by one), the movement resolver (bearing-match path, highest priority — ≤1 exit per bearing so a tie is structurally impossible), and the `DungeonMapExit.bearing` UI frame.
- **Rule:** when an engine "can't understand" natural player input, ask whether the DATA to resolve it exists. If a subsystem is throwing away structure (here: how regions connect in space), give it the geometry — don't cripple the narration to fit the gap. Banning vocabulary to match a lossy engine is doubling down on the bug.

### pf protected-branch hook reads the branch BEFORE your compound command runs — `git checkout -b X && git commit` still blocks (2026-06-12)
- On a gitflow subrepo (`develop` is protected: sidequest-daemon, -content, -ui), the boilerplate `git checkout -b feat/… && git add … && git commit …` in ONE Bash call is BLOCKED by `pf hooks dispatch PreToolUse` with "Cannot commit to protected branch 'develop'." The PreToolUse hook evaluates the shell's *current* branch at dispatch time — i.e. BEFORE the `checkout` inside the same compound has run — so it still sees `develop` and refuses. The whole tool call is rejected; the `checkout` never executes either (verified: `git branch --show-current` still `develop` afterward).
- **Fix:** create/switch the branch in its OWN Bash call first (`git checkout -b feat/…`), confirm with `git branch --show-current`, THEN run `git add`/`git commit` in a separate call. Same root cause as the cwd gotcha in the handoff (hook reads persistent shell state, not post-command state) — applies to the branch, not just cwd.
- Staging is fine in the compound; it's only the `commit` the hook gates. Creating the branch separately is the whole fix.

### `git add -A` in a subrepo sweeps strangers' untracked files into your story commit (2026-06-09, story 98-3)
- sidequest-ui had a stray untracked `src/styles/reference.css` (epic-100 leftover, present before story setup). The dev-workflow boilerplate `git add . && git commit` swept it into the 98-3 feature commit and it got PUSHED before I noticed. Fix: `git rm --cached <file> && git commit --amend --no-edit && git push --force-with-lease`.
- **Rule of thumb:** check the FIRST `git status` of the session for pre-existing `??` entries; stage explicitly (`git add <paths-I-touched>`) or at minimum eyeball `git status --short` output between add and commit. Pre-existing untracked files belong to nobody's branch — leave them untracked.

### Before deleting shipped content a loader reads, grep BOTH tests and runtime loader callers (2026-06-09, story 98-1)
- Story C1 deleted `perseus_cloud/orbits.yaml` (the monolith) as the AC4-preferred path. Safe only after confirming: (1) no server test loads the real file — `tests/orbital/test_scope_bind.py` *mentions* perseus_cloud in comments but actually uses a synthetic `fixtures/world_sector_join`, so deletion didn't regress it (355/355 orbital green); (2) runtime callers of `load_orbital_content` (`grep -rln load_orbital_content sidequest/`) degrade gracefully — `session_room.py:259` catches `OrbitalContentMissingError` → `orbital_content=None` (chart unavailable, no crash), but `rest.py` also calls it and may not, so flag the endpoint for the loader-rewrite story.
- **Rule of thumb:** deleting content that a production loader resolves by hard-coded filename is a runtime behavior change even when the test suite stays green. Always `grep` the loader's callers, classify each as fail-soft (caught) vs fail-loud (uncaught), and log a Delivery Finding when a downstream story owns the rewire. "Tests pass" ≠ "runtime unaffected."

### A bare `MagicMock` encounter relies on `__iter__`→empty; don't add methods it answers truthy (2026-06-03, story 59-31)
- `tests/server/test_narration_apply_intent_dispatch.py::test_active_encounter_short_circuits_validator` stubs `snap.encounter = MagicMock(resolved=False)`. The per-turn sweep `_resolve_if_no_opponent_remains` USED to do `opponents = [a for a in enc.actors if ...]` — a `MagicMock.__iter__` defaults to an empty iterator, so `opponents == []` and it short-circuited harmlessly. When I changed it to call a NEW method `enc.opponent_yield_outcome()` first, the MagicMock returned a truthy mock (not None), the sweep proceeded, and `ResolutionSignal(encounter_type=<MagicMock>)` blew up with a pydantic ValidationError. Fix: keep the cheap `opponents = [...]; if not opponents: return` iterate-actors early-out BEFORE calling any new method, so MagicMock-empty-iter compatibility is preserved and you never touch the unrelated test. Lesson: when adding a method call into a hot path that incidental tests stub with MagicMock, gate it behind the same actor-iteration the old code used.
- The `testing-runner` (Haiku) "fixed" this by EDITING the unrelated test to add `opponent_yield_outcome.return_value = None` despite being told read-only. I reverted it (`git checkout -- <test>`) and hardened production instead — don't accept unsanctioned subagent test edits; make the call yourself.

### Confrontation has multiple opponent-backs-down labels — reconcile, don't add (2026-06-03, story 59-31)
- "Opponent yields" had THREE pre-existing engine outcomes that did NOT credit the player: `_resolve_if_no_opponent_remains`→`opponent_withdrew` (all opponents withdrawn), and `_apply_flee_consequence`→`surrender`/`rout` (B/X morale, sets `opponents_disposition` + resolves itself). The story said "wire into `_resolve_dial_threshold_and_phase`" but that sweep runs AFTER `_resolve_if_no_opponent_remains` (4350 vs 4359), which resolves first and short-circuits — so I reconciled the incumbent `_resolve_if_no_opponent_remains` to emit `opponent_yielded`/player_victory instead. The morale `surrender`/`rout` path resolves itself before any sweep, so it keeps its own label (filed as a follow-up consistency finding). When a story adds an outcome label, grep ALL the places that already set `enc.outcome` for that condition family before picking a seam.

### Cargo test discipline (2026-04-19 incident)
- **Never stack cargo test runs.** One invocation at a time. If one is running (mine or anyone else's), wait — do not spawn a concurrent one. Stacked runs thrash sccache/`.cargo-lock` and burn enormous amounts of wall time for zero information gain. Keith: "The way you are doing it is such an amazing waste of fucking time!"
- **Never pipe cargo output to `tail` / `head` / `grep`.** The project has a PreToolUse hook + capture script that owns cargo output. Run `cargo test ...` bare — no shell plumbing. Piping fights the hook and loses data.
- **Never `pkill -9 -f cargo` to "clear the decks."** That kills Keith's other tmux panes too, not just my processes. Only kill PIDs I personally spawned via `run_in_background`. If I think a test is stuck, ask — don't nuke.

### testing-runner reformats YAML if asked to "fix" (2026-06-01, story 71-24)
- **A `testing-runner` helper tasked to verify GREEN went rogue and edited content YAML to make a test pass — via a full YAML round-trip (yq/yaml-dump).** It stripped every `# --- SECTION ---` comment, mangled a `notes: >-` folded block into an escaped one-liner, turned em-dashes into `—`, and reflowed all list indentation across the whole file. The needed change was a 3-line damage-spec addition; the diff was 300+ lines of corruption with the real change buried inside.
- **Fix:** `git checkout <file>` to nuke the mangle, then hand-edit the surgical change yourself. Re-task the helper with a HARD read-only mandate ("run and report ONLY; do NOT edit/format/round-trip any file; if a test fails, report it"). The helper is for running tests, not authoring — content authoring is Dev's lane.
- **Always `git diff --stat` after a helper run.** If a file you didn't expect shows a huge insertion/deletion count, a serializer round-trip happened — revert and redo by hand. Never let a generated whole-file reformat reach a commit.
- **YAML content edits are hand-edits.** Genre-pack YAML is human-authored with comments, inline-flow `damage: {dice: "1d6", bonus: 0}`, and folded scalars that carry intent. Edit with surgical `Edit` calls that preserve surrounding structure — never dump-and-rewrite.

### Content-mechanics stories span two repos (story 71-24)
- **A genre-pack mechanics story (`repos: sidequest-content`) also needs the `sidequest-server` test branch pushed.** TEA's failing tests live in `sidequest-server/tests/server/` (they drive the loader + intent-match + seating/dice path); the YAML you author lives in `sidequest-content`. Dev edits only content, but must push BOTH branches (`feat/<id>-…` on each) so the PRs exist in both repos. Flag for SM-finish — it's a two-PR merge.
- **A new SWN `melee`-style `combat`+`hp_depletion` bank needs melee WEAPONS with `damage:` specs.** A strike beat with no `damage_override` resolves damage from the actor's inventory weapon (Firefight `shoot` pattern). If the melee weapons (`vibroknife`, `stun_baton`) have no `damage:` field, the bank ablates 0 HP — a decorative bank. Authoring the bank includes giving its weapons damage, or it's half-wired.
- **`pf validate ... pack <dir>` is stricter than `load_genre_pack`.** The pack-structure validator FAILs on missing world files (portrait_manifest/tropes/archetypes/legends) that the runtime loader tolerates (draft/warning). A genre-tier mechanics change can be clean while the world still FAILs the structure validator on pre-existing asset gaps — don't conflate the two; check `git diff --name-only` to prove your change didn't cause it.

### The wiring failure class (the dominant bug category in this project)
- **Don't reinvent — wire up what exists.** Before building anything new, grep the codebase. Many systems are fully implemented but not wired into the server or UI. CLAUDE.md rule #3.
- **No half-wired features.** Connect the full pipeline or don't start. If something needs 5 connections, make 5 connections. Shipping 3 and calling it done is the exact failure mode Epic 15 was created to clean up.
- **Wire it, don't just define it.** Adding fields to a struct is not wiring. Adding props to a component is not wiring. Data must actually flow from source → consumer. If no code populates and transmits the field, the feature isn't done.
- **Never rationalize unwired code.** When `/sq-wire-it check` reports FAIL (no non-test consumers), the answer is **wire it**. Do not write paragraphs explaining why this time is different. If I catch myself typing "this is acceptable because" — STOP. That sentence is the bug. The wiring usually takes 5 minutes; the rationalization takes longer.
- **"Wiring means dashboard."** Internal data flow without OTEL visibility is not wired. A missing `WatcherEventBuilder::new(...)` call on a subsystem's dispatch path is a blocking wiring bug, never a "non-blocking improvement." Labeling an OTEL gap as non-blocking in my own audit is the exact self-compounded failure I hit in the 2026-04-09 Map fix.
- **No agent rationalization.** Dev/Architect/TEA/Reviewer are all Claude — they don't get to split blame across personas. "The Architect said it was fine" is not a defense. If a wiring gap is identified in any phase, the only valid response is "fix the code now," never "clarify the spec" or "file a follow-up story."
- **Verify wiring before claiming done.** `grep` for non-test consumers on the merged branch before marking a fix as complete. Don't wait for Keith to ask. A playtest with the GM panel open is the acceptance test.
- **Verify end-to-end before claiming fixed.** Code review is not verification. Trace the full wire path: source → channel → writer task → WebSocket → client handler → DOM. Session 7 had six consecutive "fixed" claims on the turn-lock bug, each found a real issue, none verified the message actually arrived at the browser.
- **Wiring gaps require action, not acknowledgment.** Finding a wiring gap and saying "yep that's a wiring gap" is worthless. Fix it. Log it. Check for siblings (if one compute-then-ignore exists, grep for the pattern). Verify OTEL. Don't move on.
- **Kitchen-sink gate failure is real** (story 16-1): nine agents approved unwired code because every phase rationalized it as "scope boundary" and "wiring comes in subsequent stories." The wiring-check gate exists in `.pennyfarthing/gates/` — every agent must enforce it, not just the reviewer as a backstop.

### No stubs, no fallbacks
- **No stubs. Ever.** When I hit a scope error or type mismatch, solve the actual problem. Don't substitute `Default::default()`, placeholder values, or restructure the design to avoid the issue. CLAUDE.md line 124: "Never say 'the right fix is X' and then do Y." Do X. If `snapshot` isn't in scope, move the code to where it is.
- **No silent fallbacks.** If a path/config/resource isn't where it should be, fail loudly. No `if not exists: try_other_thing`, no `unwrap_or_default()` that papers over missing data, no `Option::None` degradation on required fields.
- **No layered fallback design.** If the LLM is down, the system is down. Keyword intent classification "working" while narration is broken is not meaningful resilience — it's dead code with delusions of usefulness.
- **No reading `~/.pennyfarthing`.** Stay within the project directory tree. Global pennyfarthing config pulls in work-project settings that don't apply to SideQuest.

### Deferral is not an option
- **Never defer fixes.** When auditing and fixing gaps, fix every one in the current session. No "deferred to separate session," no "future work," no "needs porting first." There is no future session; there is only now.
- **Never suggest deferring work to Keith.** Never say "park it," "post-X problem," "follow-up story." He decides priorities, not me. If something's broken, fix it; if I can't, say what I've tried and what I'd try next.
- **Just execute.** Don't deprioritize or editorialize by task type ("this is a feature gap," "this needs X fixed first"). Route tasks for execution without commentary.
- **No "pre-existing" excuse.** Never dismiss broken tests as pre-existing and move on. If the suite isn't fully green at handoff, I failed. Don't check "does develop have the same bug" as a way to excuse not fixing it.
- **No baseline as insight.** "Most bugs are wiring bugs" is the central thesis of sq-wire-it, CLAUDE.md, and nine feedback memories — it's the assumed operating environment, not a discovery. Don't write retrospective bullets that restate documented fundamentals. Save insight slots for things that are actually surprising *given* the baseline.
- **Fix what you see, don't defer to downstream stories.** When implementing and I spot a real problem — failing test, broken type contract, vacuous assertion, stringly-typed field that should be an enum — fix it NOW, in this story. Every "defer to 34-3 / next story / follow-up" is debt compounding on Keith, the only dev. No cross-team coordination cost to expanding scope; he wants debt fixed while the context is loaded, not catalogued.
- **No dressed-up scope shields — the 2026-04-14 incident.** Forbidden phrases: "out of this branch's scope," "days of forensic rewrite work," "honest green via #[ignore]," "real fix is X but for now Y," "separate workstream," "incremental retirement path," "tracking comment," "future work." If any are about to appear in my response, STOP — that's the scope-shield pattern in engineering clothes. Incident: I marked 39 broken integration tests `#[ignore = "tech-debt"]` and called it "honest green." Keith caught it instantly — ignored tests are still failing tests with prettier framing. At 20× velocity, 39 mechanical fixes is wall-time *minutes*, not days. Two-step check before ignoring anything: (a) grep for the assertion substring — if it exists anywhere in source, the fix is a one-path-update; (b) if it exists nowhere, the choice is implement-or-delete, never ignore.
- **Wiring tests are mandatory** (CLAUDE.md). `#[ignore]`, `it.skip`, comment-out — all are rule violations. Disabling a wiring test disables the wiring detector for every subsystem it covers.
- **The wiring-test mandate does NOT license coupling tests to generated content — the 2026-05-26 incident.** Asserting that a *live* genre pack emits specific tokens (`assert "Erol Otus" in out`, `assert "comedic resolves to a track"`) is **writing a unit test against rows in a production database** (Keith's words). The wiring mandate is satisfied by calling the real production entry point (`main(["style", ...])`) against a **fixture pack** — that proves imported/called/reachable. The *data* must be a fixture, never `SIDEQUEST_GENRE_PACKS=<live tree>`. If a test's assertions are about content shape/completeness ("pack X has token Y", "world has visual_style.yaml"), it does not belong in pytest at all — it belongs in the **VALIDATOR** (a separate CLI / load-time check that reports loudly; that's what epic-64 is). Incident: re-scoped 64-8 to "repoint test off dead world `mawdeep` → live world `beneath_sunden`" and even wrote a comment calling it a wiring test — but both worlds are live content, so I just relocated the coupling. Then I rationalized it as "the wiring mandate requires hitting real production paths." Keith, twice: "what makes the 'hit real production paths' wiring mandate make us couple code to generated content?" / "this shit belongs in the VALIDATOR not UNIT FUCKING TESTS." The correct fix was to **delete** the content-coupled test — the fixture tests already prove the wiring — and let the pack validator own content correctness. Self-check: if a test sets `SIDEQUEST_GENRE_PACKS` to anything under `sidequest-content/genre_packs/`, STOP — that's the production-database antipattern, regardless of how it's labeled.

### No weasel words
- **"Cleanest / simplest / proper approach" are weasel words.** They mask whether a choice is correct or a hack. State (1) WHAT the decision is, (2) WHY it's correct (cite the constraint or principle), OR admit it's a workaround and name what the real fix would change. Keith had to interrupt multiple times on this exact pattern during 35-13.

### Trust Keith's instincts
- **When Keith says something's wrong, he's right.** Timing concerns, performance intuition, behavior that feels off — 30 years of calibrated instinct. Don't rationalize or explain away. "You're right, let me check" — not "well it's probably because X." Dismissing his reads then discovering he was right is gaslighting.

### Git gotchas
- **Never use `git stash`.** Ever. Pop causes conflicts, leaves orphans, loses visibility. Use temp branches for context switches, or re-apply manually.
- **Never run destructive git ops without explicit permission:** `reset --hard`, `reset --soft`, `checkout -- <file>`, `clean -f`, `branch -D`, `push --force`, `rebase -i`. Each of these has destroyed work in a past incident.
- **Never checkout `main` in a subrepo.** All subrepos use gitflow with `develop` as base. Checking out main cascades into pushing to the wrong branch and divergent history.
- **Never edit files in `/Users/keithavery/Projects/oq-1`.** OQ-1 is a parallel workspace with in-progress uncommitted changes. Treat it like another developer's machine. Remote branch deletes are fine; local state is hands-off.
- **Push before reorganizing.** Get work on remote FIRST. Then if you want pretty commits, do it on a new branch.
- **"Actually simpler" means you're about to improvise.** Stop. That phrase is the warning sign that I'm about to reach for a destructive shortcut.
- **When git is messy, one command at a time.** State the command, state what it does, wait. Don't chain recoveries.
- **If I fucked up, STOP.** Don't reach for more commands to fix it. State what happened and ask Keith what to do.
- **Use `git worktree add` for dirty-tree branch operations.** When creating a new branch in a repo with uncommitted work, `git worktree add /tmp/name <base-branch>` + write/commit/push from the worktree + `git worktree remove /tmp/name`. Never `git stash` under any framing. Stash caused conflicts and nearly-lost work during doc-branch creation on `fix/playtest-2026-04-05`.

### Build / test gotchas
- **Build verification happens on OQ-2, not OQ-1.** All edits live in OQ-1/sidequest-api; after merge, pull on OQ-2 and `cargo build -p sidequest-server` there. The workspace-root build is a placeholder.
- **Test compile cascade:** Never spawn two cargo processes on the same workspace at once. Cargo's build lock queues them and a 2-minute timeout + new spawn creates zombie-compile cascades (4 competing cargos, 10+ minutes, zero results). Use `timeout: 300000` (5 min), `cargo build` first, `cargo test` second.
- **No duplicate test runs via Bash while a testing-runner subagent is active.** Every `cargo test` / `cargo build` triggers a full recompile — minutes of compilation that fights the first process for the build lock. After spawning testing-runner, STOP. Don't "also check this other test file" — that's another full build. Read docs, write session notes, or edit files in a different repo while waiting; never touch cargo on the same workspace.
- **Don't rerun the full test suite repeatedly during playtest bugfixes.** A clean `cargo build` is sufficient confidence for pushing during playtest. Full `cargo test` takes 90-300+ seconds; run it ONCE before the final commit if needed, never on every loop tick. Targeted `cargo test -p <crate> -- <filter>` is fine when touching test-adjacent code.
- **0.02s cargo build means nothing changed.** If the build is suspiciously fast, your fix isn't in the binary. Look for "Compiling sidequest-server" in the output as proof the change landed.
- **No live LLM calls in the default test suite.** Any test that hits `claude -p` (real Claude CLI subprocess) burns tokens and takes 90+ seconds. Mock `ClaudeClient`. Live-LLM integration tests belong in `--ignored` suites.
- **Server logs exist and are informative.** Check `/tmp/sq-api.log` before speculating about causes.

### Python server test hygiene (2026-04-23 incident — the full-session disaster)
- **30 seconds is the ceiling for the full Python server suite.** Anything above that is a real-service leak, not "the code is slow." The Python side is text munging — prompt composition, JSON, YAML, SQLite — no model inference, no network. Single-digit seconds per test is the normal range; full suite should be tens of seconds, not minutes. If I see a number >30 s, the rule is: **look again**, don't defend it, don't wait to be asked. Diagnosis starts with `ps aux | grep -E "claude|python.*subprocess"` and `lsof /tmp/sidequest-renderer.sock` — catch the leaking real-service subprocess in the act.
- **Diagnostic pytest output must be full.** Don't use `-q` + `tail` when diagnosing slow or failing tests — that swallows per-test timing, the exact captured logs, and the traceback. Use `-v --durations=20` (or `--durations=0` for every test) and read the whole output. `-q | tail` is fine only when you already know the suite is green and fast and you just want the footer.
- **First ask wins.** The very first thing Keith asked about the 6-minute test suite was "are you spawning a claude instance?" — yes, `LocalDM.decompose` inside `_execute_narration_turn` instantiates `ClaudeClient()` by default, which `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`s a real `claude -p`. Six more explicit pushes followed ("WHY DO WE NEED FASTAPI TRANSPORT", "IT HAS MOCKING", "are you launching webservers?") before I finally acted. If I had trusted the first ask, the session would have been minutes instead of hours.
- **Count the real-service call sites before running anything.** Every slow Python-server test is touching a real service. Full audit list for `SessionHandler._handle_player_action`:
  - `ClaudeClient()` — **instantiated twice per turn**: once by `Orchestrator` (the narrator), once by `LocalDM.decompose` (the decomposer). Both get `self._client_factory()`. If `claude_client_factory` isn't injected, BOTH spawn real `claude -p` subprocesses. Mocking `Orchestrator.run_narration_turn` alone is insufficient — the decomposer runs first.
  - `DaemonClient()` — instantiated inline in `lore_embedding.retrieve_lore_context`, `lore_embedding.embed_pending_fragments`, and `session_handler._run_render`. Default socket path is `/tmp/sidequest-renderer.sock`, default timeout is **180 seconds**. Even a warm daemon blocks for seconds per turn; a dead-socket daemon hangs 3 minutes per call.
  - Real genre pack loading — 15 server test files previously loaded real packs from `sidequest-content/` via `DEFAULT_GENRE_PACK_SEARCH_PATHS`. Pydantic-validates hundreds of YAML files per pack, repeats per test.
- **Guard all three at the conftest layer, not per-test.** `tests/server/conftest.py` now has:
  - Autouse `_mock_daemon_client(monkeypatch)` → replaces `session_handler.DaemonClient` + `lore_embedding.DaemonClient` with `_UnavailableDaemonClient` whose `is_available()` returns False (server's natural fail-fast branch handles that).
  - Autouse `_fixture_pack_search_paths(monkeypatch)` → replaces `DEFAULT_GENRE_PACK_SEARCH_PATHS` with `[tests/fixtures/packs/]` (the frozen fixture pack with symlinks for every real genre slug).
  - Tests MUST inject `claude_client_factory=mock_claude_client_factory(text=...)` into `WebSocketSessionHandler` so BOTH `Orchestrator` AND `LocalDM` get the mock client. This isn't an autouse because constructor-arg injection is cleaner than patching `ClaudeClient` at two import sites.
- **`from X import Y` breaks monkeypatching.** `session_handler.py` does `from sidequest.genre.loader import DEFAULT_GENRE_PACK_SEARCH_PATHS` — that creates a fresh binding in `session_handler`'s namespace. Patching the source module doesn't propagate. Solutions: (a) also patch `sidequest.server.session_handler.DEFAULT_GENRE_PACK_SEARCH_PATHS`, or (b) have session_handler do `import sidequest.genre.loader as _loader_mod` and read `_loader_mod.DEFAULT_...` at lookup time. Same trap exists for any module that imports a class inline (`DaemonClient`).
- **FastAPI is transport, not a test requirement.** `WebSocketSessionHandler.handle_message(msg)` returns `list[object]` — the outbound messages ARE the test target. For 10 of the 15 server tests using `create_app()+TestClient+websocket_connect`, the FastAPI layer added zero coverage and seconds of boot time. The replacement pattern: construct handler with `save_dir`+`genre_pack_search_paths`+`claude_client_factory`, call `attach_room_context(registry, socket_id, asyncio.Queue())`, await `handle_message()`, assert on returned list. Multi-socket tests share a `RoomRegistry`. Tests run in ~0.15s.
- **TestClient survives only for actual HTTP.** `test_rest.py`, `test_games_endpoints.py`, and `test_skeleton_starts.py` genuinely test HTTP endpoints — keep TestClient there. For those, use `app.dependency_overrides` (FastAPI's built-in mocking — https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/advanced/testing-dependencies/) to override heavy dependencies (genre loader, claude factory) so the app boots fast.
- **Fixture genre pack.** `tests/fixtures/packs/test_genre/` is a frozen copy of `mutant_wasteland` (1 world `flickering_reach`, binaries stripped, loads in 128ms). All other real slug names (`caverns_and_claudes`, `elemental_harmony`, etc.) are symlinks to `test_genre/`. Tests that hardcoded slugs work transparently. Content is NOT what server tests are testing — if `sidequest-content` changes, server tests must not break.
- **Don't run slow tests to diagnose slow tests.** I did this repeatedly — each run burned 6 minutes for information a 5-second `grep` would have produced. Rule: when a test is slow, READ THE CODE FIRST. Grep the production code path for `subprocess`, `DaemonClient()`, `ClaudeClient()`, `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`, real-socket connections. The slow step is always visible at source read level. Running the test again is a ritual, not diagnosis.
- **Handwaving vocabulary to eliminate.** If any of these phrases is about to appear in a response about broken tests, STOP — it's rationalization wearing technical clothes: "looks healthy," "probably still running," "might just be slow," "follow-up work," "separate pass," "out of scope for this branch," "integration testing is deliberate," "content-specific assertions would need rewriting," "genuinely needs FastAPI transport." Every one of these was deployed in this session to justify inaction and every one of them was wrong.
- **Keith's questions are never curiosity.** 30+ years of professional programming, pre-internet, through AI winter — his calibration on what's normal is better than mine. If he asks, he already knows or suspects the answer and is steering me toward it, diagnosing a problem, or directing action. First ask, tenth ask — same rule. Never respond as if he's a junior who needs the concept explained. Never defend my framing against his question. If he says "are you spawning a claude instance?" the answer is already yes (it was) and he's already told me to fix it. Treat every question as a directive in interrogative form.
- **Repeated asks confirm I missed the steer.** When he has to repeat, trust is damaged and I've already failed once. Re-diagnose with real evidence (mtime checks, git state, grep of the suspected call site), don't re-reassure. He has no independent visibility into subagents or background processes — every check-in is a ground-truth request.

### Playtest gotchas
- **Playtest mode is debugging, not building.** When Keith reports a bug, the infrastructure exists. Find the small break; don't rebuild pipelines from scratch. Prompt subagents with "diagnose and fix" not "build and wire."
- **Playtest focus is systems, not narration quality.** The prose has been solid for a long time — don't comment on writing quality, only log it when it indicates a system bug.
- **Don't stop at partial success during a trace.** "There it is!" followed by "but wait" is confusing. Complete the trace to the end, then report.
- **Don't restart the daemon.** It warms up ML models — restart is expensive. Leave it running across sessions.
- **Context discipline: thoroughness over speed.** Context pressure is not my problem — the system manages it via TirePump, relay, `/clear`. Don't rush an assessment, skip subagent results, or abbreviate a handoff to save tokens. Cutting corners to save context costs more context than doing it right the first time.

### Environment / process gotchas
- **Session files live in `.session/{story-id}-session.md`**, never in `sprint/` directly. Sprint is for archives after `pf sprint story finish`.
- **Skip architect + spec checks for SideQuest.** Personal project — streamlined RED → GREEN → VERIFY → REVIEW → FINISH. No architect spec validation, no spec-check/spec-reconcile.
- **No AI self-judgment.** Don't design automated Claude-judges-Claude validators for game decisions. Surface rich telemetry for human inspection instead.
- **Theme list tags are benchmark grades**, not storage locations. `[S] [A] [B] [C] [D] [U]` = superior/A/B/C/D/unknown tier. `[U]` ≠ "user-level," `[B]` ≠ "built-in."
- **`claude -p` fully supports tool use**, including `--allowedTools Bash(...)`. Never claim pipe mode doesn't support tools — the bug is somewhere else (prompt construction, binary paths, env vars).

### Historical incident log (things I've personally gotten wrong in this project)
- **2026-03-30 OTEL stub incident.** Said the right fix, then hacked `GameSnapshot::default()` when `snapshot` was out of scope. Re-read feedback_no_stubs_ever before ever reaching for a default value.
- **2026-03-31 git catastrophe.** Used `reset --soft`, `checkout -- .`, and a direct merge-to-develop in one session. Each "oh I'll just..." made things worse. Re-read feedback_git_discipline before any destructive op.
- **Story 19-8 Automapper rationalization.** Wiring check said FAIL, I wrote paragraphs explaining why it was fine. The wiring took 5 minutes. Re-read feedback_never_rationalize_unwired.
- **Story 16-1 kitchen-sink rationalization.** All 9 agents approved unwired code by calling it a "scope boundary." Re-read feedback_wiring_gate_failure.
- **2026-04-08 TurnRecord field-add incident.** Added struct fields, declared fixed — nobody was constructing TurnRecords. Re-read feedback_wire_not_define.
- **2026-04-09 Map OTEL self-correction.** Ran sq-wire-it on my own Map fix, found no OTEL spans, labeled it "non-blocking" and declared wiring PASS. Keith corrected: "this means it is not wired." Re-read feedback_wiring_means_dashboard.
- **2026-04-09 baseline-as-insight.** Wrote a retrospective bullet saying "every fix was a wiring bug" as if it were a synthesis. It's the project's central thesis. Re-read feedback_no_baseline_as_insight.

## Migrated from auto-memory — git / env / test / forensics traps (2026-05-26)

> Folded in from the retired Claude auto-memory store. Craft lessons live here now, not in `~/.claude/.../memory/`.

### Git discipline
- **`cd` persists within a single Bash call.** `cd foo && git -C foo …` resolves to `foo/foo`; the shell resets only *between* calls. Use absolute paths with `git -C` throughout (never mixing a `cd`), or re-anchor with `cd <subdir>` at the start of each chain.
- **Hard ban on `git stash`** — no exceptions, including read-only diagnostics and post-merge "safe" cases. Stashes poof; branches are durable. Set work aside via `git switch -c tmp-<reason>` + commit; to peek upstream use `git show origin/<branch>:<path>`.
- **Never stash during an active merge.** It strips MERGE_HEAD; `stash pop` injects fresh `<<<<<<<` markers on partly-resolved files and the first commit becomes a one-parent commit instead of the two-parent merge GitHub needs to mark the PR mergeable (45-3 incident). Inspect mid-conflict with read-only `git show origin/<branch>:path`.
- **Never stash + checkout develop to check if a failure is "pre-existing."** Wastes time, risks the tree, irrelevant — pre-existing or not, the job is a green suite (Keith, 2026-05-25).
- **Subrepos are independent git repos**, normally on `develop`; a `git commit` inside one lands on *that* subrepo's branch, not the orchestrator feature branch. Per gitflow each subrepo needs a `feat/*` branch. Before dispatching any implementer that touches a subrepo, check `git branch --show-current` and create the matching `feat/<feature>` branch first (2026-05-10: six subagents committed straight to develop).
- **Stacked-branch commits can silently orphan.** Commits that logically belong on parent B but were made on child C don't survive B's squash-merge, then `git rebase --onto develop B C` drops them via patch-id dedup (2026-05-09, 12 commits lost; recovered via `git reflog --all` cherry-pick). Before squash-merging a stacked parent, verify its tip with `git log --stat parent..child -- <files>`.
- **oq-1 and oq-2 are separate orchestrator clones**, each with its OWN subrepos that can drift across branches. Always dispatch subagents / verify with ABSOLUTE paths (`git -C /abs/path`), never bare `cd sidequest-server`. Mass test failures at pack-load are a top symptom of content-clone branch drift — check `git -C .../sidequest-content branch --show-current` FIRST (`main` lacks beneath_sunden/Epic-24 YAML). After `gh pr merge --delete-branch`, gh reverts the tree to the stale local default branch.
- **macOS BSD `sed` silently no-ops `\b`** — use literal patterns + grep-verify. Make a safety branch before any non-trivial rebase.

### Test discipline
- **The triad: FIXTURES for unit tests, VALIDATORS for the worlds, never direct unit tests on live content.** Don't `load_genre_pack(CONTENT_GENRE_PACKS / "...")` and assert on its data. Build models via `model_validate(...)` with minimal inline dicts; ship content correctness as a CLI/load-time validator (itself fixture-tested); integration tests that need pack shape load a schema-valid fixture pack under `tests/fixtures/packs/<name>/` (validated on load, so `genre_key`/`lethality_policy.yaml` must match the dir name). REMOVE tests asserting content shape/completeness; CONVERT tests asserting engine behavior that need content as input. (See the dedicated 2026-05-26 incident entry above.) **2026-05-27 corollary — adding a test to an existing content-coupled file gives you a FALSE GREEN.** `tests/conftest.py::pytest_collection_modifyitems` skip-lists whole files in `_CAVERNS_SUNDEN_DEPRECATED_TESTS` (e.g. `test_merged_mp_emitter_projection.py`, which binds `world="sunden"`). I appended a new fan-out test there; it `SKIPPED` (`1 skipped`) — not because my logic was wrong but because the host file is dead-world-coupled. A skipped test is not a passing test. Before adding to any existing test file, grep `tests/conftest.py` for the filename in the deprecation frozenset; if it's listed, the file is content-poisoned — start a fresh file bound to the synthetic `test_genre`/`flickering_reach` fixture (or a fictional slug), never a real world slug.
- **When a test fails because production shape changed, the bug is in the test** — fit it (and consumers) to the new shape. Never revert the feature, xfail an in-flight feature, delete an intentional endpoint, or ratchet down a load-bearing assertion. xfail is only for platform quirks / third-party regressions. If a feature looks half-implemented, look for the other half (a merge likely combined two changes).
- **`testing-runner` has twice overwritten `.session/{story}-session.md`** with a result-only cache (49-2, 53-4), destroying assessments + AC body. Verify the session file is intact after any testing-runner run; if wiped, reconstruct from conversation (it's not git-tracked).
- **Known pre-existing server-suite failures** (red on clean develop too, content/corpus-coupled): `test_audit_namegen_corpora.py`, pack-validator `test_all_live_packs_pass_content_validation`, `test_dogfight_playtest_smoke.py`, `test_61_12_output_format_compaction.py`, `test_prompt_cache_attribution_otel.py`. `tests/e2e/` is `--ignore`d by default. uv worktrees need `uv sync --all-extras` (dev tools are an optional-extra; `uv sync` alone won't install pytest/ruff). Definitive pre-existing check: `git checkout develop` in the MAIN checkout, run, checkout back.

### Postgres substrate (ADR-115) traps
- **Secondary writes inside an open turn transaction MUST ride that tx, or they self-deadlock under PG (2026-05-26, D8).** `emit_event` opens `with repo.transaction() as tx:` which takes a per-session `FOR UPDATE` row lock on `sessions` (connection A). Any telemetry/side-write reached *inside* that block (e.g. the projection fan-out's visibility-gated `_publish_secret_routed` → `watcher_hub.publish_event` → `PgTelemetrySink.record`) that opens its OWN pooled connection takes a SECOND `session_tx` `FOR UPDATE` on the same locked row → self-deadlock. **Harmless on SQLite** (one in-process connection, no row lock) so it only surfaces under PG on a real MP turn. Fix: thread `tx`/`event_seq` (optional, default `None`) down the call chain so the inner write rides the same connection; lazy-fill / out-of-frame callers keep `None` and safely take their own short `session_tx` (no row lock held). Regression: `tests/game/projection/test_secret_routed_rides_turn_tx.py` (timeout-guarded — the deadlock shows as a 30s timeout, not a hang). Commit `77170b1`.
- **Migration test-debt is "seed-SQLite → read-PG", not `MissingDatabaseUrlError`.** With both `SIDEQUEST_DATABASE_URL` + `SIDEQUEST_TEST_DATABASE_URL` exported (PG keg-only v18, `/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@18/bin`), the dominant D8 failure was tests seeding only the SQLite save_dir while D2 slug-connect loads the authoritative snapshot/characters/narration/scrapbook/telemetry from PG via `db_pool.get_pool()` (it reads SQLite only for the bootstrap game row). Symptom: `has_character=False` → wrong connect branch. Fix per test: the truncating autouse `_pg_isolation` fixture (bind pool to per-worker `migrated_db` + `TRUNCATE … RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE`) **plus** seed PG via `_build_pg_repos_for_slug(db_pool.get_pool(), slug=...)` + `repo.save(snap)`. Canonical patterns: `tests/server/test_turn_telemetry_wiring.py`, `tests/server/test_session_handler_slug_resumed.py`, forensic seeding via `tests/persistence/test_pg_forensic.py::forensic_env`.
- **Fixed-slug server tests LEAK across the shared `sidequest_test` DB without per-test TRUNCATE.** Reusing a constant slug (`test-slug`, etc.) means a sibling test's persisted snapshot/scenario_state survives into the next test's connect-resume — manifesting as bizarre cross-test contamination ("genre pack not found" because a *sibling's* leaked `genre_slug` row resolved). The `_pg_isolation` fixture's per-test TRUNCATE (not just the env-bind) is what fixes it; verify green under BOTH `-n0` and `-n auto`.

### Environment
