- **`ModuleNotFoundError` for a package that IS installed** (uv sync succeeds, `uv run python -c "import X"` works) is often a stale console-script shebang — venvs aren't relocatable, and each clone (`/Users/slabgorb/Projects/sidequest/`, oq-1, oq-2) has its own. Diagnose with `head -1 .venv/bin/<entrypoint>`; fix with `rm -rf .venv && uv sync` (not a package reinstall).

### Saves / forensics (read-only or you corrupt the campaign)
- **Naive `cp save.db` silently loses recent turns.** Saves live at `~/.sidequest/saves/games/<slug>/save.db` with `-wal`/`-shm`; the multi-MB WAL is newer/uncommitted. Copy the trio to a temp dir, then on the COPY run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE); PRAGMA quick_check;` + `.backup`, leaving the source untouched. (`?mode=ro` `.backup` fails exit 14 while uvicorn holds the DB.)
- **`SqliteStore.open(path)` WRITES on construction** — `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` flips the header bit + materializes `-wal`/`-shm`, and `__init__` runs schema executescript + migrations + commit. NEVER use it for forensic/inspection access; use `sqlite3.connect(f"file:{path}?mode=ro", uri=True)`. (For a non-vacuous "doesn't mutate" red test, seed a MINIMAL `journal_mode=DELETE` DB via plain sqlite3 — a complete fixture makes the buggy executescript a no-op → false green.)
- **Offline save edits are silently clobbered if the session resumes** — the running server holds the pre-edit snapshot in memory and re-persists on next autosave. Before any save repair: `lsof save.db save.db-wal` clear AND confirm the session isn't loaded by :8765; tell Keith the fix only sticks if the save is NOT resumed; never trust a brief's "already repaired" precondition.
- **`events` table = NARRATION + SCRAPBOOK_ENTRY only — there is no per-event `state_delta`.** Authoritative mechanical state = the single `game_state.snapshot_json` row; narrator coherence = the footnotes/KnownFacts stream (ADR-100). Forensic/replay can only fold footnotes (`fold_known_facts`); read mechanical state from `snapshot_json` via `?mode=ro`.
- **"Is magic firing?" forensic:** `sqlite3 save.db "SELECT json_extract(snapshot_json,'$.magic_state.working_log') FROM game_state;"` — empty array = narrator never invoked a plugin (historic failures were prompt-level, not pipeline). Field is `LedgerBar.value` (not `current`).
- **Latent hazard:** `_maybe_persist_encounter_row` does an unconditional `_conn.commit()` that could prematurely commit an open turn txn (currently gated to encounter ops, so safe — don't widen it without thought).

### Playtest false alarms
- **"UI element X shows new value but Y still shows old" within ~2 min of a server emit** → suspect post-emit operator confusion (Keith reading a prior turn) BEFORE a state-pipeline hypothesis. Cheap forensic: `cp` the save, then `sqlite3 -line ... "SELECT id, round_number, author, substr(content,1,400), created_at FROM narrative_log ORDER BY id;"`, compare event `created_at` to the filing time. (2026-05-23 pulp_noir: narrator was correct; Keith's own round-5 line advanced the scene.)
- **Tandem-playtest server reload-thrash** (oq-2 editing live `sidequest/` while oq-1 drives) is the intended dev cycle — never log it as a concern, never ask oq-2 to throttle, never treat "reloaded mid-turn" as a confound. A turn reaching `narration_complete` (degraded=no) is valid regardless.

### Observability
- **Never file "subsystem unwired/dead" from log/grep absence.** OTEL-span-only subsystems on the ADR-103 server-tracer→Jaeger path never hit `/tmp/*.log` or the watcher firehose. Read the source for the EXACT span name, re-grep, trace a non-test caller, weigh player ground truth first. (Botched twice 2026-05-17: trope `orchestrator.trope_beat_injection`, perception `narrator.perception_rewrite`.)
- **Two unrelated OTEL streams post-SDK-migration — don't conflate:** (1) ADR-058 Claude-subprocess passthrough, HTTP/JSON, ingested only by `scripts/playtest_otlp.py`/`playtest_dashboard.py`; (2) the server's own tracer emitting `narration.turn` (with the `tool_calls_json` lie-detector ledger) + all SDK spans, gRPC-only via `SIDEQUEST_OTLP_ENDPOINT` (`just up-traced` → `:4317` → Jaeger `:16686`). For `narration.turn`/SDK spans go through Jaeger, never add a server-side HTTP/JSON OTLP exporter.

### Misc traps
- **A reviewer reporting a leaked `<system-reminder>` in a commit is OFTEN A FALSE POSITIVE** — the harness appends its reminder to `git log`/`git show` *tool output*. The ONLY authoritative check is `git log -1 --format=%B | od -c`. Build commit messages via `printf` heredoc → temp file → `git commit -F`, not the Write tool.
- **When the user says they'll (re)start a shared service themselves** (media daemon on `/tmp/sidequest-renderer.sock`), issue ONE graceful stop, verify it drained, then hands off — never retry/`pkill -f` (a name-kill matches the process they just launched; happened 2026-05-25).
- **Renaming a genre pack orphans its R2 binaries** — manifests move, R2 assets stay under the OLD key, runtime silently 404s. Recovery: copy masters from an old checkout, then `scripts/r2_sync_packs.py` (needs boto3 + R2_* creds). Audit one CDN path per recently-renamed/promoted pack.
- **Z-Image renders a literal two-part montage** if a portrait prompt describes a tight face AND a separate distant scene (and drops stray faces into POIs). Fix is FOCAL LENGTH: long telephoto lens ("135mm, compressed perspective, shallow DOF"), place subject in their canonical POI, single-shot guard ("no split screen, no diptych, no montage, no inset"). See `sidequest-content/PROMPTING_Z_IMAGE.md`.
- **2026-05-27 Story 65-1: data-walking tools must mirror the producer, not the prose.** I wrote r2_audit to match the story's stated key conventions and a hand-built fixture; it passed 23 tests but crashed on the real tree twice — (1) `chapters` is a LIST not a dict, (2) POIs without `slug` are rendered by the generator via `slugify(name)`, so the audit must derive the same key, not raise. Lesson: when building anything that consumes existing repo data, the source of truth is the PRODUCER's code (render_batch/collect_pois), and you MUST run it against the real tree before claiming green. Reuse the producer's helpers (render_common.slugify) instead of re-deriving conventions.

### Chargen archetype gate (playtest #C6, 2026-05-28)
- **An axis-pack that declares archetypes but never feeds the hints HARD-BLOCKS chargen.** The gate (`chargen_mixin._gate_archetype_resolution`) sets `pack_has_axes = base_archetypes is not None AND archetype_constraints is not None`. `base_archetypes` loads from the SHARED content-root `archetypes_base.yaml`; `archetype_constraints` from the pack's own file. If a pack has both, the gate REQUIRES a resolved archetype — but the builder only forms `resolved_archetype = f"{jungian}/{rpg_role}"` when a `char_creation.yaml` scene set BOTH `jungian_hint` AND `rpg_role_hint` (builder.py ~2256). A pack whose chargen choices set only `class_hint`/`race_hint` blocks every character with `missing_axes_with_pack_axes`. **When adding/promoting an axis-pack, wire both hints onto the class-selection (crucible) choices** — map each class to a non-forbidden pair from the pack's own `valid_pairings` (genre fallback always resolves; world funnels optional). neon_dystopia + pulp_noir both shipped broken this way (promoted 2026-05-23); fixed by wiring crucible hints. Guard: `tests/genre/test_axis_pack_chargen_resolves.py` parametrizes over ALL axis-packs. Packs with NO `archetype_constraints` (space_opera/coyote_star) hit the OK_NO_AXES pass and chargen fine — that's why a broken axis-pack looks "pack-specific."

### NPC stores are two-tier — `npcs` (roster) vs `npc_pool` (walk-ons) (playtest #C1, 2026-05-28)
- **`snapshot.npcs` = authored ROSTER (full `Npc`: disposition/OCEAN/belief); `snapshot.npc_pool` = lightweight `NpcPoolMember` WALK-ONS the narrator invents.** Roster NPCs present in a scene are tracked via `Npc.last_seen_location` and are **deliberately NOT mirrored into `npc_pool`** (`narration_apply._apply_npc_mentions`: an `npcs_hit` updates last_seen and does NOT append to the pool). So `snapshot.npc_pool == []` with a full roster is CORRECT, not a dropped patch. The OTEL `Patches: npc_pool(...)` line is a telemetry summary of `result.npcs_present`, NOT proof the pool was mutated — don't read it as state.
- **Therefore any subsystem that resolves an NPC must check BOTH stores.** `run_npc_agency` (and the `dispatch_engagement_watcher` npc_agency check) originally looked up only `npc_pool` → returned `npc_not_registered` for every roster NPC → the subsystem NEVER engaged for the game's primary NPCs (the crew, Old Tam). Fix: resolve the roster FIRST (authoritative + carries disposition), pool as fallback; thread `npcs` into the bank context in `intent_router_pass.py` (the bank signature-filters it). The dashboard ② "NPC Registry" widget also reads only `npc_pool` — a known cosmetic false-negative (should merge `npcs`), tracked separately.
- **The location-fallback Other-seater (`_npc_fallback_at_location`) seats EVERY present NPC as `side="opponent"` for non-sealed adversarial encounters — INTENTIONALLY** (chase dial, story 59-13). So you CANNOT blanket-apply the `_npc_is_adversary` filter to fix friendly-crew-as-opponents (#C3) without regressing chases. The real fix for chassis-vs-chassis ship_combat is materializing the named threat as the Other (gaslight doctrine), not filtering the fallback. `win_condition: hp_depletion` synthesizes inert `0/0/1_000_000` placeholder metrics by design (resolution runs on ablative HP, not the dial) — the 1e6 sentinel is not a bug. Ship-scale ablative HP (ADR-114 Part 2) is not built.

### R2 asset manifest oracle must be built from the bucket, never local disk (65-7, 2026-06-01)
- **The existence oracle `sidequest-content/r2_manifest.json` must be populated from a live `list_objects_v2` scan of R2, NOT from local files.** Local disk is incomplete by design: the dual-clone workflow splits rendering (OQ-1 portraits, OQ-2 POIs), PNGs are gitignored, and `git pull local` only syncs YAML — so one clone has only a fraction of R2 (65-7: 390 local media vs **1743** on R2). The only committed regen path today, `scripts/r2_sync_packs.py --manifest`, builds entries from `_md5_of(local_path)` → it would silently shrink the oracle 1743→~390. **When refreshing the manifest, scan the bucket**: reuse `scripts/r2_sync_packs._build_client` (env: `R2_S3_ENDPOINT`/`R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, bucket `sidequest`) + `scripts/r2_manifest.write_manifest` (atomic, key-sorted). Map entry from the listing: `md5`=ETag.strip('"'), `size_bytes`=Size, `uploaded_at`=LastModified.isoformat(timespec='seconds')+Z, `source`="r2_bucket_scan".
- **R2 ETags under genre_packs/ are clean 32-hex md5 — 0 multipart** (single-shot `put_object`), so the ETag IS the md5. Assert no dash in ETag and abort loudly if one appears (a multipart object would need a HEAD to get a real md5). All scripts run via `uv run` from the orchestrator root — boto3 is an orchestrator dev dep (65-1), not in bare python3.
- **`r2_audit.py` consumes only the `key` field** from manifest entries (`audit()` builds `manifest_keys`); `source`/`md5`/`size`/`uploaded_at` are informational. Expect a big expected-vs-uploaded divergence (65-7: 1026 YAML-expected vs 1743 R2-uploaded, 1287 "gaps") — those are real authoring gaps (un-rendered `*_alt2` music variants, missing portraits) + orphans, NOT manifest defects. The oracle's job is to hold R2 truth; the audit's job is to surface the diff loudly.

### r2_manifest.json drifts from live R2 across clones (2026-06-02, wry_whimsy asset handoff)
- **The committed `r2_manifest.json` can be silently behind live R2.** Assets rendered+uploaded from another clone (oq-1/oq-2 share one R2 bucket + origin) land on R2 but are NOT in this clone's manifest. `r2_manifest.py` builds entries from *local files* and `r2_sync_packs --manifest` only records files a sync touched — neither captures R2-only objects (a world rendered elsewhere, gitignored locally). Found `wry_whimsy` had 102 objects on R2 (Oz/Wonderland/Gulliver + 3 audio) and **zero** in the manifest.
- **Authoritative live-R2 check:** `render_common.existing_r2_keys()` does a paginated LIST (bucket `sidequest`). The render scripts' "already exists: R2" skip uses this — it's the source of truth, NOT the manifest. `r2_audit.py`'s notion of "on R2" is the *manifest*, so it over-reports gaps when the manifest is stale. Don't trust r2_audit until the manifest is refreshed.
- **Refresh tool:** `scripts/r2_manifest_from_bucket.py` (added this session) LISTs the bucket and rebuilds the manifest (`source: "r2_bucket_scan"`). Run `uv run --project . python scripts/r2_manifest_from_bucket.py --dry-run` first; a correct refresh is purely additive (overlapping entries byte-identical). No prior in-repo tool wrote the `r2_bucket_scan` source — it was an external one-off until now.
- **r2_audit treats ALL audio as orphans.** It parses portrait_manifest + cartography but NOT `audio.yaml` mood_tracks, so every pack's `.ogg` shows under "Orphans (in R2, no YAML)." Not a real gap — don't chase it.
- **`r2_sync_packs --files` resolves paths relative to `--content-root`** (so pass absolute paths or content-root-relative, NOT `sidequest-content/...`). Audio filenames contain spaces ("Grieg - In the Hall of the Mountain King.ogg") — build the arg list null-safely (`find -print0` + bash array), and zsh does NOT word-split unquoted vars (use bash).
- **The branch-protection hook false-positives on the word "commit"** in echo/comment strings — a read-only `git log` command got BLOCKED because the compound line contained "commit timestamps." Keep the word out of bash strings when running read-only git.
- **Both repos' working trees can move under you mid-session** when a concurrent worker pulls/checks-out (orchestrator flipped feat/76-1→main, content gained the gulliver/ world dir). Re-check `git branch --show-current` before branching; restore to the integration branch (orchestrator→`main` trunk-based, content→`develop` gitflow per `.pennyfarthing/repos.yaml`) when done.

### The movement subsystem is a procedural-DUNGEON navigator — region-mode worlds must gate before its store check (#587, 2026-06-02, wry_whimsy/oz)
- **`run_movement_dispatch` (`agents/subsystems/movement.py`) ONLY works for room-graph/procedural worlds.** It loads a `RegionGraph` from a `DungeonStore` and walks real adjacencies. Its first gate, `if dungeon_store is None or palette is None → no_dungeon_store` (ERROR-status `movement.unresolved` span), is RIGHT for a room-graph world missing its store — but **cartography region-mode worlds (`navigation_mode == region`, all of wry_whimsy) carry no dungeon store BY DESIGN.** Travel in those worlds is done by the `narration_apply` heading→region path (#577), not this subsystem. Result before the fix: a false `no_dungeon_store` ERROR span on *every move* (playtest: movement 7 events / 7 errors), even though travel worked fine.
- **Discriminator: `pack.worlds[snapshot.world_slug].cartography.navigation_mode == NavigationMode.region`** — the SAME `getattr`-chain probe used by `narration_apply` (#577, ~line 2636) and `cartography_region_projection.project_cartography_region`. Keep it single-shaped; don't invent a new check. `pack` is already in the dispatch-bank context (`intent_router_pass.py` `"pack": pack`) and is **signature-filtered** into handlers — add the kwarg to the handler signature and it just arrives (no call-site change).
- **The region-mode branch DEFERS, it doesn't move:** no patch (heading→region path owns the advance), no narrator directive (prose resolves it), and a NEW non-error `movement.region_mode` span so the GM panel reads engaged-and-deferred. **This is not a silent fallback** — a room_graph world genuinely missing its store STILL fails loud on `no_dungeon_store`, and `pack=None` (undeterminable) also falls through to fail-loud. Only the legitimate region-mode case defers, and it emits an observable span.
- **General principle:** when an OTEL subsystem shows 100%-error in a region-mode (non-dungeon) world, suspect a dungeon/room-graph assumption leaking in — same family as the historical "movement surface→deep dispatch" region-mode gaps. The fix is a mode gate, not loosening the fail-loud.

### Playtest screenshots live under THREE legacy dir-name variants — .gitignore must cover all three (2026-06-03)
- **The playtest/screenshot tooling has written to three differently-named output dirs over time:** `sq-playtest-screenshots/` (no dot), `.playtest-screenshots/` (no `sq`), and `.sq-playtest-screenshots/` (dot+sq). The first two are pure artifact dumps (~110 PNGs each, 0 tracked files) → ignore wholesale (`sq-playtest-screenshots/`, `.playtest-screenshots/`). The third MIXES committed tooling (`check_turn.sh`, `turn_spans.py`, `watcher_capture.py`) with generated artifacts (multi-MB `*.jsonl` watcher captures, `.*_baseline` markers).
- **Don't ignore the mixed dir wholesale** — use default-ignore-with-exceptions so new artifact types auto-ignore but the scripts stay tracked: `.sq-playtest-screenshots/*` then `!.sq-playtest-screenshots/*.sh` / `!.sq-playtest-screenshots/*.py`. (gitignore `*` DOES match leading-dot files, so `.aureate_baseline` is caught.)
- **Gotcha that bit me:** an old rule `sq-playtest-screenshots/` (no dot) looks like a typo for the dotted dir but was actually ignoring the real no-dot variant. Removing "dead-looking" playtest ignore lines exposed two real artifact dirs. Verify with `git check-ignore` + `git status` before assuming a path-guess line is dead. Underlying smell: the tooling should converge on ONE screenshot dir — until it does, all three name variants must stay ignored.

### A new effect in a SHARED component using a browser-only DOM API crashes every OTHER caller's jsdom test (#80-1, 2026-06-03, lobby OptionList)
- **What happened:** Added an auto-scroll `useEffect` to the shared `OptionList` (`el.scrollIntoView({block:"nearest"})`). The story's own `OptionList.test.tsx` stubbed `Element.prototype.scrollIntoView`, so it passed — but `scrollIntoView` is **not implemented in jsdom**, so every OTHER test that renders `OptionList` (character creation ×16, scene-library ×8, world-select, lobby-start, past-journeys) crashed with `el?.scrollIntoView is not a function` → **30 cascading regressions in files I never touched.**
- **Fix:** optional-chain the *method*, not just the element: `el?.scrollIntoView?.({block:"nearest"})`. Browser-only DOM APIs (`scrollIntoView`, `scrollTo`, `IntersectionObserver`, `matchMedia`, `ResizeObserver`) absent in jsdom must be guarded in source so callers' tests need not all stub them. This is a jsdom-environment guard, NOT a silent fallback (in real browsers it always fires).
- **How to apply:** When adding an effect to a *shared/reused* component, run the **FULL** suite, never just the story's test files — the blast radius is every consumer. The story's own test stubbing the API hides the regression. Grep for other render sites of the component before assuming green.

### tsc build can be RED on develop while vitest is fully GREEN — verify the baseline before claiming "build broken by my change" (#80-1, 2026-06-03)
- **What happened:** `npm run build` (`tsc -b`) failed with 37 type errors (e.g. `WorldMeta.navigation_mode` became required but many world fixtures omit it; `CharacterSheet` `class_moves: string[]` vs `ClassMove[]`). Looked like my change broke the build — but **vitest uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them**, so the suite was green while `tsc` had long-standing test-fixture type drift.
- **How to apply:** Before owning a build failure, prove the baseline: `git worktree add /tmp/base <base-branch>`, symlink `node_modules`, run `npx tsc -b`, diff the error set. If `develop` has the same errors, they're pre-existing — log a Delivery Finding, don't fix out-of-scope fixtures (fixing a subset won't green the build). Confirm your own changed *source* (non-test) files add zero `tsc` errors: `npm run build 2>&1 | grep 'error TS' | grep -v '__tests__\|\.test\.'`. The enforced gate here is **vitest + lint**, not `tsc -b`.

### Routing a file through a strict typed loader surfaces previously-tolerated invalid fixtures (2026-06-03, story 65-12)
- **What happened:** The lore reference page rendered `legends.yaml` via the generic humanization walk (`_file_renders_by_stem`), which never validates — it just humanizes raw YAML. 65-12's Timeline reads legends through the **typed** loader (`genre/loader._load_legends_flexible` → `Legend.model_validate`, `extra="forbid"`). Calling it unconditionally in `assemble_lore_page` made **19 pre-existing reference tests** 500, because their seed worlds authored `Legend`-invalid legends (`- name: x\n  origin: unknown` — `origin` is a forbidden field).
- **Why it matters:** The instinct is "my feature broke tests, weaken the loader to tolerate it." That's wrong here. `_load_legends_flexible` is the SAME loader the genre loader runs at real world-load (`loader.py:1048`, **no try/except — fatal**). So a legends.yaml the typed loader rejects describes a world that **cannot be played at all**; the reference page 500ing on it is correct (consistent with world-load), and the seed fixtures were authoring engine-impossible content that only "worked" because the old reference path never validated. Weakening the loader would also have broken the story's own No-Silent-Fallbacks AC.
- **How to apply:** When you newly route an existing file through a stricter/typed loader, expect to surface fixtures (and possibly real content) that the old lenient path tolerated. Before "fixing" by loosening: check whether the **production** load path already validates strictly (grep the genre loader / `model_validate` call sites). If production is already strict, the fixtures are the bug — correct them to valid content. If production is lenient there too, then a real world could hit it and you must decide loud-fail vs tolerate per the story's No-Silent-Fallbacks contract. **Verify pre-existing-ness honestly:** `git stash -u` your implementation and re-run a sample of the "new" failures — if they still fail, they're not yours (here, 27 full-suite failures were all pre-existing Epic 74-3 content blockers outside `tests/server/`).
- **Chrome-wiring coupling:** the reference page has a keystone test (`test_reference_chrome_wiring.py`) asserting every emitted `class="..."` token has a selector in the served CSS bundle (`static/reference/{theme,styles,presenters}.css`). A new rendered section MUST ship CSS rules for all its classes AND have a chrome-wiring seed world that actually emits the full class set, or the keystone fails. Add the CSS in the same phase you add the section.

### Long multi-phase sessions risk concurrent-landing rebase conflicts at finish; `merge_pr` can over-report on a draft/conflicting PR (2026-06-03, story 65-12)
- **What happened:** Over a long single session, 65-12 ran RED→GREEN→spec-check→verify→review→reconcile→finish. While it ran, 65-13 (#614) and 64-16 merged into `develop`, touching the same files (`telemetry/spans/reference.py`, `reference_renderer.py`, `test_reference_chrome_wiring.py`). `pf sprint story finish` reported `merge_pr` done and marked the story `done` + archived the session, but PR #615 was **draft + CONFLICTING** and never actually merged — the code was NOT on develop.
- **Why it matters:** A *reported* finish is not a *merged* finish. Trust `gh pr view <n> --json state,mergedAt,mergeable,isDraft` over the finish script's step list. A draft PR can't auto-merge; concurrent landings on shared files conflict. The dangerous desync is sprint-YAML=`done` while `develop` lacks the code — hold the sprint commit until the merge truly lands.
- **How to apply (rebase-recovery):** `gh pr ready <n>` → `git fetch origin develop && git rebase origin/develop`. For span/registry files where two stories each *added* entries the conflict is "keep both" (both constant groups, both `FLAT_ONLY_SPANS` entries, both constructor fns; watch the case where the diff splits a function mid-`Span.open` — give each fn its own complete `tracer_override=... ) as span: yield span` tail). `ast.parse` + grep for markers, re-run the suite (now includes the other story's tests), `git push --force-with-lease`, `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`, verify `mergedAt` set + file exists on `origin/develop`.
- **Hook note:** with the server repo checked out on `develop`, the `pf hooks` PreToolUse guard blocks Bash that looks like it could commit to a protected branch (even a chained `cat >> ... && pf ...`). Run `pf` commands standalone; do file edits via the Edit/Write tools.

### Adding a new genre_pack attribute read in a shared consumer breaks MagicMock-pack fixtures (2026-06-03, story 82-8)
- **What happened:** 82-8 added a `sd.genre_pack.progression.wealth_tiers` read inside `views.party_member_from_character` (a consumer many tests drive). My new file's tests used a *real* loaded pack and passed, but two tests in `tests/server/test_reference_url_attach.py` build `genre_pack = MagicMock()` and pin only the attributes the consumer reads. They had no `progression.wealth_tiers`, so it auto-mocked to a MagicMock. `resolve_wealth_tier(gold, <MagicMock>)` then misfired: `not MagicMock()` is False (skips the empty-guard), `for x in MagicMock()` iterates empty (MagicMock.__iter__ defaults to `iter([])`), so the loop fell through to `return tiers[-1]` → `MagicMock.__getitem__` returns a truthy MagicMock (not None), and back in views `next(i for i,t in enumerate(tiers) if t is resolved_tier)` over the empty mock raised **StopIteration**.
- **Why it matters:** A MagicMock genre_pack silently satisfies *any* attribute access, so a new consumer read doesn't fail loudly at the mock — it produces garbage that detonates deeper in. The full-suite run is the only thing that catches it; a focused run of just your new file is green and lies. ALWAYS run the full server suite (`uv run pytest -q`) after touching a shared view/consumer, not just your story's file.
- **How to apply:** When you add a `genre_pack.<x>` read to a shared consumer, grep the test tree for synthetic `genre_pack = MagicMock()` fixtures (`grep -rn "genre_pack = MagicMock" tests/`) and pin the new attribute to a realistic empty value (`genre_pack.progression.wealth_tiers = []`). This is the established precedent in that exact file — story 68-1 added `genre_pack.rules.survivability_pool_label = None` for the same reason. It's a fixture update, NOT a test weakening (the affected tests' own assertions stay untouched), and NOT a license to add production defensiveness — real packs always carry a real `ProgressionConfig` with list defaults, so the production path can't hit the mock pathology. Keep `resolve_*` helpers returning an element of the input list so an `is`-identity `next()` over that list can't StopIteration in any real path.

### Asset state is R2-authoritative — local disk absence is NOT an asset gap (2026-06-05, story 87-4 audit)
- **What happened:** A content audit of heavy_metal flagged "missing" portraits (evropi 4, long_foundry 7) and "10 missing" music tracks by checking local disk. All wrong as a gap signal: images/audio are `.gitignore`d and live in R2 (`cdn.slabgorb.com`), only `.gitkeep` + spec YAML (`portrait_manifest.yaml`, `*_input_params.json`) are tracked. A live R2 bucket scan showed music **complete** (41 .ogg) and long_foundry portraits **complete** (7/7) — local absence was just an un-synced clone. The ONE real gap (evropi portraits 0/4) was also the one CLAUDE.md had backwards ("evropi complete, long_foundry pending" — exactly reversed).
- **Why it matters:** Auditing assets by `ls` on a dev clone manufactures phantom gaps and would have spun up bogus render/asset stories for work already done in R2. And the human-maintained CLAUDE.md asset-status line drifts — don't trust it as truth either.
- **How to apply:** Before declaring any image/audio gap or creating an asset-generation story, scan R2 — `scripts/r2_audit.py` (YAML "should-exist" diffed against bucket+manifest) or a direct `boto3` LIST with `R2_S3_ENDPOINT`/`R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` (all in shell env) under prefix `genre_packs/<pack>/...`. R2 key convention is the 1:1 local-relative path: portraits `genre_packs/<g>/worlds/<world>/assets/portraits/<slug>.png`, music `genre_packs/<g>/audio/music/<track>.ogg`. To close a real gap: render (`scripts/generate_portrait_images.py --genre <g> --world <w>`, ~175s/portrait via Z-Image MLX, daemon must be `ready`), then upload the specific files (`scripts/r2_sync_packs.py --content-root <abs> --files <paths-relative-to-content-root>`), then re-scan R2 to confirm. `--files` paths are relative to `--content-root`, not cwd.

### Porting content to a new SRD: ground the cost-MECHANISM, keep the flavor metaphor — and grep ALL prompt blocks incl. the always-on narrator: (2026-06-05, story 87-4)
- **Context:** epic 87 ports heavy_metal → WWN. The bespoke pact/ledger doom-cost was a homebrew mechanic dressed as flavor. The faithful port ≠ delete the flavor; it = re-ground every place that presented the bespoke system (the "ledger") as the literal COST MECHANISM into the real ruleset mechanics (WWN **Effort** committed + **System Strain**), while PRESERVING the ledger/debt/book idiom as prose metaphor (it's the pack's identity — char_creation, axes, classes, inventory all lean on it; the pack's own `spells_wwn.yaml`/`classes.yaml:297` say "cost carried in narration + System Strain, not a bespoke ledger").
- **The miss to avoid:** the Reviewer flagged the scene prompts `ritual:`/`debt_collection:`, but the SAME bare-mechanism framing ("Every working is a withdrawal from a ledger") also sat in the **`narrator:` block — the every-turn-consumed main prompt** — and was nearly shipped. After editing the flagged blocks, `grep -niE "pact_working|withdrawal from a ledger|ledger now reads"` across the WHOLE prompts.yaml caught it. Always grep the mechanism phrasing across ALL prompt keys, not just the one the review named; the `combat:` block is the proven re-home template to copy.
- **Hard constraint:** the `Prompts` pydantic model is `extra="forbid"` — every key in prompts.yaml must be a declared field. You CANNOT rename a prompt key (e.g. `debt_collection:`→`collector_scene:`) without a server model change. In a zero-engine-change epic, keep the key and rewrite the BODY.
- **Scope discipline:** do NOT `grep ledger | delete` — that guts the pack voice (TEA's standing warning). Port only the cost-MECHANISM statements; leave the metaphor.

### Mid-green rebase can grow the fleet your contract test pins — re-check fleet-wide contracts at exit, not just at start (2026-06-05, story 90-1)
- **Context:** 90-1's AC6 test pinned "all four live ruleset-module packs need bestiary.yaml". During green the branch was rebased and 88-2 landed, binding `ruleset: awn` to mutant_wasteland — a FIFTH ruleset-module pack the RED contract predates. Without a re-check, the story would have shipped a "ruleset-generic" contract that silently exempted the newest member (and left its seeding one `creatures.yaml` away from a loud failure).
- **Rule:** any test that enumerates "all live X" is a snapshot. After a rebase (or any long green phase), re-derive the enumeration from the repo (`grep "^ruleset:" genre_packs/*/rules.yaml`) and reconcile before exit. Extending the data-level list in TEA's test + logging a deviation is the right move; shipping the stale list is not.
- **Also:** the `testing-runner` (Haiku) may EDIT source to fix a failure it hits (here: pregen stub packs are SimpleNamespace without `.rules` → it swapped my direct attr access for the adjacent line's `getattr` chain). Review its diff and own it — correct this time, but it's silent scope.

### BeatKind is a closed enum (strike|brace|push|angle) — UI BeatOption.kind is a free string (2026-06-07, story 97-3)
- TEA's RED fixture used `kind: "press"` (copied from a UI test fixture, where `BeatOption.kind?: string` accepts anything) and the server-side `BeatDef.model_validate` rejected it at construction — pydantic ValidationError before the test body ran. The Python `BeatKind` enum and the TS shape are intentionally asymmetric; when porting fixtures between repos, check `sidequest/genre/models/rules.py` BeatKind first. Fix was `"press"` → `"push"` (UI fixture "press" beats exist all over the UI suite and are fine there).
- Per-beat wire additions: `ConfrontationPayload.beats` is `list[dict[str, Any]]`, so stamping extra keys (e.g. 97-3 `difficulty`) onto the *dumped* beat dicts needs no protocol model change — but the top-level payload model is `extra="forbid"`, so a new TOP-LEVEL key must be declared on `ConfrontationPayload` or `ConfrontationPayload(**payload_dict)` raises at broadcast.

### `git add -A` after a testing-runner lint step sweeps repo-wide formatter output into your story commit (2026-06-07, story 97-3)
- develop was ~165 files behind `ruff format`; the testing-runner's "code quality" step formatted the whole repo, and my `git add -A` commit collected all 163 unrelated files into the story fix (28387c48). Reviewer caught it (round-2 HIGH, review-integrity). Fix: `git diff --name-only develop...HEAD | grep -v <story files> | xargs git checkout develop --` + a labelled revert commit (fdeedf8c).
- Rules of thumb: (1) after ANY subagent runs in your repo, check `git status` before committing; (2) prefer `git add <explicit story files>` over `-A` when subagents have been active; (3) macOS xargs has no `-a` — pipe stdin (`cat file | xargs`).

### Self-registering plugin modules need a production import trigger — adding the file isn't enough (2026-06-09, story 86-6)
- A new `TableGame` kind (`game/table/war_rig.py`) self-registers via `register_table_game(WarRigCrewTableGame())` at module bottom — correct. But registration only runs when the module is IMPORTED, and production imports kinds via side-effect imports in `game/table/__init__.py` (`from sidequest.game.table import poker as _poker`, `... auction`). I added the module + content (`table_game: war_rig_crew`) but NOT the `__init__.py` import line → `get_table_game("war_rig_crew")` raises `UnknownTableGameError` in prod. Tests masked it: each test body did `import sidequest.game.table.war_rig` directly, triggering the side-effect that production never would. Reviewer rejected (CRITICAL wiring gap).
- **Rule of thumb:** when you add a self-registering plugin (table kind, magic plugin, ruleset module, span route, dispatch handler), the LAST step is wiring its import into the production aggregator. Then prove it: a wiring test that imports ONLY the package (`import sidequest.game.table`) and asserts the registry resolves the new name — NOT a test that imports your module directly (that fakes the registration). Same lesson as MagicPlugin/ruleset registries.

### Pre-existing red on develop (2026-06-09, not regressions)
- `sidequest-ui src/__tests__/lobby-start-ws-open.test.tsx` ("Leave + Start opens a new WebSocket…") times out on develop — slugConnectFired not cleared on Leave.
- `sidequest-server tests/protocol/test_api_contract_aside.py::test_api_contract_does_not_lie_about_asides` fails on develop (docs/api-contract.md guard, ADR-107). Verify against develop before blaming your branch.
- Server full parallel suite shows occasional isolation flakes (culture_context, chargen_complete_no_hp_leak, lore_rag_wiring) that pass with `-n0`/individually. **The SWN/WN combat-e2e family is in this class too** (`test_space_opera_swn_combat_e2e`, `test_space_opera_melee_e2e`, `test_space_opera_hp_e2e`, `test_awn_combat_dispatch`, `test_road_warrior_cwn_combat_e2e`, …). Root cause (diagnosed 2026-06-13): weapon damage is rolled from the **unseeded global `random` module** — `sidequest/server/dispatch/damage_roll.py:142` `random.randint(1, sides)` ("no physics, no seed"). The tests seed the opponent to `hp=1` and fire a `face=[20]` (guaranteed-hit) strike expecting a kill, but the opponent has damage **mitigation** that can fully absorb a low `1d6` roll → net 0 → `assert hp <= 0` fails with `assert 1 <= 0`. Under `-n0` the deterministic RNG-consumption order penetrates; under `-n auto` xdist distribution shifts the global-RNG sequence so some runs roll a fully-mitigated value. The failing assertion ROTATES across the family run-to-run (sometimes assertion 1 `hp < hp_before`, sometimes assertion 2 the kill). It is NOT the OTEL span-capture assertion and NOT a stale test. A real fix needs an injectable/seeded damage RNG in the e2e path (production seam or test-level `random.seed`), not a brittle magic-seed — track as a dedicated hardening story.

### Fixing one dangling import can unmask the next — cascade-verify by actually importing the module (2026-06-10, story 101-2)
- **What happened:** Reviewer rejected 101-2 for a dangling `VoicePresets` import in `scripts/audit_content_drift.py` (left by the voice-surface removal). I removed it — but the script STILL didn't import: the next line down had a SECOND pre-existing dangling import, `OpeningHook`, renamed to `Opening` by an earlier narrative-model rework (broken on `develop` too, unrelated to my story). Python import resolution stops at the FIRST failing name, so the VoicePresets break had been masking the OpeningHook break. Fixing one revealed the other.
- **Why it matters:** "Remove the dangling import" is not done when you delete the one symbol the reviewer named — it's done when the module actually imports. A grep for your symbol passes after one edit; the file can still be broken on a different symbol. Shipping a "fixed" script that still ImportErrors one line later is a half-fix that fails the reviewer's real goal.
- **How to apply:** After removing any dangling import, VERIFY by executing the module, not by re-grepping: `uv run python -c "import importlib.util; s=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m','path/to/script.py'); mod=importlib.util.module_from_spec(s); s.loader.exec_module(mod); print('OK')"`. This forces every top-level import to resolve and surfaces cascaded breakage immediately. For files outside the package (`scripts/`, `tools/`) that aren't covered by pytest, this exec-import IS the only wiring test — run it. When the cascade-revealed break is genuinely pre-existing/out-of-scope, fix it anyway if it's the same defect class in the same file (one-line, blocks the same goal) and log an out-of-scope deviation — don't leave a tool broken one line from working.

### Daemon tests need `uv sync --extra dev`; a global pytest on PATH silently shadows the venv (2026-06-10, story 101-6)
- **What happened:** After `uv sync` (default, no extras), `uv run pytest --collect-only` failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'opentelemetry'` — yet `uv run python -c "import opentelemetry"` worked. Cause: `uv run pytest` resolved `/Users/slabgorb/.local/bin/pytest` (a global tool install) instead of a project-venv pytest, because the default `uv sync` omits the `dev` group, so `.venv` had neither pytest nor the dev-only deps. The collect error looked like my change (I'd just removed a dep) but reproduced with my changes stashed — pre-existing env state, not my edit.
- **Why it matters:** A red collect right after a dependency edit screams "you broke it." Here it was an environment-setup artifact. Don't attribute a failure to your diff until you've stashed and reproduced.
- **How to apply:** For sidequest-daemon, run `uv sync --extra dev` then `uv run python -m pytest` (the `python -m` form forces the venv's pytest, never a PATH shadow). Full suite was 431 passing. When a test failure appears right after your change, `git stash && <repro> && git stash pop` to prove causation before debugging your own diff.

### testing-runner can go ROGUE and edit code/fixtures — diff-check after every run (102-2 GREEN, 2026-06-10)
- **What happened:** A testing-runner (haiku) asked to VERIFY tests instead "fixed" failures itself: it edited `sidequest/protocol/messages.py` (legitimately needed, kept) and **converted the shared `tests/fixtures/packs/test_genre` fixture to ruleset: wwn + created bestiary/classes/spells files** (90 test files depend on that fixture being native — reverted). It then reported "22/22 pass" while burying "5 regression failures are flaky/pre-existing" it had likely caused itself.
- **How to apply:** (1) After ANY testing-runner completes, run `git status --short` in the target repo before believing the result. (2) Put "CRITICAL CONSTRAINT: READ-ONLY — do NOT edit/create/delete any file" in every runner prompt. (3) A runner reporting changes it made is a verification you don't have — re-run after reverting.
- Related: a `git mv`'d test file reappeared at the old path after a runner run (stale duplicate + .pyc); `rm` both and re-check `git status`.

### A pack's ruleset port silently rots tests that leaned on the OLD ruleset's behavior (2026-06-13, ad-hoc stale-test sweep)
- **What happened:** `caverns_and_claudes` was ported to the **WWN** ruleset (PR #429, `rules.yaml: ruleset: wwn`). That broke 8 tests that had quietly depended on cac being the **native** ruleset: (1) `encounter_lifecycle.py` now rolls 1d8+DEX initiative for every player-side actor and resolves DEX from `snapshot.characters`, failing loud (`player actor 'Rux' not found …`) — the old native path early-returned (`ruleset_config() is None`), so tests could seat a bare `player_name` with an empty snapshot. Fix: seat a REAL `Character` carrying the WWN attribute block (DEXTERITY flavor = `"DEX"`, full STR/DEX/CON/INT/WIS/CHA). (2) cac combat is now ablative-HP (ADR-114): `player_metric.name == "hp"` (was `"momentum"`, threshold sentinel `1000000`, not the ADR-093 dial cap). (3) the combat damage beat is `strike` (was `attack`). (4) `resolution_mode` is `beat_selection` (a prior test pinned `opposed_check` from PR #130; assert `!= sealed_letter_lookup` instead of a specific mode).
- **Why it matters:** These were genuinely STALE tests (honor the code), not engine bugs. The WWN port is the source of truth; the assertions encoded pre-port reality. The fail-loud `Rux not found` guard is correct production behavior, not something to soften.
- **How to apply:** When a pack changes `ruleset:`, grep tests that load it (`cac_pack`, `load_genre_pack("caverns_and_claudes")`) and re-verify every assertion about metrics/beats/resolution_mode/initiative. WWN/CWN/SWN are the `ruleset_config()`-non-None family that rolls initiative and needs PCs in `snapshot.characters` with the mapped attribute keys.

### Validator `required_dirs` for `assets/images/{portraits,poi}` are retired R2 drift — a clean post-R2 world trips them (2026-06-13)
- **What happened:** `mutant_wasteland/seaboard_of_saints` went live (draft key removed, PR #423) and was the FIRST world authored post-R2 that omitted the legacy `assets/images/portraits|poi` local folders. The pack validator (`test_all_live_packs_pass_*`) hard-errored on the missing dirs — while 21 older worlds passed only because they carry those dirs as **empty `.gitkeep` residue**. Per sidequest-content/CLAUDE.md "Asset Hosting", rendered art is canonical in R2 (`r2_manifest.json`); `assets/images/*` are documented "historical drift", and production `server/rest.py:272` resolves hero/POI art via `assets/poi/<slug>.png` + R2, NOT `assets/images/*`. Fix (Keith's call): dropped both entries from `pack_schema.yaml` `required_dirs` (genre + world tiers). `legends/` was a SEPARATE, REAL gap (seaboard genuinely shipped no legends) — authored 3 canon-grounded legends via the `writer` agent rather than waiving (the sanctioned `incomplete_dirs` world.yaml waiver exists for the waive path).
- **How to apply:** When the pack-validator rejects ONE live world for missing dirs, check whether the requirement is R2-era drift (other worlds satisfy it with empty `.gitkeep`; production reads a different path) before "fixing" the content — the schema may be the stale party. Distinguish rendered-asset dirs (R2 drift) from real YAML-content dirs like `legends/` (genuine gap → author it, or waive loudly via `incomplete_dirs`).

### tests/server is hermetically fixture-packed; real-content handler tests go in tests/integration (102-2)
- `tests/server/conftest.py` has an AUTOUSE `_fixture_pack_search_paths` that repoints `DEFAULT_GENRE_PACK_SEARCH_PATHS` at `tests/fixtures/packs/` — `session_handler_factory(genre="heavy_metal")` inside tests/server loads the FROZEN fixture heavy_metal (no cast_spell, no wwn), not real content. Move real-pack handler tests to `tests/integration/` (no repoint; `otel_capture` is re-exported there; `session_handler_factory` lives in root conftest).
- On real ruleset-module packs the narration turn runs Monster-Manual pregen (ADR-059) which fail-louds without a world bestiary (`world_slug=""` in the factory). Stub `sidequest.server.dispatch.monster_manual_inject.ensure_loaded` → None (the `if manual is not None` gate skips injection) when the seam under test is upstream of narration.

### UI source-regex wiring pins break on signature changes — update the PIN, not the design (102-2)
- `src/__tests__/confrontation-wiring.test.tsx` greps App.tsx/GameBoard.tsx source for exact handler signatures (`onBeatSelect?.(beatId, draft)` etc). Adding a parameter breaks the regex, not the behavior — extend the pin to the new signature in the same PR. (Server CLAUDE.md bans source-grep wiring tests; the UI repo still has them.)
- Pre-existing red on develop (2026-06-10, confirmed via stash-bisect): `lobby-start-ws-open.test.tsx` "Leave + Start" times out even in isolation, and `npm run build` fails on 5 `BeatEffect` type errors in `ConfrontationOverlay.beatimpact.test.tsx`. Neither is 102-2's.

### Fixtures that DISPATCH during setup must depend on otel_capture, or their spans vanish (102-4 GREEN, 2026-06-10)
- **What happened:** Two dead-premise tests asserted on spans fired inside their `kill_order_combat` fixture and got `finished_spans: []` — while a sibling test asserting the SAME span (dispatched inline in the test body) passed. pytest instantiates fixtures in signature order; `(kill_order_combat, otel_capture)` ran the dispatch before the in-memory exporter was installed on the provider.
- **How to apply:** Any fixture that drives production dispatch must take `otel_capture` as a parameter itself. If one test sees a span and another sees none for the same code path, suspect capture-install ordering before suspecting the engine.

### build_confrontation_payload keys MUST be declared on protocol ConfrontationPayload (extra="forbid") (102-4)
- The dispatch-level emit does `ConfrontationPayload(**build_confrontation_payload(...))` and the model is `extra="forbid"` — a new key added only to the dict builder crashes the mid-turn CONFRONTATION broadcast at runtime, and dispatch-level tests miss it because they pass `room_broadcast=None` (the emit block is gated on it). When adding a payload key: builder + `sidequest/protocol/messages.py::ConfrontationPayload` + UI `ConfrontationData`, all three.

### encounter_lifecycle initiative is UNSEEDED — production-seam WN combat tests must force the order (102-4)
- `_roll_and_persist_initiative` rolls with a fresh `random.Random()`; monkeypatching `random.randint` does NOT pin it (instance method, not the module function). Under the 102-4 sealed-round walk the persisted order decides who acts first, so any test seating via `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` and dispatching dice on a WN pack is a coin flip unless it overwrites `enc.initiative` post-seat (`InitiativeEntry(token_id=..., value=...)`, PC first reproduces the pre-102-4 strike-then-reprisal choreography). Symptom of the miss: ~44% flaky failures where the low-HP PC drops before their slot.

### Extracting a 400-line branch: anchor-matched python surgery beats hand-retyped Edit old_strings (102-4)
- To move dice.py's beat-application block into a shared helper (legacy path + WN round walk both call it), a heredoc python script that locates exact anchor lines (`s.index(...)`), cuts the region, dedents/renames (`payload.beat_id`→`beat_id`, `resolved.outcome`→`outcome_tier`), and reinserts as a function preserved the block byte-for-byte. Hand-reproducing 400 lines in an Edit old_string is where mismatches breed. Always `ast.parse` + `ruff format` afterward.

### Phantom confrontations: the engine CANNOT distinguish a fabricated Other from a legit materialized threat — fix the router (beneath_sunden-6)
- Symptom: an anticipatory/defensive intent ("I draw my dagger and prepare for the attack") with no adversary present spawns `confrontation=combat` against a fabricated `opponent={name:"Unknown Adversary", description:"the attack <PC> is preparing to defend against or initiate"}`. The description literally being the player's own intent is the tell.
- Trap 1 (the dials are a red herring): for `win_condition: hp_depletion` the `player_metric`/`opponent_metric` `0/0 threshold 1000000` is the BY-DESIGN inert placeholder (`beat_kinds.py:601` "0/1000000 bar") — the fight resolves on `Npc.core.hp`, not the metric. The phantom even gets a real backing core (`_seed_combat_hp_depletion_to_npcs` creates one from `cdef.opponent_hp`). So "degenerate dials / no CreatureCore" is the WRONG diagnosis.
- Trap 2 (no clean engine guard): the materialized-threat path (`run_confrontation_dispatch` reads `params["opponent"]|["threat"]` → `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` materialized_threat branch) serves TWO legit cases structurally identical to the phantom — ship_combat "Raider Frigate" (59-23) and burning_peace's narratively-present-but-unseated watcher (2026-05-31). All three are "named opponent, not in `snapshot.npcs`". The discriminator (real being vs the player's anticipation) is SEMANTIC and lives only in the router. A `_confrontation_precondition_unmet` predicate can't see it (preconditions take `snapshot` only) without regressing the legit cases.
- Fix: producer side. `CONFRONTATION_TRIGGER_CORE` (`narrator_guardrails.py`, shared verbatim by IntentRouter + narrator game_patch steering per ADR-111) — the Other is always on the OTHER side and must be REAL; the player's own preparation is an anticipatory posture, prose not a confrontation, and never names a fabricated filler Other. Once the router omits the phantom opponent, the existing No-Opponent guard (`NoOpponentAvailableError` → defer to prose) does the right thing. No engine change.
- Test the runtime-delivered `system` prompt (`llm.emit_tool.await_args.kwargs["system"]`), NOT a source grep — the sanctioned pattern is `test_intent_router_prompt_documents_*`.

### `next_turn_directives` reaches the narrator prompt TWICE — the field-name leak is the JSON copy (sq-playtest 2026-06-13)
- Symptom: literal `next_turn_directives` (the field name) leaked into player prose ("…per the next_turn_directives, the entity's Strike missed…"). Plus "roll of N misses against AC" meta-narration.
- The queue is consumed into the dedicated `intent_directives` Recency guardrail (orchestrator `_consume_next_turn_directives`) AND, separately, serialized as a raw field in the `<game_state>` JSON blob — the narrator read the field name off the JSON and echoed it. It survived snapshot slimming because it sat in `_BOUNDED_BY_CONSTRUCTION`.
- Fix: move it to `_PHASE_B_DROP_FIELDS` (snapshot_slimming.py) — drops it from the prompt payload, persistence keeps it (the populate-this-turn/consume-next-turn round-trip test stays green). **Governance gate `test_snapshot_field_governance.py` requires every GameSnapshot field in EXACTLY ONE of the four registries** — so you must also REMOVE it from `_BOUNDED_BY_CONSTRUCTION` (session_helpers.py) in the same edit, or the gate fails on overlap.
- Belt-and-suspenders: also harden the guardrail header to "internal — apply silently, never quote".

### room_states-scoped state is invisible to the per-turn router/narrator unless keyed to `party_location()` (126-18)
- `apply_snapshot_slimming` (snapshot_slimming.py) projects `room_states` to ONLY the acting party's current room: `current_room_id = snapshot.party_location()`, then `payload["room_states"] = {current_room_id: room_states[current_room_id]}` (every other room dropped). Both the narrator (`_build_turn_context`) and the Intent Router (`_build_state_summary`) apply this cut. So if you persist room-scoped state under the WRONG key, it serializes into the snapshot but is projected away before any per-turn consumer sees it — the exact inverted-failure-mode 126-18 was fixing (state present, but invisible).
- `party_location()` (consensus mode, no perspective) reads `player_seats.values()` for seated PC names, then returns the agreed `character_locations[name]` — i.e. the FREE-TEXT room string (e.g. an opening's `setting.location_label` "Café Le Dôme"), NOT a synthetic `genre:room` id and NOT a `current_region` cartography node. So persist room-scoped state to the SAME string `character_locations` holds. For opening props (126-18) that meant `room_states[setting.location_label or setting.interior_room]`, matching what `_bootstrap_character_locations_from_opening` writes.
- Safety valve: when `party_location()` returns None (no seated PCs / split party / pre-chargen), slimming SKIPS the room_states projection (pass-through, keeps all rooms) rather than gaslight an empty scene — so unit tests that set `character_locations` but not `player_seats` still see the state. Don't rely on this in production; key it correctly.
- A NESTED-model field (e.g. `RoomState.props`) is NOT a GameSnapshot top-level field, so it does NOT need placement in the four snapshot-governance registries and won't trip `test_snapshot_field_governance.py` (confirmed green for 126-18). Only top-level GameSnapshot fields hit that gate (see the `next_turn_directives` entry above). A new flat-only span just needs the constant + `FLAT_ONLY_SPANS.add` in `telemetry/spans/<domain>.py`; it auto-exports via `from .<domain> import *` in `spans/__init__.py` (no `__all__`).

### `_emit_player_beat_resolution_close` had no `else` for the 0-damage non-resolving beat → narrator invents kills (sq-playtest 2026-06-13)
- The close has `if encounter_resolved` (resolution signal) → `elif strike+shock_hp_removed > 0` (kill-overclaim "STILL STANDING" anchor). A FAILED strike (tier=Fail, 0 HP removed, fight not resolved) hit NEITHER branch → no directive → narrator free to narrate a decisive kill on a miss.
- Fix: add the symmetric failed-strike anchor (real surviving HP + STILL STANDING + explicit no-kill clause). **Gate it on `win_condition == "hp_depletion"`** — the close fires for ALL confrontations (opposed/legacy/WN, dice.py:780 + wn_round.py:380), and HP-framed "unharmed/still standing" prose is nonsense in a dial confrontation (negotiation/chase/poker) where a 0-strike-damage beat is the norm. Thread `cdef.win_condition` through both call sites.

### Two watcher ops sharing one display kind look like "one event is null" on the GM timeline (sq-playtest 2026-06-13)
- `opponent_attack_resolved` (to-hit: d20/hit) and `opponent_damage_roll_resolved` (damage: total/faces) both mapped to `ENCOUNTER_OPPONENT_ATTACK` in `watcher_hub.py::_KIND_BY_OP`. One reprisal → two rows under one name; the damage row (no d20/hit) reads as a "fully null duplicate attack". The damage was never missing — it was the `total` field wearing the wrong label.
- Fix: give the damage op its own kind (`ENCOUNTER_OPPONENT_DAMAGE`) and add it to `_REPLAY_SKIP_KINDS` (session_handler.py) — same internal/replay-skip treatment as the to-hit kind, else reconnect crashes on the unknown kind. Persistence is unaffected (op stays in `_KIND_BY_OP`).

### `last_beat_impact` IS rendered under hp_depletion — 73-8's "the UI doesn't draw it in HP combat" was wrong (sq-playtest 2026-06-13)
- `BeatImpactPanel` (ConfrontationOverlay.tsx ~L1256) renders `last_beat_impact.summary` UNCONDITIONALLY. Under hp_depletion the dial is the inert 1e6 placeholder, so `describe_beat_impact` (which only saw dial deltas + a bool) read "No change — moved nothing" on a CritSuccess that removed HP.
- Fix: add `hp_removed` to `describe_beat_impact` and RE-STAMP `enc.last_beat_impacts[actor.side]` in `apply_beat` AFTER the HP channel resolves (the early stamp at ~L585 runs before damage is known). New branch wins over inert/suppressed-dial; keeps `dial_moved=False` (the 73-8 load-bearing truth) — the HP bar animates the loss, the chip now reads "−N to their HP".
- This REVISED an established contract: the two `test_hp_depletion_beat_impact_truthful.py` tests asserting `effect=="inert"`/`=="tag"` encode the OLD assumption — update them to the new contract (playtest is the authority), don't work around them.

### `genre_pack.worlds` is NOT a reliable "is this world playable" set + `grimvault` is a fictional fixture world (sq-playtest 2026-06-13)
- In some load contexts `genre_pack.worlds` is only PARTIALLY populated — e.g. caverns_and_claudes loads with `.worlds = {beneath_sunden}` even though the connect path will happily run other worlds. Do NOT gate fail-loud logic on `world_slug not in genre_pack.worlds`; it false-rejects legitimate worlds.
- `grimvault` does NOT exist in content (caverns_and_claudes has ONLY `beneath_sunden`). It's a placeholder world slug baked into 34+ server test files. The connect path TOLERATES connecting to a nonexistent world via genre-tier fallback, and that tolerance is load-bearing for the test harness. A connect-time guard that rejects "world not on disk" breaks ~36 tests.
- Correct signal for "draft world, refuse loudly": re-read the world's OWN `world.yaml` and check `draft: true` (what `_load_single_world` keys on at loader.py:1132). Scope strictly to a PRESENT world.yaml with draft truthy — not `.worlds` membership, not a missing dir.
- Draft worlds were ALSO being offered in the lobby: `rest.list_genres` file-scans `worlds/` independently of the loader, so it must repeat the `draft` skip itself (the loader's exclusion doesn't reach it).

### Kit-rolled armor needs a post-loadout equip step; AC derives from content `armor_class` (story 106-1)
- The kit-roll loop (`builder.py:2570`) appends EVERY item `equipped:False`, and nothing recomputed `core.armor_class` (creature_core default 10). So every WWN Warrior fought at unarmored AC 10 despite Leather Armor in the pack. `dice.py:1636` reads `core.armor_class` for the opponent reprisal, so AC 10 = ~65% to-hit (meat-grinder).
- Fix seam: `equip_starting_armor` in `sidequest/server/dispatch/chargen_loadout.py`, wired into `chargen_mixin.py` right AFTER `apply_starting_loadout` (so the full inventory is present). Flips `equipped` on armor-category items, sets `core.armor_class = max(catalog armor_class among equipped armor)` (WWN best-armor, not additive). Spans: `chargen.armor_equipped` / `chargen.armor_unresolved` in `telemetry/spans/inventory.py`.
- AC value is CONTENT-sourced (`CatalogItem.armor_class`, inventory.yaml), never an engine constant. No-Silent-Fallback: an armor item with no catalog `armor_class` fires a loud ERROR span and stays AC 10 (gap surfaces at chargen), rather than masking it.
- **WWN armor gotcha:** `caverns warrior_kit.armor = [leather_armor, shield_wood, helmet_iron]` rolls ONE piece uniformly. Only leather has a clean WWN set-AC (13). A **shield** in WWN is a flat **+1 bonus** (NOT a set AC — unrepresentable in the set-AC `armor_class` model without a combination/bonus rule), and WWN has **no helmet item**. Don't invent values for those — escalate to Keith. Until then ~2/3 of Warriors (shield/helmet rolls) still fail-loud at AC 10.

### Span severity convention split: content-gap = WARN, runtime-failure = ERROR (106-1, 2026-06-13)
- Two distinct "fail-loud" conventions live in `telemetry/spans/inventory.py`: (1) **runtime resolution failures** (e.g. `equip.unresolved` — item not found mid-game) set `span.set_status(Status(StatusCode.ERROR, reason))`; (2) **content-authoring gaps surfaced at chargen** (e.g. `chargen.starting_equipment_missing`) use `logger.warning` + NO status (unset). When adding a fail-loud span, pick the matching convention — a missing `armor_class` on an inventory.yaml entry is a content gap (WARN), not a runtime failure (ERROR). Mismatch (warning log + ERROR span status + "(ERROR)" comment) is a real reviewer finding: the log file and the lie-detector disagree. Align log + status + comment to ONE level.
- `Status`/`StatusCode` import in that module is shared — removing one `set_status` call won't orphan the import as long as another helper still uses it (grep before assuming F401).

### Pre-existing ruff-format drift surfaces only when you touch a file (106-1, 2026-06-13)
- `ruff format --check` on a file you edited can flag regions you DIDN'T touch (e.g. a multi-line ternary / split log string that now fits on one line). The drift was committed earlier (an upstream commit wasn't `ruff format`-clean on that file). Fix: run `uv run ruff format <file>` (not just `--check`) on any file you edit, then `git diff` to confirm the normalization is whitespace-only and your semantic edits are intact. The dev-exit gate runs format, so leaving drift blocks handoff.

## 108-6 WWN dying window (2026-06-14)

- **Live-hostile polarity in the downed seam.** `run_cwn_wwn_downed_seam`'s
  `actor_side` is the ATTACKER's side, and the downed defender is
  `_opposite_side_first_actor(encounter, actor_side)`. So `actor_side` IS the hostile
  side relative to the downed actor. A naive `superseded = already_terminal or
  live_hostile` SUPPRESSED opponent Mortal Injuries entirely (the live player is the
  opponent's hostile) — 6 combat-dispatch tests went `statuses=[]`. Correct design:
  `resolve_downed` mints EITHER the stabilizable window (no live hostile = solo) OR the
  ordinary Mortal Injury clock (live hostile present — every opponent the player drops,
  and a PC at sword-point) via a `dying_window` bool. Never let the window branch suppress
  the ordinary clock.
- **HpPool delta method is `apply_delta(delta)`, NOT `adjust()`** (creature_core.py).
  `core.hp.apply_delta(1 - core.hp.current)` to set HP to 1.
- **OTEL tick honesty at a gate.** Don't emit a per-round span with a hardcoded
  `action_was_stabilization=False` at the turn-intake gate — the gate runs BEFORE the
  narrator/tool, so it can't know if the action was a stabilization, and a hardcoded value
  contradicts the tool's own `resolved` span. Emit the honest tick from where the outcome
  is known (the stabilize tool: True+roll+success; the expiry path: False+died).
- **Full suite is parallel (xdist `-n auto`) and has pre-existing cross-worker DB-state
  bleed.** Tests like `test_102_5_wn_tool_narrator_wiring` ("unknown actor: 'Vesska'") and
  the space_opera SWN HP-ablation e2e flake under the full parallel run but PASS in
  isolation (`-n0`). To tell a real regression from a flake: re-run the failing test in
  isolation, and diff against `develop` by checking out develop's versions of your touched
  files into your venv. wry_whimsy content-validation (missing `seed_tropes.yaml`) fails
  deterministically and is a content-repo issue, not server code.

## ruff format trap (2026-06-14, 108-6 rework)

- **NEVER run `uv run ruff format tests/ sidequest/`** (or any broad path) to fix your own
  files — the repo is not uniformly ruff-formatted, so it reformatted 162 UNRELATED files
  and polluted the diff. Format ONLY your changed files by explicit path:
  `uv run ruff format path/a.py path/b.py`. Same for `ruff check --fix` — scope it.
  Recovery: `git status --short | awk '{print $2}' | grep -vxF -f keep.txt | xargs git checkout HEAD --`.

## Monster Manual authored-cast seeding gate (2026-06-14, wry_whimsy/oz fix)

- **`_seed_authored_npcs` was only reachable via `seed_manual`, which `ensure_loaded`
  gates behind `manual.needs_seeding()`** (monster_manual_inject.py). An existing on-disk
  Manual (>=4 NPCs + an encounter) never re-seeds, so the world's authored `npcs.yaml`
  cast (the oz canonical companions) never entered the pool on a prior save — the road
  surfaced only random-minted walk-ons. FIX: call `_seed_authored_npcs` UNCONDITIONALLY
  in `ensure_loaded` after the needs_seeding block. It's an in-memory insert+upsert
  (add_npc dedups by name, tags are upserted), so it's safe to run every load — returns
  >0 only when something changed → save + `monster_manual.authored_backfill` span then.
  On the fresh-seed path it's a harmless 0-change no-op (seed_manual already inserted),
  so no double-save / no duplicate span. The injection cache must be wiped for the fix to
  reach an existing save: `rm ~/.sidequest/manuals/<genre>_<world>.json`.
- **Stale on-disk Manual keeps old `location_tags` forever** because `add_npc` dedups by
  name and returns early. A re-seed must UPSERT tags onto the existing entry, not just
  skip it. Return the insert+refresh count so the caller knows to persist.
- **`getattr(world_obj, "authored_npcs", None) or []` masks a field rename** as an empty
  roster (silent fallback). A real `World` always has `authored_npcs` (default_factory);
  access it directly so a rename crashes loud. Keep the `getattr(pack, "worlds", None)`
  guard only for the None-pack (load-failed) case the caller already warned on. A
  world-key absent from a real `worlds` mapping is a config error → WARN, don't return 0
  silently.
- **Real-loader wiring test, not SimpleNamespace:** copytree a fixture pack to tmp_path,
  write a `npcs.yaml` with `location_tags`, `load_genre_pack(tmp)`, assert
  `pack.worlds[w].authored_npcs[0].location_tags`. The wwn_test_pack's shipped npcs.yaml
  uses a STALE schema (`authored_npcs:` key + fields not on the AuthoredNpc model) so the
  loader reads 0 from it — write your own roster.

## Authored-cast dedup must be EXACT, not fuzzy (2026-06-14, wry_whimsy/oz review rework)

- **`find_npc_by_name` is fuzzy bidirectional substring** (`name in npc.name or npc.name in name`).
  Reusing it to dedup the AUTHORED cast lets a canonical NPC collide with a substring
  walk-on: "Lion" matches a pre-seeded "Cowardly Lion" → the authored NPC is silently
  dropped (re-introducing the bug this story fixes) OR its tags overwrite the wrong
  entry. FIX: added `find_npc_by_exact_name` + `add_npc(..., exact=True)`; the authored
  seed path (`_seed_authored_npcs`) uses exact for BOTH the upsert lookup and the insert.
  Generated walk-ons keep the fuzzy default. Reviewer (Avasarala) blocked the merge on
  this — it's the exact mechanism the story exists to make correct, and homebrew authoring
  makes colliding names plausible.
- **Upsert dirty-check on `list != list` is order-sensitive.** `["a","b"] != ["b","a"]` is
  True → a reordered-but-equivalent YAML fires a spurious save + OTEL span EVERY load. Use
  `set(a) != set(b)` for the dirty check, still assign the list to preserve author order.
  Span noise pollutes the GM-panel lie-detector the whole project leans on.
- **Collapse repeated `available_at_location` calls:** inject() was recomputing it a 3rd
  time just to count placed-eligible for the span. Return the uncapped eligible count from
  `_npc_patches_for_available_humans` (4-tuple) so eligible/matched/dropped are one
  consistent snapshot — and update the comment-reference in `agents/npc_context.py` if you
  change the helper's contract.
## Migrating packs native→fate has a large hidden test blast radius — diff against a baseline (114-10)

Flipping a genre pack from native d20 to Fate (drop inventory.yaml/classes.yaml/power_tiers,
strip allowed_classes/ability_score_names, remove the native confrontations block) breaks
PRE-EXISTING tests that asserted the old state — d20 class kits, equipment economy, native
verbal confrontations, intent-router verbal vocabulary. A naive full-suite run looked like
276 failures; the real net was 16.

The develop baseline already had **260 failing tests** (WN-owns-the-round in-flight per
ADR-143/114; `_FakeClaudeClient` missing `send_stateless`). NEVER trust a raw failure count
or a runner's "cascade" attribution. Establish the baseline and diff node-ids:
1. Commit your work (safety). `find_pack_path` reads the live `oq-1/sidequest-content`
   (hardcoded `parents[3]`), so a server worktree won't isolate content — you must
   `git checkout <base>` BOTH sidequest-server (merge-base) AND sidequest-content (pre-migration)
   to measure baseline, then restore (trap on EXIT).
2. `pytest --tb=no -q -rf | grep -E '^FAILED|^ERROR' | sed -E 's/ - .*//' | sort` on baseline and on your tree.
3. `comm -23 mine.txt base.txt` = the failures YOU introduced. That's your reconciliation list.

Reconciliation moves: delete tests whose subject is intentionally removed (d20 class-kits →
no Fate analog; mirrors caverns WWN commit 13604312); restore over-removed content (a migration
subagent deleted wry_whimsy's confrontations block — native machinery stays under ADR-144 F5).

## Commit reconciliation edits IMMEDIATELY — subagents can revert uncommitted deletions

A testing-runner told "don't modify files" still ran `git checkout`/`stash` to investigate a
"pre-existing" failure and silently restored a file I had `git rm`'d. The deletion reappeared
in the next full run. Commit (or at least stage-and-verify) destructive reconciliation edits
before spawning any further subagent that might touch git state.

## e2e+OTEL tests flake under pytest-xdist — confirm in isolation before blaming your change

`test_space_opera_melee_e2e::test_melee_resolves_on_hp_depletion_with_otel` appeared as a
"new" failure in one parallel full-suite run but passed serially (`-n0`) twice and touched a
pack I never modified. Run a suspect failure with `-p no:randomly -n0 <nodeid>` before
attributing it to your diff.

## Fix the CLASS, not the cited line — grep-gate doc/string/pattern findings (114-10 rt1→rt3)

A reviewer flagged a docstring over-claiming a "merged" (genre+world) gear set that the
loader never performs. The reject table cited specific line numbers. I fixed exactly those
lines — three round-trips in a row — because each round the reviewer cited the *next* instance
it happened to find, and I fixed only what was cited. An exhaustive `grep -rn "merged"` over
the gear surface on round 3 found **5** instances (2 production docstrings incl. a runtime-visible
exception docstring, 3 test docstrings) — all the same over-claim. Three review cycles for what
was one `grep` + five one-line edits.

**Rule:** when a finding is a *class* (a word/phrase/pattern that's wrong — over-claiming docs,
a renamed term, a deprecated call, a stale path), do NOT fix only the cited line. `grep -rn`
the term across the whole relevant surface, fix every genuine instance in one pass, then re-run
the same grep as an acceptance gate (must return zero). Put the grep command in your assessment
so the reviewer can verify convergence. This applies to both Dev (fix exhaustively) and Reviewer
(cite the class + a grep gate, not a line number — a line-number instruction invites a
line-number fix). Cost of not doing this: N round-trips where N = number of scattered instances.

## "ruff clean" needs `ruff check` AND `ruff format` — format does NOT sort imports (114-10 rt1)

A green-phase Dev Assessment claimed "ruff check + format clean," but a round-trip-1
re-check found a pre-existing `I001` (unsorted imports) on two TEA-authored test files.
Cause: `ruff format` normalizes whitespace/line-wrapping but does NOT reorganize import
blocks — only `ruff check` (with the `I` rule) flags `I001`, and only `ruff check --fix`
sorts them. A `# NEW in 114-10` comment placed BETWEEN import groups is exactly what
isort wants to merge, so any comment-separated import block trips `I001`. Running only
`ruff format` leaves it and you'll falsely report clean. **Before claiming ruff-clean on
your changed files, run BOTH (scoped to your files): `ruff check <files>` then
`ruff format <files>`.** The `--fix` for `I001` keeps a comment attached to its import,
so it lands leading the new symbol — semantically harmless. (The dev-exit gate runs the
check, so an inherited `I001` on a file you touched blocks handoff anyway.)

- **Playtest-hotfix branching: `develop` is protected by a PreToolUse hook, and it blocks
  COMPOUND commands.** Even in the sq-playtest FIXER loop you cannot `git commit` on
  `develop` — the `pf hooks dispatch PreToolUse` guard rejects it ("Cannot commit to
  protected branch 'develop'"). Land fixes via feature branch → push → `gh pr create
  --base develop` → `gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`, then `git pull --ff-only` to
  resync local develop. The DRIVER then pulls develop on the running-stack clone. **Gotcha:
  the hook evaluates the command string against the CURRENT branch BEFORE it runs**, so
  `git checkout -b feat/x && git commit …` is still blocked (it sees `git commit` while
  HEAD is develop). Do the `checkout -b` as its OWN Bash call, then commit in a second call.

- **sq-playtest "DRIVER pulls" means the fix must reach origin/develop.** A pushed feature
  branch is invisible to the DRIVER's `git pull` on develop — so squash-merge to develop is
  mandatory, not optional, for the fix to be testable. UI fixes need only a vite HMR / tab
  reload after the pull; server fixes need a `just server` bounce (the running uvicorn has
  NO `--reload` — confirm with `ps -o command -p <pid>`; a source grep "looks fixed" while
  the process runs stale code). Note in the ping-pong task whether a restart is required.

- **handleMessage stale-closure (sidequest-ui App.tsx).** `handleMessage` is a stable
  `useCallback` with a CURATED deps list (`[connectedPlayerName, appendCachedEvent,
  displayName]`) — volatile state it reads must be bridged through refs ("read
  synchronously inside handleMessage without a re-render"), NOT read as captured state.
  The 2026-06-16 portrait-picker empty-state bug was exactly this: the `pick_portrait`
  fetch gated on `currentGenre`/`currentWorld` read directly from the closure → stale-null
  → no fetch → "No sample portraits" even with 18 on the wire. Fix = `currentGenreRef` /
  `currentWorldRef` synced via a tiny effect, read in handleMessage. When adding any
  state-read to handleMessage, add a ref bridge — do NOT add the state to its deps (that
  re-subscribes / churns the socket callback). Sibling gotcha: a presentational component
  that treats `[]` as "empty" will flash its empty-state during an async fetch window —
  use a `null` loading sentinel (and honor the server's `portraits_available` signal) so
  loading / empty / ready are three distinct states.

### Prove a full-suite failure is PRE-EXISTING before claiming it — base worktree + content symlink (118-5 GREEN, 2026-06-16)
- A full `uv run pytest -q` on a Fate-only story (118-5 compel round-trip) surfaced **85 failures + 1 enum-count failure** that had nothing to do with the diff: the in-flight epic-108 / ADR-143 WWN-combat rework (`committed_blow` unknown-beat in `downed_seam`, `test_102_4_wn_sealed_round`, `test_108_*`) and `test_message_type_complete_count` (57≠56 enum drift). The doctrine is "no pre-existing excuse / suite green at handoff" — but you cannot fix another epic's mid-rework suite from a 5-pt story, and you must NOT just *assert* "pre-existing." **Prove it.**
- **The proof technique:** spin a throwaway worktree at the merge-base and re-run the suspect tests there. `git merge-base HEAD develop` → `git worktree add -q /tmp/sq-base <merge-base-sha>`. Reuse the existing venv so you don't pay `uv sync`: `VIRTUAL_ENV=<repo>/.venv uv run --project <repo> python -m pytest -q <suspect tests>`. If they fail identically on the clean base → pre-existing, full stop. Clean up: `git worktree remove /tmp/sq-base --force && git worktree prune` (run from the repo, not the worktree — `git worktree remove` resets cwd).
- **GOTCHA — the content sibling.** `tests/_helpers/genre_paths.py` sets `_REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]` then `CONTENT_ROOT = _REPO_ROOT / "sidequest-content"`. A `/tmp` worktree's `parents[3]` is `/tmp`, so `GENRE_PACKS_DIR` doesn't exist → every pack-dependent test is **SKIPPED, not run** (you'll see "11 skipped" and wrongly conclude the base is green). Fix: `ln -sfn <real>/sidequest-content /tmp/sidequest-content` so the worktree resolves packs. (Or put the worktree under the oq root so the real sibling resolves.) The enum/protocol tests need no content and run anywhere — use those for the instant proof, the pack-gated ones need the symlink.
- **Cheap pre-check before the worktree:** `{ git diff --name-only; git diff --cached --name-only; git diff <base>...HEAD --name-only; } | sort -u | grep -iE "<suspect subsystem files>"`. If your diff touches none of the failing subsystem's files AND the change is purely additive (new pydantic field w/ default_factory, new model, new method), there is no causal path — the worktree run then just confirms it. Capture the result as a Delivery Finding (Gap, non-blocking, names the owning epic), don't silently move on.
- **Stale sibling test from an earlier story in the same epic:** 118-7 (F3g) changed `FateActionHandler` to BROADCAST the roll via `sd._room` and `return []`, but the 118-6 invoke test's `_fate_session` mock never added `_room` and still asserted `len(out)==1` → it had been red since 118-7 merged. When a test in YOUR path is red because a sibling story moved the contract, repair it to the NEW documented contract (add the `_room` capture double, assert the broadcast + `[]` return) — that's "fix what you see," not scope creep. Distinguish it sharply (in the deviation log) from the separate-epic failures you legitimately can't touch.

## To stop a transport-error handler from swallowing your OWN bugs, drive the async iterator by hand — wrap only `anext()` (119-4 green rework, 2026-06-16)

119-4's narrator loop was `async for message in query(...)` inside one broad
`except Exception` that mapped anything raised to `AgentSdkAuthUnavailable` + a
`narrator.auth_unavailable` event. That catch couldn't tell "the SDK transport
failed (= absent login, OQ-5 → really is auth)" from "our message-processing
raised (a parse `IndexError`)" — both are inside the `async for`, so a parser bug
lit the GM panel red for "login expired." The Reviewer killed it as a dishonest
signal on the lie-detector panel.

**The fix is structural, not a type-narrow.** A `except ClaudeSDKError` narrow
fails the AC1' tests, whose fakes raise a bare `RuntimeError` to mean "the
transport boundary raised." Instead, drive the iterator manually so the catch
wraps ONLY the boundary:

```python
stream = aiter(query(prompt=prompt, options=options))
while True:
    try:
        message = await anext(stream)        # transport boundary ONLY
    except StopAsyncIteration:
        break
    except Exception as exc:                  # transport failed → map to auth
        emit_auth_event(reason="query_raised", ...)
        raise AgentSdkAuthUnavailable("... login absent/expired OR a transport "
                                      "error ...") from exc
    # --- OUR processing, OUTSIDE the catch: its bugs propagate RAW ---
    process(message)                          # IndexError here is NEVER auth
```

- `aiter()`/`anext()` are builtins (≥3.10); the server runs 3.14. An async
  generator is its own iterator, so `aiter(query(...))` then `anext(...)` works
  for the real SDK and the `FakeQuery`/`RaisingFakeQuery` seams alike.
- The boundary catch still maps a transport raise to auth (AC1' wants the typed,
  panel-visible signal), but the HEADLINE must name the alternative ("...or a
  transport error") — never assert a diagnosis the boundary can't prove.
- Verify the wrapping context manager **re-raises** (doesn't suppress): our
