  `llm_request_span` does `except Exception: record; raise`, so an internal error
  escapes cleanly. Check that before relying on propagation.
- Regression matters: the restructure touches the hot narrator path. Full
  `tests/agents/` (1975 tests) stayed green — run the whole blast radius, not just
  the story file, after an iteration-mechanics change.

### Narrate a SERVER-RESOLVED mechanical outcome via `_execute_narration_turn(suppress_intent_router=True)` — never hand-roll `run_narration_turn`+`NarrationMessage` (2026-06-18, 126-8)
- When a handler resolves a mechanical beat server-side and then wants prose (dice replay, a resolved Fate exchange), the canonical seam is the dice precedent: `lore = await session._retrieve_lore_for_turn(sd, replay_text)` → `tc = _build_turn_context(sd, lore_context=lore, room=session._room)` → `return await session._execute_narration_turn(sd, replay_text, tc, suppress_intent_router=True)`. See `handlers/dice_throw.py` (`[BEAT_RESOLVED]`/`[DOGFIGHT_SHOT_RESOLVED]`). `suppress_intent_router=True` skips the pre-narrator router pass (the outcome is already applied — re-classifying is the [COST-1] 8×/turn driver) while keeping husk-reaping, double-narration-prevention, per-peer NARRATION fan-out (via `_emit_event`), and persist-via-emit. A hand-rolled `sd.orchestrator.run_narration_turn(...)` + manual `room.broadcast(NarrationMessage(...))` REINVENTS that seam and silently drops all of it (Don't Reinvent). `_execute_narration_turn` BROADCASTS narration internally and RETURNS frames for the dispatcher — return its result up through `handle`; do NOT also `room.save()` (it persists).
- Lightweight wiring harness trick: when the test session is a `SimpleNamespace` double that can't drive the heavy `_build_turn_context`, extract a THIN session method (e.g. `WebSocketSessionHandler._narrate_resolved_fate_exchange(sd, action)`) wrapping the two calls, and have the handler delegate via `session.<method>(...)`. The test stubs that ONE method (stub boundary sits ABOVE `_build_turn_context`), so the double stays minimal — no enriching sd with 15 fields + genre_pack.{prompts,tropes,...} to satisfy `_build_turn_context` (whack-a-mole). Tradeoff: the wiring test no longer exercises the method body; log a follow-up integration-test finding (the body mirrors the dice precedent, which is covered).

### A `resume`-style "RNG only feeds the NPC" helper that mints its OWN `random.Random()` is non-deterministic in tests — thread an OPTIONAL seeded rng, default-unchanged (2026-06-18, 126-8)
- `resume_fate_exchange` created `random.Random()` internally for the NPC defense roll at RESUME. Migrated tests that assert "the PC's attack takes out the foe" depend on the foe's RESUME-time defense (whether the attack beats it) → ~1–8% flaky across the suite. Fix: `def resume_fate_exchange(..., rng: random.Random | None = None)` → `rng if rng is not None else random.Random()`. Production (the live handler calls it with no rng) is byte-identical; tests pass a seeded/`_FixedRng` so surviving NPC rolls are deterministic. Additive, trivial severity.
- For a depleted/doomed foe the PC must take out regardless of the foe's roll, set the foe's defense to its FLOOR (`_FixedRng(-1)` → 4dF (-1,-1,-1,-1) → defense = skill-4) so any PC attack ≥ skill beats it — robust even when the HANDLER mints its own `random.Random()` for the PROACTIVE roll and you can't seed that.

### A spec-mandated behavior REMOVAL breaks pre-existing sibling tests sharing a dispatch entry — they only surface in `-k <feature>`, not the story's new tests (2026-06-18, 126-8)
- 126-8 changed "a live PC is attacked" from inline-server-rolled-defense → park-at-DEFEND-barrier (`dispatch_fate_action` now returns `awaiting_defense`/`exchange=None` instead of resolving in one call). TEA's RED wrote 28 NEW tests but did NOT migrate the ~10 PRE-EXISTING Fate tests (8 files, back to ADR-144 F2d) that asserted single-call inline resolution through the SAME `dispatch_fate_action`/bank/handler entry. The story's own suite was green; the breakage only showed in `pytest -k fate`. **During GREEN run the feature-wide regression (`-k <feature>`), not just the new test files.** Migration tool: a shared `resolve_parked_defenses(encounter, snapshot, ruleset, target_shift=None, rng=...)` driver in `tests/_helpers/fate_fixtures.py` reads the `encounter.pending_defenses` ledger (works for dispatch- AND bank-level entrypoints), throws a PC defense per entry, then resumes — lets a one-call test reach the SAME resolved state through the two-phase flow. `target_shift=1` calibrates faces to land exactly one shift (`_faces_for_sum(attack_total - target_shift - skill)`); `None` throws max defense (the PC's own attack decides).

### Known PRE-EXISTING failure classes on `feat/126-8-*`/develop (NOT your change) — verify by `git stash`-ing ALL uncommitted work, don't trust testing-runner's root-cause (2026-06-18, 126-8)
- The full server suite shows **88 failures unrelated to any Fate work**: (1) WN combat **beat-pool empty** (`beat id(s) [...] not in pool`, `available: []`) across all WN packs; (2) Fate **SRD reference content not provisioned into test temp fixtures** (`GenreLoadError ... requires reference content under rulesets/fate/srd/`) — the content repo (develop `b1b10b7`) ships Fate SRD at TOP-LEVEL `rulesets/`, but the genre-pack test fixtures don't copy that tier into their temp dirs; (3) one stale `tests/server/test_app.py::test_create_app_uses_build_llm_client_by_default` expecting a `build_async_anthropic` symbol that no longer exists. All content-provisioning/environment, not server dispatch/handler code.
- **The haiku `testing-runner` will confidently MISATTRIBUTE these to your diff** ("your narrator delegation broke the WN beat pool"). It has no causal model. When the failing subsystem has no code path to your change, VERIFY: `git stash push` ALL uncommitted work → run a representative sample on the clean branch HEAD → if they fail identically, they pre-exist → `git stash pop`. Works even when your whole story is uncommitted on a feature branch (you can't diff against develop without losing the prior session's work too). Don't accept a regression claim you can't trace.

### A handler that fills/mutates server state keyed by a client-supplied id MUST authorize the actor owns that id — add the check when you WRITE it (2026-06-18, 126-8 review-rework)
- The 126-8 defend path shipped GREEN, passed review's 4 subagents, and was REJECTED only by the Reviewer's Devil's Advocate: `dispatch_fate_defense` matched the parked `pending_defenses` entry by client-supplied `request_id` and recorded the throw WITHOUT checking `entry.defender == actor_name`. In MP, player A sends `FATE_THROW(defend, request_id=<B's>)` (id is derivable: `def:{round}:{attacker}->{target}`, broadcast to all) → A's dice+skill fill B's defense, B is locked out ("already recorded"). The fix is one guard: `if entry.defender != actor_name: raise FateConflictError(...)`. The tell I should have caught as Dev: the SAME handler 20 lines up already does the ADR-119 `player_id` spoof-rejection — so the per-actor-authorization standard was right there. **Heuristic when writing any handler:** if you look up server state by a client-supplied key (request_id, idempotency_key, beat_id, seat token) and then mutate it on the sender's behalf, add `assert authenticated_actor owns that resource` BEFORE the mutation, and cross-check the sibling auth guards already in the file. "We trust the playgroup" covers PvP *visibility*, not server-side action authorization (which the codebase enforces via ADR-119 regardless).
- **Rework triage:** when a Reviewer finding is a spec INCONSISTENCY (AC-4 "server records a concession" vs AC-6 "no protocol field for it"), don't unilaterally resolve it in a Dev rework — removing the tested-but-unwired code drops AC coverage + breaks TEA's test; inventing the missing protocol field is scope creep / a spec change. Leave it, log a deviation flagging it for SM/Architect, and fix only the findings that are unambiguously yours (the auth guard, the AC-8 role tag). Fix the blocking + cheap-clear-wins; defer spec/doc/test-design calls with a documented deviation.

### A "no player roll on concede" test that counts roll_4df is WRONG — the NPC legitimately rolls at RESUME (126-14)
Counting `fate_resolution.roll_4df == 0` to prove a Fate **concession** didn't roll the player's defense is a false invariant when the harness is `playing_session_with_fate_conflict` (PC proactively attacks an NPC). At RESUME the round walks BOTH commits: the conceding PC's fold (no roll) AND the PC's own proactive attack, against which the NPC server-rolls ITS defense via `ruleset.resolve_action` (`_roll_defense`) — so `roll_4df` is non-zero and the assert fails on correct production. The `parked_conflict` dispatch fixture has ONLY the NPC's attack commit (no PC counter), so there `roll_4df==0` holds — that's why the dispatch-level concede test passes but the handler-level one doesn't. Assert the PLAYER path specifically: spy `FateRulesetModule.resolve_action_from_faces` (the physics-is-the-roll player resolver, role="defense") and assert it's never called; the NPC uses `resolve_action`, a different method. Equivalent observables: no defender `FateRollMessage` broadcast (concede sets `defense_roll=None`), and no `fate.action_resolved` span with `role="defense"` + `source="player_thrown"`.

### sidequest-ui GREEN: run `tsc -b` ALONGSIDE vitest — esbuild won't catch a wrong-shape state access (126-17)
A useStateMirror test that read `result.current.latestFateDefendRequest` instead of `result.current.state.latestFateDefendRequest` failed under `vitest run` as a plain "expected undefined" assertion — looked like a missing production slice. It was a TEST defect: the harness returns `useGameState()` (a `GameStateContextValue` = `{ state, setState, localPlayerId, setLocalPlayerId }`), so every mirror slice lives at `result.current.state.<slice>`, NOT `result.current.<slice>`. `npx tsc -b` pinned it instantly ("Property 'latestFateDefendRequest' does not exist on type 'GameStateContextValue'") while vitest only showed the symptom. **Always run both for a GREEN that touches types** — vitest (esbuild) strips types and reports the downstream symptom; `tsc -b` names the real cause AND is your only regression signal that a shared-type change (here: making `FateThrowPayload.face` optional, widening an action union) didn't break an existing consumer. The fateRoll/fate state-mirror tests are the canonical access pattern to mirror: `result.current.state.latestFateRoll`.

### A required-but-ignored server field still has to be sent (126-17 concede)
`FateThrowPayload.throw_params` is required on the server model (no default) even though the concede branch (`_finish_defense`, "unless they conceded") never replays it. So the UI Concede must synthesize a neutral zero-gesture `{velocity:[0,0,0],angular:[0,0,0],position:[0.5,0.5]}` to pass server validation — omitting it 422s. Tell when a wire model has a required field a given code path ignores: send a neutral value, don't drop the field. The faces, by contrast, ARE omitted on concede (`face?` optional, server validator rejects faces alongside concede=true).

### An "author an ADR" story scoped `repos: server` is secretly TWO repos — ADRs live in the orchestrator, not the subrepo (126-15)
ADRs live ONLY in the orchestrator (`docs/adr/`); subrepos (server/ui/...) have no `docs/adr/`. So a story whose title is "author the X ADR (+ fix citations in N server files)" but whose YAML `repos: server` is mis-scoped — the ADR is an orchestrator artifact, the citation fixes are the subrepo half. Split the landing: **ADR + cross-ref fixes + regenerated indexes → orchestrator `main`** (trunk-based per `.pennyfarthing/repos.yaml`; a direct `docs(<story>): ADR-NNN — …` commit, exactly like ADR-150's `docs(126-12)`; the protected-branch hook does NOT block trunk-based main, and the staged-commit hook validates ADR frontmatter for you). **Citation fixes → the subrepo feature branch** (base `develop`) for the normal review→PR→finish. Log a Delivery Finding so SM finish accounts for BOTH landing points (only the subrepo half needs a PR; the orchestrator commit is already on main). After writing/editing any ADR frontmatter, run `python3 scripts/regenerate_adr_indexes.py` (rewrites `docs/adr/README.md` + the generated block in `CLAUDE.md`; valid primary tags are the `TAG_SECTIONS` keys — `game-systems`, `frontend-protocol`, `observability`, etc. — an unknown tag prints a WARN and lands the ADR in no section).
- Citation-fix mechanics: the wrong pattern was `ADR-148/149` (149 = SRD content, miscited for DEFEND-barrier behavior; correct home is the new ADR-151). `grep` for the standalone wrong ADR FIRST (`grep -rn 'ADR-149' --include='*.py'`) — here it returned nothing, proving every `149` was the `148/149` pairing where 148 (roll source) is correct, so the categorized fix is a uniform `149→151`, not a per-citation split. Watch line-WRAPPED citations (`(ADR-148/` at EOL, `149,` on the next line) — a contiguous `ADR-148/149` sed misses them; fix the continuation line separately and grep `^    149,` to confirm none remain. zsh does NOT word-split unquoted `$FILES` — pass sed file args explicitly or the whole list becomes one bogus path.

### The 108-2 roster reconciliation conscripts an ambient bestiary HAZARD into a non-combat confrontation — gate it on `category == "combat"` (2026-06-19, 150-2 Defect A / wrong-creature opponent)
- Symptom (dust_and_lead Fate playtest): a "standoff" against a human drifter seated a **"Western Diamondback"** rattlesnake as the Other (with a gunfighter FateSheet). Root: `_resolve_opponent_from_roster` (`server/dispatch/encounter_lifecycle.py`) is the 108-2 "reconcile a router-named free-string opponent to a bound statted adversary" path. Its candidate filter is `creature_id is not None AND co-located AND _npc_is_adversary AND not FRIENDLY` — and **every bestiary-materialized creature defaults to disposition −20 (HOSTILE)**, so an ambient hazard snake passes `_npc_is_adversary`. The reconciliation is also INTENTIONALLY name-agnostic ("Hold-Dead" → "Molgrath"), so the snake and a legit Molgrath are **structurally identical** to it — you canNOT discriminate by name/disposition/creature_id without breaking the existing 108-2 tests (`tests/server/test_opponent_roster_resolution.py`, all `encounter_type="combat"`).
- The clean discriminator is the **confrontation category**. The reconciliation's whole purpose is preserving a bound creature's COMBAT hp stats (ADR-059); it can only ever pull a bestiary monster (creature_id-gated), never an authored human NPC. So a bestiary monster is the right Other ONLY for `category == "combat"`. For a non-combat confrontation (`pre_combat` standoff, `social`, `movement`/chase — see `valid_categories` in `genre/models/rules.py`) the Other is a narrative/human NPC, never a bestiary mob. **Fix: pass `confrontation_category=cdef.category` into `_resolve_opponent_from_roster`; after the candidate scan, if `!= "combat"` and candidates exist, emit `encounter.roster_resolution_skipped` (new lie-detector span) and return None** so the seater seats the router-named human threat instead. Combat path untouched → all 108-2 tests stay green. `cdef.category` is in scope at the call site (it's `find_confrontation_def(defs, encounter_type)`).
- SCOPE (Keith, 2026-06-19): this is the SEATER half ONLY ("seater fix first"). It stops the snake; it does NOT make the seated opponent BE the narrated antagonist ("Henry Shaw" — it seats the router's generic string), and it does NOT fix Defect B (namegen `shuffle_fallback` rerouting an Anglo "Henry Shaw" → Ndé "Sees-far" in `narration_apply.py::_resolve_invented_naming_context`). Those + the post-narration identity binding are a tracked FOLLOW-UP. Note the prior "one seam closes both via promote-before-seating" diagnosis is WRONG: confrontation seating runs PRE-narration (only live caller is `agents/subsystems/confrontation.py` via the intent router), so the narrated antagonist doesn't exist yet at seat time — you can't promote it before seating.
- Verification baseline holds: full server suite is **88 pre-existing failures** on clean `develop` (WN beat-pool + Fate SRD-fixture provisioning, per the 126-8 note above). My change: 88→87 (net −1, it fixed one) + 3 new tests. Always `git stash` and re-run to attribute, don't trust raw counts. **Git gotcha this session:** a PreToolUse/auto-mode denial blocks the WHOLE bash call (no part runs), so a `git commit && git push` that's denied leaves NOTHING committed — do NOT then `git reset --hard` assuming the commit landed (it discards the still-uncommitted working-tree edit). Check `git status`/`git log` first.

### The 88 pre-existing failures are now a tracked story (125-8); attribute by error signature, not just count (126-37, 2026-06-20)
The recurring "88 pre-existing failures on clean develop" (noted above for 126-8 / 150-2) was characterized during 126-37's green phase and FILED as tech-debt **story 125-8** (epic 125). They're ALL the WWN/WN-combat de-nativization drift: tests sending combat beats (`committed_blow`/`strike`/`brace`/`break_contact`/`cast_spell`) that content commit `34612bf` (108-3, 2026-06-15) stripped from WWN combat pools — they fail-loud at either `loader.py:900 _validate_class_filter_refs` (PackError "references beat id(s) [...] not in pool") or `dice.py:420` (DiceDispatchError "unknown beat_id ... available: []"). The WN round now supplies the action set (`is_wn_action_beat`/`wn_action_beat`, only `attack` recognized). ~34 files: `test_102_4_wn_*`, `test_106_2_wwn_*`, `test_108_*`, `test_wwn_*`, `test_class_signature_wiring`, `test_cc_chargen_e2e`, `test_dice_path_spell_cast_102_2`, `test_barsoom_cast_beat_live_content`, etc.
- **Triage lesson:** when a green-phase full-suite run shows mass failures, DON'T trust a subagent's "your change exposed a pre-existing bug" inference (testing-runner claimed 126-37's `apply_beat` guard caused the loader failures — impossible: a runtime function can't cause a load-time `PackError`, and the guard is `win_condition=='fate_conflict'`-gated while every failure is a WN/`hp_depletion` path). Attribute by ERROR SIGNATURE + merge-base: the failing code + stale tests predated the branch's merge-base, and `grep`ing the failure set for your story's symbols returned zero. Fate (126-30/126-37) and WN-combat (epic 108) are orthogonal paths — a `fate_conflict` guard cannot break a WWN `hp_depletion` test.
- **Stale-fixture fix pattern (the one cleared under 126-37):** `tests/genre/test_classes_yaml_loader.py` wrote a synthetic `classes.yaml` hardcoding `encounter_beat_choices: [strike,brace,break_contact]`; the loader EXEMPTS an empty list for WN classes (108-7, "the engine owns the combat action set"), so `[]` is the fix — matches the de-nativized real content (whose WN classes carry only chase/negotiation dial beats). Most of 125-8 follows this shape, but the WN-round integration tests (sealed-commit/reprisal/initiative) need the test driven through the synthesized WN action set, not a trivial data swap — coordinate with in-flight epic-108 before sweeping.

### Inventory item id is minted from the NAME, not the narrator's declared id (126-33, 2026-06-20)
`narration_apply.py::_narrator_item_dict(entry)` builds the stored item dict's `id` as `f"narrator:{slug}"` where `slug = name.lower().replace(" ","_").replace("-","_")` — it IGNORES any `entry["id"]` the narrator emitted (the catalog path strips the `narrator:` prefix only to MATCH a CatalogItem, then uses the *authored* id; with no catalog match it bare-mints from the name). Consequence for the gained-lane dedup (126-33): an id-based identity match must include the **entry's raw `entry.get("id")`** in the incoming key set, not just `item_dict["id"]` — otherwise a re-grant that re-references an item by its real id but spells the display name differently (`{"name":"the silver slippers","id":"narrator:silver_shoes"}`) mints `narrator:the_silver_slippers` and never matches the held `narrator:silver_shoes`. The dedup keys I used: `{item_dict.id, item_dict.name, entry.id}` (all strip+casefold, empties dropped) vs each existing entry's `{id, name}`; match on non-empty set intersection. Items "arrive as quantity=1 singletons" today, so a re-narration dup is a no-op (quantity untouched), not a quantity-merge — mirrors the 45-13 container-retrieval block. Made it observable with `_watcher_publish("item_gain.deduped", {...}, component="inventory", severity="warning")` (parity with `container_retrieval_blocked` — a narrator-emitted known-bad dup is a python.md #4 client-error path). `Inventory.items` is `list[dict]` (`creature_core.py:98`).

### `PgSaveRepository.for_slug` is a WRITE — it bumps `last_played` on every call, even from a "read" endpoint (126-34, 2026-06-20)
`PgSaveRepository.for_slug(pool, slug=…, mode=…, genre=…, world=…)` calls `sessions.ensure_session` (`save_repository.py:108`), whose `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (session_slug) DO UPDATE SET last_played = excluded.last_played` rewrites `last_played` to `now()` for an EXISTING row. `last_activity_ts` (the GM-dashboard auto-follow sort key) is derived from `last_played` (`forensic.py::list_saves`, `int(datetime.fromisoformat(last_played).timestamp()*1000)`). So `GET /api/debug/state` — a "read" — was bumping every enumerated session's timestamp on every poll, floating idle/test sessions to the top of auto-follow purely because the operator's panel polled them. **Rule: never route a read-only path through `for_slug`.** To bind a repo for reads without the bump: `sid = sessions.resolve_session_id(pool, slug=slug); if sid is None: continue; repo = PgSaveRepository(pool, session_id=sid)`. The direct constructor (`__init__(self, pool, *, session_id)`, `save_repository.py:18`) takes NO mode/genre/world and runs no upsert. The dropped `GameMode(game.mode)` parse a read used to do is pure win — reads don't need `mode`, so a malformed mode can no longer affect the projection. `for_slug` stays correct for the actual save/connect paths (it's *meant* to create-if-absent there).

### `publish_event` envelope fields that ride the broadcast but are NOT persisted (126-34)
`watcher_hub.publish_event` builds the event dict with `session_slug` at the TOP LEVEL (not in `fields`) precisely because `_persist_turn_telemetry` serializes only `fields` — so top-level envelope keys reach the GM panel over the WebSocket but never hit `turn_telemetry`. To add another broadcast-only marker (here: `session_type="test"` when `is_test_session(slug)`), put it at the top level next to `session_slug`, conditionally (omit for real sessions so `.get("session_type") != "test"` holds). Pattern for any "tag the live stream without write-amplifying the DB" need. `is_test_session(slug)` = `slug.startswith(("test-","tool-test"))` (None → False); mirrored verbatim in the UI's `isTestSession` (`useLiveSource.ts`) since the two repos can't share code — keep them in sync.

### A full-suite "regression" verdict is worthless without a baseline — stash and re-run (153-1)
After the 153-1 output-slim GREEN, the full server suite showed "41 failed" and the testing-runner declared CRITICAL FAILURE / 41 regressions. It was wrong: those tests were ALREADY failing on `develop`. The proof is a controlled experiment, not a guess — GREEN source edits were still uncommitted, so `git stash push <the 5 source files>` reverted them, re-running the failing set gave the IDENTICAL count (40 failed / 56 passed base == 40 failed / 56 passed mine), then `git stash pop`. Zero regressions added. As of 2026-06-20 `develop` carries ~40 pre-existing failures concentrated in integration tests (chargen e2e `choices: [None,None,...]`, `test_app` monkeypatching a non-existent `llm_factory.build_async_anthropic`, wwn spell-cast dispatch, lore-link/class-signature wiring) — almost certainly missing local services/DB, NOT contract breakage. NEVER accept a subagent's "N regressions" without establishing the base: stash the (still-uncommitted) change, run the exact failing node-ids, diff the counts. The far-from-your-diff failures (`test_app`, `test_watcher_events`) are the tell that it's a shared-fixture/environment issue, not your change.

### Selecting parametrized tests by node-id breaks on spaces in the id; zsh won't word-split `$var` either (153-1)
Two harness traps when verifying a subset: (1) `pytest $(cat ids.txt | tr '\n' ' ')` splits `...prose_is_pronoun_agnostic[Warrior-Killing Blow]` at the space inside the brackets into two bogus args -> "no tests ran"/silent miss. Pass FILES not node-ids, or `-k`-filter, when ids contain spaces. (2) The shell here is zsh: `FILES="a.py b.py"; ruff check $FILES` passes the whole string as ONE path (`E902 No such file`). zsh does not word-split unquoted parameters — use `${=FILES}`, an array, or inline the paths.

### pydantic `@model_validator(mode="before")` fires on kwargs too — a strip-deprecated validator tolerates BOTH `model_validate(dict)` and `Model(field=...)` (153-1)
When you remove a field under `extra="forbid"` but Haiku may still emit it, a `@model_validator(mode="before")` that pops the dead key makes the package survive instead of being rejected (mirror `_coerce_stringified_lists`). Non-obvious payoff: mode="before" receives the raw input whether validation was entered via `Model.model_validate({...})` OR `Model(resolved=[...])` kwargs — so old-style constructions in un-migrated callers get the key SILENTLY STRIPPED (and logged), not an error. That's why removing `PlayerDispatch.resolved/lethality` didn't blow up callers that still pass them. Log the drop (No Silent Fallbacks) so it's visible on the GM panel.

### Adding a field to TropeDefinition? `resolve.py::_merge_trope` will silently drop it (157-5)
`_merge_trope` (genre/resolve.py) rebuilds the merged trope via an EXPLICIT dict and `TropeDefinition.model_validate(data)` — any field not listed in that dict is reset to its default for every `extends:` trope. 157-4 added `factions` to the model + the Seam-3 gate but missed this call-site, so the three gulliver tropes that `extends` genre parents silently lost their zone scope (`[]` = permissive → bleed into every zone). This is a recurring half-wiring trap: model field + one consumer is NOT enough — grep for `model_validate(data` / explicit-dict rebuilders (resolve.py, any *_merge/*_resolve) when adding a TropeDefinition/SeedTrope/Bestiary field. Fix used the same child-overrides/empty-inherits semantics as `tags`/`triggers`. Guard it with a merge test (`tests/genre/test_resolve.py`) — the field-drop has no other tripwire.

### Proving content tags actually drive an engine filter: load the REAL pack, run the real predicate (157-5)
For a content-tagging story whose acceptance is "verify no bleed", the honest proof is a server test that `load_genre_pack(<content path>)` the REAL world (pattern: `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "sidequest-content/genre_packs/<pack>"`) and drives the real predicate (`zone_eligibility.is_eligible`/`world_is_zoned`) against the authored tags — NOT a synthetic fixture (that only re-tests the engine seam) and NOT a YAML-field-presence assert (existence != wiring). `world.tropes` is the RESOLVED (post-inheritance) list the Seam-3 gate reads, so testing it also catches merge-drop regressions. See `tests/genre/test_157_5_gulliver_no_bleed.py`.

### develop has a RED `tsc -b` from a stale fate-tab test — stash-test before blaming your branch (153-25, 2026-06-21)
`sidequest-ui` `npm run build`/`tsc -b` errors at `src/components/GameBoard/__tests__/GameBoard-fate-inventory-tab.test.tsx:203` — `new Set([..., "fate"])` but `"fate"` was removed from `WidgetId` (story 126-26 deleted the standalone Fate tab). It is PRE-EXISTING on the branch, not introduced by your story. Confirm any surprise `tsc -b` error is pre-existing with a targeted stash test: `git stash push -u -- <your files> && npx tsc -b 2>&1 | grep 'error TS' && git stash pop`. If the error persists with your changes stashed, it's not yours — log it as a Delivery Finding, don't fix it inside an unrelated story (scope creep). Vitest (esbuild) does NOT typecheck, so green tests can coexist with a red `tsc -b`.

### The SERVER suite also carries a large pre-existing red baseline — prove isolation WITHOUT git stash, then focused-handoff (153-9, 2026-06-21)
A broad `uv run pytest` on `sidequest-server` returned **37 failures / 13743 passed** on a branch whose only diff was one file (`encounter_lifecycle.py`). The 37 span unrelated subsystems (chargen wiring, lore RAG, spellcasting payloads, premise loader, encountergen/pregen bestiary, app LLM-default, WWN-content beat de-nativization) — a real develop-baseline red from recent big merges (e.g. epic-157 `0f199b0f`/#1002 added `pack.effective_bestiary(world)` at `pregen.py:494` and missed several `_stub_pack` SimpleNamespaces; CAC's `strike` beat assertion in `test_sealed_letter_dispatch_integration.py` is stale post-WWN-de-nativization → `cdef.beats == set()`).
- **Prove your change is clean WITHOUT `git stash` (stash is forbidden here — see tea-gotchas).** Three stash-free signals, cheapest first: (1) `git diff --stat` → diff isolated to N files; (2) run the *targeted* suite that actually exercises your change (here the 22 roster-resolution + fate-seating tests) → all green; (3) confirm every full-suite failure lives in a test file that does NOT import/drive your changed symbol. A read-only `git worktree add /tmp/x origin/develop` is the gold-standard baseline, but `uv sync` in a fresh worktree may not resolve git/path deps (`claude_agent_sdk` was missing) — don't burn cycles fighting it if signals 1–3 already settle it.
- **`testing-runner` (haiku) mis-attributes failures — get the real traceback for anything you'll act on.** It reported all 5 dispatch failures as `effective_bestiary` AttributeErrors; one was actually a `strike`-beat assertion (`cdef.beats == set()`). Run the single suspicious test yourself (`-n0`, full traceback) before concluding a root cause or "fixing" the wrong thing.
- **Do NOT fix unrelated pre-existing reds inside your story** — even trivial ones (I started adding the 1-line `effective_bestiary` stub to 3 epic-157 test files, then reverted: it doesn't get the full-suite gate green anyway since other causes remain, and it muddies a focused bugfix PR). Log a single blocking Delivery Finding enumerating the causes; when the full-suite gate will block, **surface to the Operator** (AskUserQuestion) rather than silently absorbing 37 cross-epic fixes. Operator chose focused-handoff. See [[story_153_9_fate_other_seating]].

### Fate other-seating fix shape: the 108-2 reconciliation is category-gated, not ruleset-gated (153-9, 2026-06-21)
`_resolve_opponent_from_roster` (`encounter_lifecycle.py`) is called at the `materialized_threat` seam BEFORE `is_fate` is computed downstream (was line 1671), and its only decline gate was `confrontation_category != "combat"` (the 150-2 non-combat decline). So under a Fate **combat** confrontation it conscripted any co-located `creature_id`-statted adversary over the router-named scene-active antagonist — the [FATE-OTHER-SEATING] bug. Minimal fix (mirrors the 150-2 sibling): resolve `is_fate` ONCE at the seating seam, pass it into the helper, extend the gate to `confrontation_category != "combat" or is_fate`, and reuse the existing `encounter.roster_resolution_skipped` span with a `reason` attr (`fate_binding`/`non_combat`). Do NOT build a new "scene-active antagonist marker" (the story's root-cause framing oversized it) — once conscription is declined the router-named threat IS seated directly. Keep native (non-Fate) combat reconciliation untouched (ADR-059). Bonus: the duplicate `is_fate = bool(pack and pack.rules and pack.rules.ruleset == "fate")` at the 126-30 de-nativization branch folds into the single resolution.

### A new `RulesConfig` `model_validator` fans out to EVERY config construction — prove isolation by loading all packs + the ruleset/chargen sweep (153-34, 2026-06-21)
Adding `RulesConfig._validate_default_spread_covers_abilities` (size-validate the WN default standard-array spread vs ability count when no array is authored) fires on every `RulesConfig(...)` / `model_validate(...)` across runtime AND the test suite. Two verification levers that settle "did I break a fixture": (1) load all 11 live packs through the real `load_genre_pack` in a one-liner (`for d in GENRE_PACKS_DIR: load_genre_pack(d)`) → `OK=11 FAIL=0`; (2) run `tests/game/ruleset/` + builder stat/walk + `test_153_4_swn_chargen_spread_wiring.py` → 641 green. Watch-outs that did NOT bite but you must check: `test_loader_binding::test_unknown_ruleset_rejected_at_bind` constructs `RulesConfig(ruleset="nonsense")` and must NOT raise — safe only because my validator early-returns on empty `ability_score_names` (it never calls `get_ruleset_module`, so it can't raise UnknownRulesetError at construction). Implementation notes: gate `point_buy` to WN-family only (`_WITHOUT_NUMBER_RULESETS`, since only WN supersedes point-buy with the fixed spread — dial point-buy generates per-ability), but `standard_array` for ALL rulesets (base `_DEFAULT_STANDARD_ARRAY`); local-import the two length constants inside the method to dodge the genre.models↔game.ruleset cycle (mirrors loader.py). The 4 `tests/genre/` reds (`test_premise_loader` ×2, `test_beneath_sunden_room_binding_107_2`, `test_wwn_spell_catalog_load`) are the KNOWN develop baseline red (fate-SRD-reference-content / clone state) flagged in the 153-9 entry above — confirmed identical with changes stashed, NOT yours. See [[story_153_34_wn_pointbuy_dead_config]].

### Authoring SWN/WWN chargen content (backgrounds/foci/skills): the seams, the DRY win, and the max-of collision (153-33, 2026-06-21)
- **Where the catalogs live + how they resolve:** genre-tier `backgrounds.yaml`/`foci.yaml`/`skills.yaml` at the pack root (`genre_packs/<pack>/`). Worlds that ship their own override genre wholesale (no merge — `chargen_defs_resolve.resolve_backgrounds/resolve_foci`, world-first). So a single genre-tier file must define EVERY `background:`/`focus_id` tag referenced across ALL the pack's world `char_creation.yaml` scenes. space_opera's 3 worlds reference 11 distinct background ids (Void-born shared) → 11 genre-tier backgrounds, not the "4-6" the story AC guessed. Skills are ALWAYS genre-tier (no world skills.yaml).
- **The DRY seam for foci:** the `crucible` (vocation/"What did you do?") scene is IDENTICAL across space_opera's 3 worlds (same 5 class_hints Officer/Engineer/Pilot/Smuggler/Diplomat). Attach `focus_id` there → author 5 foci once, wire identically in all 3 worlds. Backgrounds (origins scene) carry the per-world flavor divergence; foci (crucible) stay uniform. The story context's "archetype scene references focus IDs" = the crucible.
- **ADR-095 (foci abilities ≠ class signature abilities):** space_opera's class abilities (`classes.yaml`) are ALL confrontation-beat abilities (Call the Shot, Reroute Power, Threading the Needle, Exit Plan, Find Common Ground). Keep focus abilities OUT-of-combat knacks (Pull Rank, Make It Hold, Know the Lanes, Know a Guy, Read the Room) — exploration/downtime/social. This both satisfies ADR-095 and fits SWN's focus model (skill + non-combat talent), and dodges the "Bind the Ruleset, don't balance it" trap (don't add new combat-beat mechanics).
- **THE COLLISION that broke a RED test:** a background grants its skills at a FLOOR (SRD §1.3 → level 0); a focus grants its level-1 skills. The builder merges background ∪ focus ∪ scene with **max-of**. So if a background and the chosen focus grant the SAME skill, the sheet shows the focus's higher level. Concrete: perseus_cloud choice-0 = Regency-raised background (Lead:0) + Officer crucible → chain-of-command focus (Lead:1) → sheet shows **Lead:1**. A test asserting `char.skills[skill] == background_floor` FAILS on that collision. Correct assertion: `char.skills.get(skill, -1) >= floor` (landed at ≥ its floor; a focus may raise it, never lower). Don't "fix" this by contorting content to avoid the skill overlap — the overlap is realistic and the next background/focus pair will collide again; fix the assertion. The foci test in the same file already used `>=` — match it.
- **Count guidance:** live-pack norm is 5-6 foci (heavy_metal 5, caverns 6), 1 focus granted per character (SRD-faithful). Don't author more foci than scene choices wire them — unreferenced foci are dead content (progression.yaml grants no foci, so there's no advancement consumer). See [[story_153_33_swn_chargen_backgrounds_foci]].

### Wiring a new IntentRouter dispatch subsystem (dogfight, 153-6 — sibling of 153-5 course): 5 touch-points + a typed-span pyright trap (2026-06-22)
- **A new dispatch subsystem is exactly 5 edits** (proven across course/quest_offer/dogfight): (1) `agents/subsystems/<name>.py` exporting `run_<name>_dispatch`; (2) register it in `subsystems/__init__.py:_register_defaults()` (BOTH the import inside the function AND the registry tuple — the import is intentionally lazy/inside-function to dodge the circular import, since the handler does `from sidequest.agents.subsystems import SubsystemOutput`); (3) a dispatch span pair in `telemetry/spans/<component>.py` (accepted + `.rejected`, with `SPAN_ROUTES` entries — `from .<component> import *` in `spans/__init__.py` re-exports them, no `__all__` edit needed); (4) a witness in `dispatch_engagement_watcher.py` (`_WITNESSES` + `_DISPATCHED_TYPE_KEY`); (5) the dispatch key + params in the `intent_router.py` system prompt. Miss #5 and the registry/handler are perfect but the router never emits the dispatch in production (unit tests stub the router, so they stay green — it's a playtest-only gap; "verify wiring, not existence").
- **A "dogfight" is NOT a new engine — it's a `ConfrontationDef` with `resolution_mode: sealed_letter_lookup`.** The handler is a THIN wrapper over the SAME `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger(encounter_type=<dogfight type>, …)` that `run_confrontation_dispatch` calls — copy confrontation.py's opponent-materialization block verbatim (`params["opponent"] or params["threat"]` → `NpcMention(role="hostile", side="opponent")`, ADR-116). Do NOT reimplement seating. Resolve the dogfight type by scanning `pack.rules.confrontations` for `resolution_mode == ResolutionMode.sealed_letter_lookup and category == "combat"` so the router needn't disambiguate it from a `beat_selection` `ship_combat` type in the same pack.
- **Anti-illusionism in the handler itself:** after `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger`, VERIFY `snapshot.encounter` is the live encounter you intended (`encounter_type == enc_type and not resolved`) BEFORE emitting the success span — a `dogfight.dispatch` span with no encounter behind it is the exact convincing-prose-no-backing failure the lie-detector exists to catch. On any seat failure (`NoOpponentAvailableError`/`SealedLetterArityError`, no type, not-seated) emit `<component>.dispatch.rejected` with a reason and return `SubsystemOutput(data={"error": …})` — never a silent success (AC "No Silent Fallbacks").
- **pyright trap with typed span params:** `threat.get("name")` (dict.get) is `Any | None`, so `threat_name = threat.get("name") if isinstance(threat, dict) else str(threat)` widens `threat_name` to `str | None` and FAILS pyright when passed to a span emitter typed `opponent: str`. confrontation.py gets away with it because it never passes the name to a typed `str` param. Coerce: `raw = threat.get("name") if isinstance(threat, dict) else threat; threat_name = str(raw) if raw else ""`. Run `uv run pyright <file>` on new handlers — `just server-test` passing does not catch this. See [[story_153_6_dogfight_dispatch_unreachable]].

### Chargen "silently rejects my name answer" = an UNCONDITIONAL hook_prompt re-prompt, not a parser bug (153-16 CHARGEN-NAME-PROSE-REJECT, 2026-06-22)
- **The finding read backwards from the code.** Playtest said the road_warrior `the_name` prose answer ("They call me Riggs. The rig's name is The Anvil — …") was rejected while a terse form parsed. On current develop the OPPOSITE is true: `extract_freeform_names` parses the prose to `('Riggs','The Anvil')` fine, and the terse "Riggs and his rig The Anvil" returns `(None,None)`. The parser (#705, 2026-06-05) was never the bug. ALWAYS reproduce a chargen-parse finding against current `extract_freeform_names` before touching patterns — a stale running clone or a misread of the UX flow can invert the symptom.
- **The real bug is in `apply_freeform` (builder.py ~2399):** any freeform scene with a `hook_prompt` transitions to `AwaitingFollowup` UNCONDITIONALLY. On a NON-name scene the hook_prompt is a WOUND/elaboration hook (correct to always fire). On the NAME scene it's a name-CORRECTION re-prompt (the pack-authored "…Both matter." text) — so a perfectly-parsed answer STILL got re-prompted, which the player reads as silent rejection. The re-prompt string is pack YAML (`char_creation.yaml` `hook_prompt`), NOT server source — grepping server code for it finds nothing.
- **Fix = gate the name-scene re-prompt on parse success:** re-prompt only when `extract_freeform_names` pulled NO name; otherwise clear the followup and advance. Only `road_warrior/the_name` is affected — it's the ONLY pack whose TERMINAL scene is a name scene (`_is_name_scene` = last scene + no choices + `allows_freeform: true`) AND carries a `hook_prompt`; every sibling ends on a no-choice DISPLAY scene (`allows_freeform: false`), which `_is_name_scene` excludes. Vessel/rig half stays optional (the existing `vessel_name()`-is-None-when-absent contract) — a name-only answer is accepted, not nagged.
- **Tests that pinned the OLD buggy behavior had to be REWRITTEN, not just added:** `test_builder_name_extraction.py::TestNameSceneFollowupCorrection` and `test_chargen_name_rig_extraction.py::test_extraction_decisions_are_observable` fed a PARSEABLE first answer then asserted `is_awaiting_followup()` / the `chargen.name_followup_correction` span. Under the gate a parseable answer no longer opens the followup — re-point those to an UNPARSEABLE (or vessel-only) first answer so the correction path genuinely fires. New `chargen.name_reprompt_decision` OTEL span (reason `name_accepted`/`name_unparsed`) is the GM-panel signal.
- **pyright trap:** gating via a `wants_followup` BOOL breaks narrowing — `AwaitingFollowup(hook_prompt=scene.hook_prompt)` then sees `str | None`. Carry a `followup_prompt: str | None` (set to `None` on accept) and gate `if followup_prompt is not None:` so it narrows to `str`. See [[story_153_16_chargen_name_prose_reject]].

### Full server suite is RED at the develop baseline — Fate-pack-loader rejects beat_selection (153-12, 2026-06-22)
`uv run pytest` reports ~156 failures (91 failed + 65 error) that are NOT your regression: `sidequest/genre/loader.py:301` raises `ValidationError: Fate-bound pack authors native-dial confrontation(s) [...beat_selection]` for `pulp_noir`/`spaghetti_western`/`wry_whimsy` (ADR-144 — a Fate pack must use `contest`/`conflict`/`table_resolution`/`sealed_letter_lookup`, never the d20 dial default `beat_selection`). The epic-157 zoned-world tests (`test_157_*_gulliver/oz/wonderland_no_bleed`) cascade from the same pack-load failure (0.02s collection error). **This blocks `gates/tests-pass` (full-suite green) for ANY story on this baseline.** Before assuming you broke something: (1) run only your blast radius + new tests — if those are green you're clean; (2) `git stash push -- <your file>` and rerun a sample of the failures — identical failures = pre-existing. The Fate packs author confrontations with NO explicit `resolution_mode` (schema-defaults to `beat_selection`); the fix is content-side (epic-144/157), not server. Note: `sidequest-content` in this clone was also parked on a stray feature branch (`feat/153-33-...`) — a cross-clone artifact — but 153-33 is space_opera-only so it does NOT cause the Fate failures; the drift is on `develop` itself.

### Root-cause NPC/state-seeding bugs by querying the live save DB, not by tracing seed code (158-11, 2026-06-24)
Harmund Fuel-Count seeded `manual_origin=False`/`location=None` while his 3 Ropefoot camp siblings seeded authored. I burned several turns theorizing about which seed path (history-chapter seeder? opening present_npcs? preload?) stamped the siblings — all wrong. The fast, certain move (Keith's nudge: "the saves should still be extant in the database"): query Postgres directly.
```bash
psql -d sidequest -t -A -c "SELECT s.session_id,s.session_slug FROM sessions s JOIN game_state g ON g.session_id=s.session_id WHERE s.world_slug='<world>' AND g.snapshot_json LIKE '%<NpcName>%' ORDER BY s.session_id DESC LIMIT 10;"
# then dump snapshot_json for the session and json.load → inspect npcs[].{manual_origin,location,disposition}
```
Ground truth (save 16097) showed ALL 16 npcs had `manual_origin=True`+location except Harmund → instantly localized to the per-turn inject, not the chargen preload. `SIDEQUEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://$USER@localhost:5432/sidequest` (the real DB, not `_test`). Measure, don't assert.

### The available-NPC inject cap silently drops PLACED authored NPCs beyond 3 (158-11)
`monster_manual_inject._npc_patches_for_available_humans` sliced `available_at_location(...)[:_AVAILABLE_NPC_INJECT_LIMIT]` (=3). The list is placed-matching first, then unplaced — so a location with **>3 location_tagged authored NPCs** lost its tail every turn. Dropped NPCs never get `_human_patch(manual_origin=True, location=…)`, so they sit at `preload_authored_npcs`'s default `manual_origin=False`/`location=None`. Fix: surface ALL placed uncapped; cap only unplaced walk-ons. Same bug the oz road's "4 companions → 3" docstring described — that earlier work only made the drop OBSERVABLE (`available_placed_dropped` span) without fixing it. The injection span carries `available_placed_dropped`; it should read 0 now (regression tripwire). The owning test `test_inject_span_reports_placed_dropped_by_cap` PINNED the bug — had to be rewritten, not just supplemented.

### Pre-existing full-suite red set shifted to ~10 WWN-dispatch/mutation/seam tests (158-19, 2026-06-24)
The ~156 Fate-pack-loader failures (153-12 note above) appear resolved on the current `develop` baseline. The pre-existing red set is now ~10: `test_wwn_*_dispatch` (caverns/heavy_metal/elemental_harmony cast_spell), `test_seam_crossing_wiring`, `test_wwn_scene_harness_fixture_proof`, `test_use_mutation_tool::test_refusal_payload_round_trips`, `test_102_7_mutant_wasteland_mutations_live`, `test_103_10_seaboard_e2e`. Verified pre-existing: `git stash` BOTH repos (server + content), run them `-n0` (serial) on the clean tree → 6 reproduce; the rest are parallel-run Postgres-pool teardown flakiness (`pool 'sidequest-save' is already closed`). None touch dungeon/quest/theme code. **Same drill as before:** if your blast-radius suite + new tests are green and these are unchanged with your work stashed, you're clean — don't chase them. Scope the green gate to your domain suite (`tests/dungeon/` here), not the full suite.

### Content depth_band changes can surface latent theme×look materialization gaps as flaky session tests (158-19, 2026-06-24)
Flattening beneath_sunden theme `depth_band`s to `{0,null}` (random-dungeon eligibility) made `sunless_temple` (built/roomcorridor) shallow-eligible, and the materializer fail-loud'd at the look→theme seam (`_resolve_look_for_theme`) because the cookbook has no roomcorridor look AND `KNOWN_GENERATOR_BINDINGS` (cookbook/loader.py) excludes roomcorridor. Symptom = seed-dependent flaky reds in `test_session_*`/`test_region_projection_*` (different set each run). **Lesson:** when a content change widens which themes are reachable, run the FULL dungeon suite a few times (seed variance) — a green single run can hide it. The fix was content (GM re-themed to depthfirst), not code — but the latent server gap (roomcorridor unsupported here) is logged as a delivery finding. See [[gm-gotchas]] "Content is VALIDATED, not unit-tested".

### IntentRouter dispatch repairs go in run_dispatch_bank; degrade combat-seat failures with a typed ValueError subclass (2026-06-25, 158-28)
- The IntentRouter (Haiku LLM) intermittently emits `subsystem="<confrontation type>"` (e.g. `subsystem="combat"`) instead of `subsystem="confrontation"` with `params.type="combat"`. `combat` is NOT a registered subsystem, so `run_dispatch_bank` silently dropped it (unknown_subsystem → continue) and combat never seated. The robust deterministic fix is a normalization pass in `run_dispatch_bank` BEFORE topo-sort: if a dispatch's subsystem is unregistered AND matches a pack confrontation type (`pack.rules.confrontations[].type`, reachable via `context["pack"]`), rewrite it to `subsystem="confrontation"`, `params.setdefault("type", original)`. Make it LOUD (No Silent Fallbacks): log it + stamp `normalized_from` on the EXISTING `intent_router.subsystem` span (don't author a new span — one mechanism per problem). The router-prompt fix is the complement but is live-eval only (the deterministic suite stubs the router).
- `run_confrontation_dispatch` only catches `NoOpponentAvailableError`/`SealedLetterArityError` (both `ValueError` subclasses). To degrade a NEW combat-seat failure loudly (ADR-006) without wedging the turn, add a typed `ValueError` subclass (e.g. `InitiativeUnresolvableError`) at the raise site, catch it in the handler, and return `SubsystemOutput(data={"error": "<code>"})` — the bank stamps that error code on the sub-span automatically (lines ~405-408), so you get GM-panel observability for free. Keep genuine config/roster defects (e.g. player-not-found-in-roster) as fail-loud bare `ValueError` — don't blanket-catch `ValueError` or you'll swallow the `unknown encounter_type` config error the seater is documented to propagate.
- The seat (`snapshot.encounter = enc`) lands at `encounter_lifecycle.py:2083`, BEFORE `_roll_and_persist_initiative` (~2164). So an initiative failure leaves a SEATED encounter — degrade (keep the seat), don't unseat.
- Verifying "pre-existing failures": don't trust a subagent's "I stashed and re-ran" claim on a load-bearing regression call. `git stash push <specific files>` → run the suspect tests → `git stash pop`. 158-28 had 49 full-suite failures that were all pre-existing spell-cast/bestiary bleed (158-33), confirmed by stash.

### Content portrait renders resolve the catalog from the daemon's served clone, not your authoring clone (160-2, 2026-06-27)
`scripts/generate_portrait_images.py` composes the *subject* from the clone you run it in, but the running media daemon resolves the `npc:<slug>` catalog from **its own** `SIDEQUEST_GENRE_PACKS` — which may be a *different clone* (here the daemon served `/Users/slabgorb/Projects/oq-3/sidequest-content`, while work was authored in oq-1). `_get_composer` → `CharacterCatalog.load(packs_root, ...)` (zimage_mlx_worker.py:78) reads the world's `portrait_manifest.yaml` **fresh per request** from that served clone, so newly-authored entries render as `COMPOSE_FAILED / CatalogMissError: CharacterCatalog has no entry for 'npc:companion_cat'` even though your manifest, sidequest-validate, and the dry-run all look perfect.
- **Diagnose:** `ps eww -p $(pgrep -f sidequest-renderer) | tr ' ' '\n' | grep SIDEQUEST_GENRE_PACKS` to see which clone the daemon serves. (That dump also exposes secrets — don't paste it back.)
- **Per-request, not cached:** the catalog is loaded fresh each render, so you do NOT need to restart the daemon — just make the served clone's manifest contain the entries.
- **Fix used (operator-authorized):** for each world, `cp` oq-1's `portrait_manifest.yaml` → oq-3 (additive; verify `oq1 HEAD == oq3 HEAD` for the file first so it's clean), render, then `git -C oq3 checkout -- <file>` to restore. Guard with a dirty-check so you never clobber uncommitted oq-3 work. The R2 upload + `r2_manifest.json` rebuild happen in the clone you RUN the script from (oq-1), so the manifest lands on your branch correctly.
- **Alternative:** repoint/restart the daemon at your dev clone (heavier; one config change vs. N transient syncs).
- **Skip logic works once assets are on R2:** existing portraits `SKIP (already exists: R2)`; only the new `companion_*` keys actually render (~168s each on this Z-Image daemon). Reaching into another clone's working tree is permission-gated — the auto classifier denies the `cp` until the user explicitly authorizes it.

### Narrator-prose bugs are fixed & verified at the PROMPT-PRECONDITION layer, not by replaying the LLM (158-35, 2026-06-27)
The dogfight dice-replay re-entry re-emitted the prior turn's prose (a sensor sweep) instead of narrating the resolved gun pass (coyote_star 2026-06-25). You can't unit-test "the LLM stopped winging it" — the project's established method (see `tests/integration/test_glenross_replay_recency_window.py`, and the new `tests/agents/test_dogfight_shot_resolved_directive.py`) is to assert the *prompt the narrator receives* carries the right preconditions: "if the cue is absent from the high-attention zone, no narrator — even a good one — has the visible context to refuse the bug." So: drive `Orchestrator.build_narrator_prompt(action, ctx)`, fetch the section by name from the returned `registry`, assert on its content/zone. The "live repro" the story asked for = the documented playtest forensics, not a fresh LLM run.

### Root cause of the dogfight re-entry: a mechanical replay tag framed as PC speech (158-35)
`build_narrator_prompt`'s default action framing is `f"{character_name} says: {action}"` (orchestrator.py, ~line 3214, in the `if merged_player_actions / elif opening_seed_shown / else` chain). The dogfight dice re-entry passes `action="[DOGFIGHT_SHOT_RESOLVED] Your laser: HIT, …"` (built in `handlers/dice_throw.py`), so it became `"Apex says: [DOGFIGHT_SHOT_RESOLVED] …"` — a mechanical tag as dialogue the narrator can't speak, so it anchored to the load-bearing Recency window (`recent_narrative_context`, 49-1) and re-narrated the prior scene. Fix = a new `elif action.startswith("[DOGFIGHT_SHOT_RESOLVED]")` branch reframing it as a resolved-beat directive ("narrate THIS gun pass; do NOT restate the prior scene"), mirroring the existing `opening_seed_shown` branch. **Do NOT strip the Recency window** — it's continuity-critical (49-1 gender-flip/dropped-prop regressions); only change the ACTION framing. Note: the normal-combat `[BEAT_RESOLVED]` re-entry has the same latent "says: [tag]" framing but partly dodges it by prepending `PLAYER_ACTION:` (see `server/dispatch/dice.py::_format_replay_action`); it was left out of 158-35's scope (filed as a finding).

### Adding a dogfight OTEL span: SPAN_ROUTES entry + @contextmanager, auto-exported (158-35)
To add a GM-panel lie-detector span (e.g. `dogfight.shot_narration_replay`): in `sidequest/telemetry/spans/dogfight.py` add a `SPAN_<NAME>` const + `SPAN_ROUTES[...] = SpanRoute(event_type="state_transition", component="dogfight", extract=lambda span: {...})` and a `@contextmanager def <name>_span(*, ..., _tracer=None, **attrs)` using `Span.open(...)`. `spans/__init__.py` does `from .dogfight import *` and dogfight.py has no `__all__`, so any public name is re-exported automatically — no extra wiring. Verify with the `otel_capture` fixture: `[s for s in otel_capture.get_finished_spans() if s.name == "dogfight.shot_narration_replay"]`. The `tests/server/test_dogfight_player_throw_roundtrip.py` file defines its own `otel_capture` (InMemorySpanExporter on the live provider) — reuse it.

### The dogfight maneuver-commit seam is the sealed-letter path, NOT dispatch_dice_throw (158-49)
The ADR-153 dogfight (`resolution_mode: sealed_letter_lookup`) commits a player MANEUVER through the narration BeatSelection path → `narration_apply.py:~6039` → `resolve_sealed_letter_lookup(enc, commits, table)` (sealed_letter.py), validated against `cdef.interaction_table.maneuvers_consumed` (the maneuver ids: `straight/bank/loop/kill_rotation` — NOT the loose "Throttle Up/Break Right" the playtest report uses). It does NOT go through `dispatch_dice_throw`. The gun ROLL is the only dogfight DICE_THROW, and the handler (`handlers/dice_throw.py`) consumes `pending_dogfight_shot` and `return`s at ~line 329 BEFORE reaching `dispatch_dice_throw` (~366) — so dispatch_dice_throw is NEVER reached for a dogfight in normal play. A beat arriving at dispatch_dice_throw for a `sealed_letter_lookup` cdef is therefore always a bug (158-49: the forced-dispatch dogfight handed the player the WN personal-combat menu and committing "attack" synthesized a STR strike → `without_number.attack_params` KeyError). Reject it LOUD there.
- **The dogfight menu source is `maneuvers_consumed`, not `cdef.beats`.** #508 dropped the vestigial `beats:` list off the live space_opera dogfight ("the dogfight resolves via the interaction table's maneuvers_consumed, never a beats list"). So `beats_available_for` must SYNTHESIZE display-only maneuver beats (id + label, NO `kind`/`stat_check` — both Optional on `BeatDef`) from `confrontation.interaction_table.maneuvers_consumed` for a `sealed_letter_lookup` cdef, and RETURN before the `is_wn_binding and win_condition=="hp_depletion"` WN personal-combat synthesis (that block ignores `resolution_mode`, which is the whole bug). Gate the new branch on `resolution_mode == ResolutionMode.sealed_letter_lookup` ALONE (maneuvers are ruleset-agnostic); the `beat_selection` ground-combat WN menu is untouched.
- **Pre-existing stale tests:** `tests/genre/test_dogfight_content_loading.py::test_dogfight_beats_cover_every_consumed_maneuver` and `::test_dogfight_has_dual_track_metrics` assert the PRE-sealed-letter dogfight shape (a `beats:` list covering the maneuvers; dual-track metrics) and were ALREADY RED on develop after #508 dropped beats + made it hp_depletion. They are NOT yours; log as a finding, don't fix in-scope. The full-suite "52 reds" on this branch were ALL pre-existing — verify by `git stash`-ing the production diff and re-running the suspicious failures (arc/tension/embedding/turn-wiring/wwn-cast clusters are environmental, per the 158-51 session note).

### "Pre-existing failure" claims need a without-my-changes bisect — but NEVER via git stash (2026-07-02, 158-43)
- The GREEN full-suite run showed 14 failures the runner labeled pre-existing — but 5 of them (PoolClosed: `psycopg_pool.PoolClosed: the pool 'sidequest-save' is already closed`) sat in files driving the exact `apply_world_patch` block I'd modified. Verification that actually proves pre-existence: re-run JUST the failing subset against the tree WITHOUT the change (parallel AND `-n0`). All 14 reproduced without it. The PoolClosed five pass in any smaller batch and only fail in the full ~14.8k-test 28-worker run — DB pool lifecycle under extreme xdist concurrency, not a code path. Don't chase PoolClosed from a full-suite run before re-running the test alone.
- **DO NOT use `git stash` for the bisect** (project rule — see `/pf-chore`: "CRITICAL: Never use git stash"; it can silently lose/revert work, and it nearly bit here: stash in sidequest-server + a later `cd` to the orchestrator repo made `git stash pop` report "No stash entries found" from the wrong repo). Safe alternatives: `git worktree add` a clean checkout of the base commit and run the subset there, or `git diff > /tmp/x.patch && git checkout -- .` then `git apply /tmp/x.patch` to restore — or just commit first and test `HEAD~1` in a worktree.
- **Pyright + `**dict` unpack into a span helper:** passing `**extra_attrs` (a `dict[str, str]`) into a signature ending `_tracer: Tracer | None = None, **attrs: Any` fails pyright — it can't prove the dict doesn't contain `_tracer`. Pass the attr explicitly (`pc_name=pc_name`) instead of building a conditional kwargs dict.

### `PoolClosed("the pool 'sidequest-save' is already closed")` in unrelated tests = a leaked frontier observer, not a DB bug (162-1, 2026-07-05)
- **Signature:** seam/region/narration tests fail in the full parallel suite with `PoolClosed` inside a dispatch (`subsystems.dispatch_failed subsystem=movement ... PoolClosed`), pass in isolation, and the VICTIM SET SHIFTS between runs (adding/deleting test files changes xdist worker distribution). Don't chase the victims — they're whoever ran after the polluter on that worker.
- **Mechanism:** `attach_dungeon_to_session` registers an expansion-quest observer into the process-global `frontier_hook._OBSERVERS`; it closes over the session's `PgDungeonRepository` (and its pool object). The mirrored unregister lives ONLY in `detach_dungeon_from_session`. A test that attaches and never detaches leaks the observer; a later module fixture calls `db_pool.close_pool()` (which nulls the singleton — a FRESH `get_pool()` is fine) but the leaked observer still holds the closed pool object. Every later region transition on that worker fires it.
- **Fix shape:** conftest autouse snapshot/restore of `frontier_hook._OBSERVERS` + `session_integration._ATTACHED_SAVES` (`tests/conftest.py::_isolate_frontier_observers`) — confines any leak to the leaking test. General lesson: any process-global observer/registry with a register-in-A / unregister-in-B lifecycle WILL leak in tests; give it a conftest snapshot fixture the day it's born.
- **Forensics:** capture the full-suite log yourself (`uv run pytest -q --tb=long > log 2>&1`) — testing-runner subagents summarize tracebacks away and their context is lost after they exit; the bottom frames of the traceback named the exact cached-object chain (`expansion_quest.reconcile_dungeon_quests_into_log → PgDungeonRepository.open_threads → self._pool.connection()`).

### Monkeypatching a module attribute is invisible to module-scope direct importers — usable as a FEATURE (162-1, 2026-07-05)
- `monkeypatch.setattr(pregen, "seed_manual", wrapper)` only affects call sites that resolve `pregen.seed_manual` at call time (e.g. `ensure_loaded`'s function-local `from ... import seed_manual`, which re-resolves per call). A test file with top-level `from ...pregen import seed_manual` bound the REAL function at import — the patch never touches it.
- 162-1 exploited this deliberately: the suite-wide tolerant `seed_manual` wrapper (swallows `EncounterSeedError` so tests binding unseedable synthetic combos don't crash at session bind) structurally CANNOT mask the 90-5 fail-loud unit tests, because those all call via module-scope direct import. No path-based exemption needed. Check the importer style before assuming a conftest patch covers (or spares) a caller.

### A flag/field added for a spec forensic needs a PRODUCTION producer, or it's a dead letter (162-1, 2026-07-05)
- `ManualNpc.authored` existed, the cap-eviction honored it, TEA's tests passed `authored=True` directly — and NO production caller set it. `_seed_authored_npcs` (the only authored-cast producer) called `add_npc(...)` without the flag, so the V3 authored-discard forensic would always report 0 and an authored insert at cap would be REFUSED (the exact spec failure). GREEN-phase grep discipline: for every new field/flag, grep for producers passing a non-default value from a production path; also decide the LEGACY story (162-1 upserts the flag on exact-name match so pre-existing on-disk manuals self-heal).

### 162-2 typed Origin GREEN phase (2026-07-05)
- **testing-runner can overstep its report-only lane and EDIT production code.** On the 162-2 GREEN run it silently patched `monster_manual_inject.inject()` (added a name-leg to the dedup) to fix `test_inject_region_population_authored_name_wins_dedup`. The fix was behaviorally correct, but review any `fix_applied` block in a runner result as if it were your own diff — you own it at commit time. Its comment mislabeled which dedup leg does what; corrected in place.
- **Id-keyed dedup at the inject seam MUST keep the name leg.** `identity_key`-only dedup regresses same-display-name/different-creature_id pairs: both patches land, then `apply_world_patch` merges BY NAME downstream and the region-pop patch's fields clobber the authored patch. The dedup filter needs `identity_key(p) not in authored_keys AND normalize_name(p.name) not in authored_names` — the id leg catches display-name drift, the name leg preserves pre-162-2 same-name dominance. (Pinned by the existing `test_inject_region_population_authored_name_wins_dedup` + new `test_name_drifted_procedural_counterpart_dedups_on_creature_id`.)
- **`sidequest.game.origin` ↔ `session.py` cycle: origin.py imports `Npc` under TYPE_CHECKING only** (same pattern as `npc_pool.py`); session.py imports `Origin` at runtime for the field. Any future origin-module helper that needs runtime session types must live elsewhere or late-import.
- **Never run bare `ruff format .` mid-story** — it reformatted 49 files (~40 unrelated, committed unformatted by earlier work), polluting the story diff. Format the explicit story file list; `git checkout --` the collateral. Also: zsh does NOT word-split `$FILES` — pass explicit file lists, not a space-joined variable.
- **Pyright baseline on these files is 19 pre-existing errors** (encounter_lifecycle seeder cluster + session/world_materialization) — verify your diff adds zero by stash-compare, don't chase the baseline. The repo gate is ruff+pytest, not pyright-clean.

### 162-5 content reconciliation GREEN: world bestiary is world-REPLACES-genre, so the world roster must be self-complete; the reconcile forces "match", not "strip" (2026-07-05)
- **`effective_bestiary` is world-REPLACES-genre** (`genre/models/pack.py:540`): a world with its own `bestiary.yaml` shadows the genre-root pool ENTIRELY (mutant_wasteland's 14 generic foes never merge in for flickering_reach). So an encounter ref can only resolve to a WORLD-tier bestiary id — you can't lean on genre-floor generics. When encounter tables reference more creatures than the world bestiary carries, the fix is: remap old-name synonyms to canonical ids + expand the world bestiary (author the missing ones), NOT map to genre floor. Same shape will recur in 162-6 (space_opera de-triplication).
- **Promoting a creatures.yaml ecology entry to the bestiary = author the bestiary-only fields, share the combat numbers verbatim.** creatures.yaml carries `hp/max_hp/ac/damage` (+ ecology: wound/niche/relationships/loot); the strict `Bestiary` model needs `level/hp/armor_class/attack_bonus` (+ optional damage/role/abilities as `list[str]`, NOT the ecology `list[{name,description}]`). Set the bestiary entry's `hp/ac/damage` EQUAL to the creatures.yaml entry's so the AC2 shared-id divergence test passes for the promoted pair with zero edits on the creatures side. Author `level`/`attack_bonus`/`save`/`morale` off the existing AWN foe ladder in the file (L1→+2/hp4, L2→+3/hp9, L3→+4/hp13, L4→+5-6/hp18, L5→+8/hp22, L6→+9/hp27) — that's content authoring per the declared ladder, not homebrew balancing (Bind-the-Ruleset stays intact).
- **When V4=YES (runtime reads creatures.yaml), reconcile the numbers — do NOT strip the stat fields.** The V4 behavioral test builds an EnemyBlock from creatures.yaml via `_collect_creatures_from_yaml`+`creature_to_enemy_block` and asserts `enemy.hp == bestiary.hp`; `creature_to_enemy_block` defaults absent `hp`→4/`ac`→10, so a stripped creatures.yaml spawns hp-4 husks and the test stays RED. Set creatures.yaml `hp/max_hp/ac` to the bestiary values and change the damage DICE PREFIX to match (the test compares only the leading `NdN[+-N]` token, so keep the flavor parenthetical: `"2d6 (glass tendril, slashing + radiation burn)"`).
- **creatures.yaml stat blocks are a contiguous `hp/max_hp/ac/damage` 4-line run, unique per creature via the damage flavor string** — anchor a 4-line Edit on the whole block (hp:1/max_hp:1 alone collides between signal_chorus and echo_frog; the ac + damage lines disambiguate). BSD `sed` on macOS has no `\s`; extracting ids with `sed -E 's/.*id:\s*//'` leaves a leading space and breaks a later `comm` diff — use `grep -oE '\- id: [a-z_]+' | awk '{print $3}'`.

### 162-5 rework: an ID-remap that passes the resolve-test still ships incoherent narration — rewrite the `situation:` prose too (2026-07-05)
- Reviewer (correctly) REJECTed the first 162-5 GREEN: consolidating encounter refs onto a bestiary id (glass_crawler/rust_louse/alkali_worm→dust_drinker, dust_strider→glass_touched_mount) passed AC1 (refs ⊆ bestiary) but left the `situation:`/`behavior:` prose naming the OLD creature — the narrator would describe "a colony of rust lice" and spawn a sacred limbless dust-bladder. The remap-to-existing move is only half the fix; the other half is rewriting every consolidated encounter's prose to describe what actually spawns. No automated test catches this (prose↔id coherence is a human-review concern) — so do it in the same pass, don't wait for the reject.
- Corollary caught in the same review: reconciling a "harmless"/"Physically helpless"/"Not an encounter" ecology creature (echo_frog, dust_drinker) to real bestiary combat stats makes its own creatures.yaml prose self-contradict. When you bump the numbers, sweep the `weaknesses`/`notes`/`description` prose in the same entry. And a remap can be simply WRONG: thorn_singer (a sessile "pruning/tree/bone-pile" lure) fit tangle_mouth, not the mobile peace_speaker I first chose — match the encounter's BEHAVIOR (sessile trap vs mobile hunter vs lure) to the bestiary creature's, not just the region/wound.

So the genre root is that file with a corrected header. coyote_star's file = same 12 + a world-specific `generics:` block, so it CANNOT collapse (whole-file-replace: deleting it loses the generics; hoisting them to genre leaks them into every falling-through world).
