### 158-55 rework: No-Silent-Fallbacks for a fixture that snapshots library-private internals (2026-07-08)
- A fixture reaching into OTel private state (`provider._active_span_processor._span_processors`) with blanket `getattr(x, "_priv", None)` **silently no-ops forever** the day OpenTelemetry renames that attr — reintroducing the exact leak the fixture fixes, invisibly. The Reviewer flagged this as the story's own thesis-violation (a determinism story shipping a silent degradation).
- Correct shape = **tolerate/raise split gated on the concrete SDK type**: `isinstance(provider, sdk.trace.TracerProvider)` → internals MUST be present, else `raise` (renamed → fail loud); the bare API `ProxyTracerProvider` (no SDK installed) owns no processor set → tolerate (`processors_before = None`, restore no-ops). Don't blanket-assert `processors_before is not None` unconditionally — that false-fails the legitimate proxy case suite-wide.
- **Keep the teardown re-fetch guard.** The Reviewer's exact surgical prescription (session line ~286) was "setup-time assert, NOT blanket removal of both guards — the teardown `active_after is not None` guard is legitimate (a test may reset to the API proxy)." I first restructured teardown to restore the setup-captured object; reverted to the re-fetch to match the Reviewer's stated model. When the Reviewer writes the fix shape into the session audit, implement THAT shape — a cleaner-but-different equivalent invites a Round 3 debate for no behavioral gain.
- Verifying an **autouse** fixture change: run the FULL suite (`uv run pytest -q`, ~100s → 14674 passed here), not just the story file — an autouse fixture runs around every test, so its blast radius is the whole suite. `-n auto` AND `-n0` both, to prove the fix isn't xdist-scheduler-dependent.

### Mapping Track C tasks 4-5 (165-2): the plan's tasks-4+ impl is CLEAN — the "plan has bugs" warning was scoped to C1 (2026-07-09, 165-2)
- **The 165-1 carryover's blanket "the plan doc's embedded code has bugs, hand-verify" applies to the C1 tasks 0-3 (mask mislabel + `cells_reachable` cost gap) — those are already FIXED in the merged `sidequest/game/tactical/adjudication.py`.** Tasks 4-5 (`seating.py`, WN binding) DELEGATE to that merged C1, so their embedded impls transcribed cleanly and passed all 15 TEA tests unchanged. Still trace each value, but don't rewrite the tasks-4+ code from scratch expecting bugs; the delegation is the point (`adjudicate_tactical_move`/`adjudicate_tactical_reach` just call `adjudicate_move`/`adjudicate_reach`). Expect the same for 165-3/4/5's plan tasks 6-13.
- **`combat_move_cells` floors metres→cells as `max(1, int(move_m / METERS_PER_CELL))`** — the `max(1, ...)` is load-bearing: `int(1/1.5)=0` would otherwise deny all movement for a 1 m/turn stock. And `getattr(core, "move", None) or DEFAULT_MOVE_METERS` means a `move=0` (or None) core silently takes the 10 m default (a falsy-`or` fallback; fine for the tests, flagged upstream as a No-Silent-Fallbacks question — don't "fix" it in GREEN without a failing test).
- **`without_number.py` carries 3 PRE-EXISTING pyright errors** (stat_modifier `str|None`, `assign_attributes` override incompatibility, `commit_effort` duration `str` vs `EffortDuration` Literal) at the file's own lines — NOT yours if you only add to it. Prove it: `git show develop:sidequest/game/ruleset/without_number.py > /tmp/base.py && uv run pyright /tmp/base.py` → same 3 errors (line numbers shift by however many lines you inserted). Don't try to clear them; they're out of scope and the gate/Reviewer accepts an unchanged baseline count.
- **Annotate new methods on this file with real types even though the plan left the adjudicators bare** — the file has `from __future__ import annotations`, so a `TYPE_CHECKING` import of `MoveAdjudication`/`RangeAdjudication` + inline `tuple[int,int]`/`list[tuple[int,int]]`/`frozenset[tuple[int,int]]` satisfies lang-review #3 with zero runtime import cost (keep the `adjudicate_move`/`adjudicate_reach` import local inside the method — no cycle, matches the module's lazy style).

### Mapping Track C tasks 6-8 (165-3): TWO plan/reality plumbing gaps + the range_band scope boundary (2026-07-09, 165-3)
- **The plan claimed `dispatch_dice_throw` already has the mask-load plumbing ("both already present in dice.py") — FALSE.** `dispatch_dice_throw` and `GameSnapshot` carry NO `dungeon_store`/mask access (grepped: zero hits). The store is only reachable via `sd.dungeon_store` (map_emit.py pattern). Resolution: added an optional `dungeon_store: object | None = None` param to `dispatch_dice_throw` AND plumbed it from the ONE production caller (`handlers/dice_throw.py:366`, which has `sd`) as `dungeon_store=getattr(sd, "dungeon_store", None)`. Without updating the caller the gate would be permanently dead (mask always None → always skip) — the exact half-wired trap CLAUDE.md forbids. `_resolve_room_mask(snapshot, dungeon_store, character_name)` decodes `load_masks()[room_id]["mask_bytes_b64"]` (base64 → ASCII newline mask).
- **Same gap in Task 8's chokepoint** `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` — no `dungeon_store` in scope. Added the same optional kwarg. The function has TWO `created_turn` stamps (table path ~1921, combat path ~2638); grid seating belongs ONLY before the combat `return enc` (poker/auction tables aren't tactical). `_seat_tactical_cells(...)` guards: dungeon_store None → clean no-op; store present + no `tactical` block → span `seated_count=0` (honest); block present → `seat_actor_cells` + span.
- **B1 range_band SCOPE at the dispatch site: `spec=None` → MELEE only.** `resolve_damage` returns a `DamageSpec` which has NO `range_band` field (only `CatalogItem` does). So the reach gate at the dispatch site can't read a band off the resolved spec → passing `spec=None` enforces melee reach (the plan's safe default; "can I even hit this" is the load-bearing question). `weapon_range_cells` DOES now parse the real `"N/N"` format (unit-tested) — but nothing feeds it a band-carrying spec from dispatch yet. Ranged/LOS range enforcement needs the equipped weapon's `CatalogItem.range_band` plumbed to the dispatch site (filed as a non-blocking Delivery Finding → a 165-4-ish follow-up). Do NOT try to resolve the CatalogItem inside `dispatch_dice_throw` for this story.
- **`_range_band_to_cells` derives from the LONG range** (`int(long_m / METERS_PER_CELL)`, min 1) — the max distance a shot can still land. Keeps categorical SRD keys (`rifle`/`near`/…) as a back-compat branch so the 165-2 categorical tests stay green; only a value that is neither `"N/N"` nor a known key raises (No Silent Fallbacks).
- **`seat_actor_cells` collision fix = side-priority over a SHARED occupancy `used` set**, not per-side index counters. Serve players → opponents → neutrals (stable within side); players prefer entrance then spill to creature, others prefer creature then spill. This is collision-free AND stops a neutral pre-empting a monster's anchor. The old `player_cells = entrance + creature` / `opponent_cells = creature` with independent `pi`/`oi` counters put PlayerB-overflow and Opp1 on the same creature cell.
- **Known PRE-EXISTING failure in this area (do not chase):** `tests/server/dispatch/test_monster_manual_inject.py::test_execute_narration_turn_refreshes_stale_monster_manual` fails on a `CartographyTreatmentWire` pydantic ValidationError (163-1 map-treatment layer) + LLM-transport hermeticity (real claude-agent-sdk reached with dev credentials present). Confirmed failing on the pre-change baseline via `git stash`. Unrelated to tactical work.

### 165-3 UPDATE — pre-review made the gate live end-to-end (supersedes the "ranged/seating are follow-ups" note above) (2026-07-09, 165-3)
- **The pre-handoff code review caught that the gate was INERT in production**: the seating half (`_seat_tactical_cells`) only ran when `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` got a `dungeon_store`, but the LIVE callers `run_confrontation_dispatch` / `run_dogfight_dispatch` never passed one → no cells ever seated → `_enforce_tactical_reach` always hit `attacker_unseated` and skipped. Classic half-wired trap. Handing that to the review phase would have bounced it.
- **The fix was cheap because `run_dispatch_bank` uses `_filter_context_for_callable(fn, context)`** — it auto-passes any `context` key whose name matches a param the subsystem function DECLARES. `dungeon_store` was ALREADY in the intent-router-pass context dict (`execute_intent_router_pre_narrator_pass` has the param; the context at ~line 1180 already sets `"dungeon_store": dungeon_store`). So the ONLY change needed was adding `dungeon_store: Any | None = None` to `run_confrontation_dispatch` + `run_dogfight_dispatch` signatures and forwarding it to `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger`. No dispatch-bank or intent-router changes. **Lesson: to plumb a session resource into a subsystem dispatcher, just declare the param — the context-filter wires it, IF the resource is already in the bank context.**
- **Ranged false-denial fixed by surfacing the real range_band, not by deferring.** `resolve_weapon_range_band_from_beat_and_actor` (combat_rules.py) mirrors the priority-3 CatalogItem lookup in `resolve_damage_spec_from_beat_and_actor` and returns `CatalogItem.range_band` (the only place it lives). The gate builds `SimpleNamespace(range_band=<resolved>)` and feeds `weapon_range_cells` — melee weapon → None → melee reach; ranged weapon → its `"N/N"` band → large room-scale cap (LOS-bound). This ALSO made BLOCKER 1's `_NN_RANGE_RE` branch reachable in production (it was dead-code-until-then). Caveat: resolves the FIRST equipped weapon carrying a band, so a melee actor with a holstered gun reads as ranged (a lenient false-ALLOW, not a false-deny — acceptable, no regression vs no-gate).
- **Scope the strike gate to `cdef.win_condition == "hp_depletion"`** (string compare in dice.py, no enum import) — else a WN social/chase skill check in a tactical room gets melee-reach-gated. The `wn_sealed_round` gate nearby already uses the same string check.
- **`_filter_context_for_callable` verified via a spy test**: `monkeypatch.setattr(conf, "instantiate_encounter_from_trigger", spy)` + `await run_confrontation_dispatch(..., dungeon_store=store)` and assert `spy` received `dungeon_store`. `SubsystemDispatch` requires `idempotency_key` (str) + `confidence` (float) — both no-default, so a bare `SubsystemDispatch(subsystem=, params=)` raises 2 pydantic missing-field errors.

### 165-3 REWORK green: the dead-store-attr fix + observability-only descope (2026-07-09, 165-3)
- **`getattr(sd, "dungeon_store", None)` was a DEAD attribute — the store lives on `sd.lookahead_handle.persistence`.** `_SessionData` has no `dungeon_store` field and NOTHING in `sidequest/` assigns `sd.dungeon_store =` (grep it). The prod resolution pattern is `websocket_session_handler.py:985-987`: `_lh = getattr(sd, "lookahead_handle", None); store = _lh.persistence if _lh is not None else None`. `LookaheadWorkerHandle.persistence` is a required non-Optional `DungeonStore`, so `.persistence` never AttributeErrors when the handle is non-None. My round-1 DICE_THROW handler copied the dead pattern; the fix mirrors the movement-subsystem resolution. **The SAME dead `getattr(sd,"dungeon_store",None)` still lives in `map_emit.py:174` (player-facing grid emit) and `websocket_session_handler.py:1622` (setpiece attach)** — both resolve None in prod. The reach gate now reads the LIVE store while the TACTICAL_GRID the player sees is emitted from the DEAD one; they must be unified (Delivery Finding, follow-up — `map_emit.py`'s shape is grep-pinned by `test_setpiece_attach_wiring.py` pending Plan 7).
- **Observability-only descope = call the gate for its span side-effects, DISCARD the verdict.** Drop the `_reach_verdict = ` assignment AND the `if not in_range: raise` — just `_enforce_tactical_reach(...)` bare. The function still emits validated/denied/skipped spans internally, so the GM panel still sees reach; keep it RETURNING the verdict (dead at the sole call site today) so the future move-verb story can consume it without re-plumbing. `DiceDispatchError` is still raised ~20× elsewhere in dice.py, so its import is not orphaned. Pin BOTH "no abort" AND "span still fires" in the test so nobody "fixes" a softlock by disabling the gate.
- **Adding type annotations to the tactical chokepoints is a SCOPE TRAP — it surfaces 7 latent pyright errors, defer it.** Annotating `_enforce_tactical_reach`/`_resolve_room_mask`/`_seat_tactical_cells` (reviewer lang-review #3) breaks pyright two ways: (1) `dungeon_store: object | None` → `object` has no `.load_masks()`; the store is duck-typed and wants a `DungeonStore`/Protocol, but the seam passes `object | None` so a precise type cascades casts to every caller; (2) annotating `actor: EncounterActor` + `mask: str | None` makes pyright compute real types for `a_cell = actor.per_actor_state.get("cell")` and `mask`, then the INDIRECT `skip_reason` guard (a computed variable, not `if a_cell is None: return`) defeats narrowing → `a_cell[0]` "None not subscriptable" + "str|None not assignable to mask: str". The code is correct (the guard guarantees non-None); pyright just can't follow it. Proper fix = refactor the guard to explicit early-returns + type the store as a Protocol — a real refactor, out of an observability-only rework's scope. `from __future__ import annotations` is on, so use unquoted forward-refs (ruff UP037 flags quoted ones). Baseline these files at 38 pyright errors and keep the diff pyright-NEUTRAL; leave #3 as a non-blocking Delivery Finding.

### 165-4 GREEN: additive protocol echoes + UI math display (2026-07-09, 165-4, plan Tasks 9–10)
Implemented the additive echo layer (server `TacticalAdjudication` + `TacticalGridPayload.adjudications` + `DiceResultPayload.range_band/distance_cells`; UI denial banner + move chip + range readout). 422 pass / 0 fail, ruff+eslint clean, pyright+tsc 0 errors. Gotchas:
- **`DiceResultPayload` is in `sidequest/protocol/dice.py:214`, NOT `protocol/models.py`** (the plan's Task 9 file list was wrong — TEA flagged it, I confirmed by implementing there). `TacticalAdjudication` + `TacticalGridPayload.adjudications` DO go in models.py. Additive fields go right after `roll_role`, before the `_require_face_count_matches_pool` model_validator.

A `hasattr(ruleset, "combat_move_cells")` runtime guard is safe but pyright STILL errors on `ruleset.combat_move_cells(...)` ("Attribute unknown for RulesetModule"), and then the call result is typed `object` → a second error at the `cells_budget: int|None` param. Fix pattern: `fn = getattr(ruleset, "combat_move_cells", None); if callable(fn): cells_budget=cast("int", fn(core))`. Both the getattr indirection AND the cast are needed. The wire parser maps `p.adjudications ?? []` so it's always a real array at runtime; the renderer reads `grid.adjudications ?? []`. Additive UI fields stay optional so old fixtures type-check.

My tests pass because the fixture (`tactical_emit_fixtures`) sets `sd.dungeon_store` directly. The echo SHAPE + UI render are correct and complete (my story's scope), but the DELIVERY inherits the pre-existing dead-store gap. ### 164-3 Track B site cutover: the movement seam ladder → SiteRegistry × enter/exit resolvers (2026-07-09)
- **The site path is `action`-driven, not `direction`-driven.** The router now emits `params={"action":"enter_site","site_descriptor":"…"}` / `{"action":"exit_site"}`; the movement dispatch resolves site kinds BY KIND via `SiteRegistry` (never `Route.to_id`). The old region-mode seam rungs (`surface_descent`/`surface_descent_adjacent`/`surface_ascent`, `movement.py:386-538`) were DELETED. In-scene graph nav (§Q1) and lateral cartography travel keep the `direction` vocabulary. `resolved_via` → `site_enter`/`site_exit`, spanned as `site.enter`/`site.exit` (NOT `movement.resolved`+seam_kind).
- **The frontier-legacy `graph.entrance_id` fallback is what preserves `to_region` across the cutover — and every store DOUBLE must model it.** Sünden's frontier keeps un-namespaced legacy node ids (`entrance`, not `frontier:entrance`) for B1 (storage is `(session, site_id)`-keyed; node-id namespacing is a B4 follow-up). So `resolve_enter_site` must prefer `graph.entrance_id` when the declared `site.entrance_node_id` is absent (guarded: the fallback target must itself be a real node, else raise `no_site_entrance`). In tests, the store double MUST return `RegionGraph(entrance_id=ENTRANCE_ID)` and IGNORE the requested namespaced entrance_id — otherwise it binds to `frontier:entrance` and `to_region != ENTRANCE_ID`. Membership of a legacy node on the EXIT path is detected via `is_procedural_region_id(from_region)` → `SiteRegistry.by_id(DEFAULT_SITE_ID)` (import `DEFAULT_SITE_ID` LAZILY — `game/pg/dungeon.py` pulls psycopg; don't drag it onto the movement hot-path import graph).
- **Cutover blast radius: ~21 pre-existing region-mode-seam tests encode the OLD contract and must be retargeted (plan says "same behavioral contract").** `grep -rln "surface_descent\|surface_ascent\|surface_descent_adjacent" tests/` finds them: seam_crossing, surface_ascent, party_split_158_7, integration/seam_crossing_wiring. Translate `_movement("deeper", "down the rope")` → `_enter("the deep")` (action=enter_site), `_movement("toward_exit", …)` at entrance → `_exit()`, keep the `to_region` assertions, and swap span assertions `movement.resolved`+seam_kind → `site.enter`/`site.exit`. **The co-located peer advance is unchanged** (`_advance_colocated_peers` still emits `movement.resolved` per peer with `party_advance`/`anchor_pc`) — only the ACTING PC's span type changes. 164-4 (DUNGEON_MAP→SITE_MAP) will have the same shape of blast radius.
- **`SiteRegistry.resolve_descriptor` is SUBSTRING-on-NAME (164-1) — brittle.** A test/router descriptor must reference the site NAME ("The Deep"); a seam-route phrasing ("down the rope") will NOT match and the enter defers. Parametrize crossing tests with site-referencing descriptors (`"the deep"`, `"down into the deep"`, or `""` which resolves the sole enterable site). REAL-ROUTER risk flagged for the e2e: if the router echoes "down the rope", the descent regresses — watch `sunden_descend_trace`.
- **Full-suite heads-up (not yours): 51 pre-existing `CartographyTreatmentWire` MagicMock `ValidationError`s at `session_helpers.py:1695`** (163-1 map-treatment layer) fail on clean develop. Prove your diff is innocent by `git stash`-ing and re-running a sample serially before blaming your change. `test_pregen_bestiary_90_1[evropi]` remains a known `-n auto` xdist flake.

### 163-7: the recurring `CartographyTreatmentWire` MagicMock failure is FIXED — one line in `session_fixture` (2026-07-09)
- **The "51 pre-existing CartographyTreatmentWire ValidationErrors" that 164-3 / 165-3 kept flagging as "not yours" are now RESOLVED.** They were all one root cause: `_build_cartography_map_message` does `world = pack.worlds.get(world_slug); mt = getattr(world, "map_treatment", None); if mt is not None: CartographyTreatmentWire(kind=mt.treatment, node_anchors=mt.node_anchors, style_hints=mt.style_hints)` (163-1 map-treatment layer). Every failing test drove a turn through `tests/server/conftest.py::session_fixture`, whose `genre_pack` is a bare `MagicMock` — so `pack.worlds.get(...)` returns an auto-mock world whose `.map_treatment` is a TRUTHY auto-mock, the `if` fires, and pydantic rejects the MagicMock kind/node_anchors/style_hints. Production is fine (real worlds return a real `MapTreatment` or `None`); it was purely a stale fixture that 163-1 never updated.
- **Fix = `sd.genre_pack.worlds.get.return_value.map_treatment = None` after the `_SessionData(...)` construction in `session_fixture`.** Pins ONLY that attribute; every other world access stays auto-mocked, so the room-binding / portrait paths that 153-23 etc. rely on are byte-for-byte unchanged (this is why the surgical pin beats `worlds = {}`, which would flip `pack.worlds.get(...)` to `None` and alter those other paths). `getattr(mock, "map_treatment", None)` can't use the default — a MagicMock never has a missing attr — so you MUST assign `None` explicitly. Same real-defaults pattern the fixture already uses for progression/rules/drama_thresholds/effective_bestiary. Cleared 47 failures (48→1); full suite 13483 passed / 1 failed, the lone remainder being the `beneath_sunden` per-room-binding CONTENT gap (`test_distinct_rooms_bind_distinct_creatures`, tracked as 158-63 — different repo, different problem, do NOT fold it in).
- **Lesson (recurring in this codebase — the fixture comment block already documents 4 prior instances):** when a new code path reads a NEW field off an object that mock-heavy fixtures flow through (`pack.*`, `world.*`, `genre_pack.*`), the shared conftest mock silently returns a truthy auto-mock and breaks every test that hits the path — usually via a pydantic `ValidationError` downstream, far from the fixture. When you add such a field access in production, grep `tests/server/conftest.py` for the mock that feeds it and pin the new field to its realistic default in the SAME change. If you see a wave of identical `ValidationError`s from a mock value, look for the newest field access on that object, not a logic bug.
### Mapping Track B 164-4 GREEN: scene-context + SITE_MAP cutover (2026-07-10, 164-4)
Both RISKY cutovers landed; full suite 14877 green (1 pre-existing content-baseline failure). Gotchas for the next Track B sibling:
- **CHECK ORIGIN FOR AN ABANDONED BRANCH BEFORE PUSHING a story branch.** `feat/164-4-scene-context-site-map-cutover` already existed on origin (`d57a24ce`, 2026-07-09) — a complete parallel RED+GREEN of THIS story from a session that died before PR/tracking (story was still `backlog`, no session archive). Push rejected; resolution: diff theirs vs mine (same design, older base), port their 2 unique coverage pins, push as `…-r2`, preserve the stale branch untouched, log a Conflict finding for Keith to delete post-merge. Never force-push over another session's unmerged work. A `git fetch origin <branch>` + `gh pr list --head <branch> --state all` costs seconds and tells you abandoned-vs-live.
- **The plan's Task 7 "generalize the runtime cavern fence at `_maybe_build_runtime_cavern_payload`" is STALE** — that function has no `applies_to` fence; it gates on `sd.dungeon_store`, which is DEAD in production (165-3 dead-gate caveat is in its docstring). Left untouched; `load_masks(site_id=…)` keying belongs to the store-unification follow-up. Third consecutive story where plan-doc code/text drifted from reality — always re-read the actual function before "replacing" a gate the plan names.
- **Palette loading generalizes with a SPECIFIC exception catch:** `load_theme_palette` raises `ThemePaletteMissingError` for a world without `themes/` — a NORMAL state for non-dungeon site worlds (tavern in spaghetti_western), so `_load_site_map_context` catches exactly that and hands `_build_site_map_payload` a null palette (every `get()` raises KeyError → the existing fail-soft display-by-region-id path). Do NOT blanket-except (No Silent Fallbacks) and do NOT loud-skip (it would fire every turn on every non-themed site world).
- **Legacy-entrance marker fallback:** namespaced site stores anchor `<site_id>:entrance`; the pre-namespacing Sünden frontier store anchors bare `entrance` (`seed_bootstrap.ENTRANCE_ID`). `_load_site_map_context` probes `site.entrance_node_id in graph.nodes` and falls back — that keeps `room_type=entrance` correct in the real-store wiring test without content migration. `load_map`'s `entrance_id` arg only seeds `RegionGraph.entrance_id`; membership is entirely `site_id`-keyed rows.
- **Blast radius as executed (~7 files, matching the 164-3 prediction):** descent-switch suite rewritten in place (kept filename; reasons `surface_phase`→`world_scene`, `deep_phase`→`site_scene`, label→SITE_MAP), per-PC suite (seam stub `_load_site_map_context(sd, site)` + `_FakeSessionData` gains frontier cartography + repo stub — scene resolution runs REAL even when the IO seam is stubbed), region-projection wiring (label/class only; its `_FakeSessionData.genre_pack` lazy-loads the REAL pack whose cartography now declares `frontier`, so scene resolution just worked), themes world-tier (loader rename + site arg), kind-registry relocation, enum wire-string. Nothing deleted, all behavioral intents preserved.
- **The UI is dark until 164-5:** the client still listens for `DUNGEON_MAP`; the renamed frame won't render until Task 9 lands. Don't playtest-gate the Map tab between 164-4 and 164-5.

## 2026-07-10 — 165-5: Task-13-style wiring goes in the helper that already holds the mask
The Track-C plan says "add the call in `instantiate_encounter_from_trigger` after `seat_actor_cells`" — but the mask load + seating live inside the `_seat_tactical_cells` helper, so the Fate zone projection extends THAT helper (new `pack` kwarg) rather than reloading the mask at the call site. Mask text decode is the `dice.py:_resolve_room_mask` one-liner: `base64.b64decode(mask_dict["mask_bytes_b64"]).decode("ascii")`. Also: a span that counts the *inputs* (zone_count) but not the *outcomes* (placed_count) is half a lie-detector — pre-handoff review caught that the projected span couldn't distinguish "2 zones, both actors placed" from "2 zones, none placed"; carry the outcome count on subsystem spans.

### 165-5 REWORK green: fail-loud via the typed-raise idiom, and a "dead" guard that's actually pyright-load-bearing (2026-07-10)
- **To convert a silent `return` guard to fail-loud AND keep pyright clean, use the `x if cond else _raise_missing_ruleset(...)` ternary idiom, not an `if cond: raise` statement.** `_raise_missing_ruleset(context) -> str` is typed to return `str` but always raises; putting it in the `else` of the assignment makes `ruleset_slug` type `str` directly, so the later `get_ruleset_module(ruleset_slug)` needs no None-narrowing. An `if ruleset_slug is None: raise` statement leaves the declared type `str | None` unless pyright can prove the raise is unreachable-after. This is the same idiom already used at `encounter_lifecycle.py:1934,2606`. Match the test's `pytest.raises(ValueError, match="ruleset")` / `match="mask_bytes_b64")` by putting those exact substrings in the messages.
- **Don't delete a "runtime-dead" `if mask_dict else None` branch just because a reviewer flags it — check whether it's pyright-load-bearing first.** In `_seat_tactical_cells`, `mask_b64 = mask_dict.get("mask_bytes_b64") if mask_dict else None` is genuinely unreachable-false at runtime (the `if not tactical_block: return` above guarantees `mask_dict` is a non-empty dict). BUT `mask_dict` is typed `dict | None` and pyright can't trace the `tactical_block`-truthy→`mask_dict`-truthy chain, so removing the `if mask_dict` guard introduces a NEW `reportOptionalMemberAccess` error. The "dead" branch is doing type-narrowing work. Kept it; noted the reviewer's finding as a false positive.
- **Fixing "unknown zone id returns free" ([H]): validate membership BEFORE the same-zone shortcut.** `adjudicate_zone_move` had `adjacent = to_zone == from_zone or to_zone in adjacency.get(...)` — the `to_zone == from_zone` short-circuit minted `free=True` for `z99→z99` (a zone the projection never heard of). Fix: `known = from_zone in projection.zones and to_zone in projection.zones; adjacent = known and (to_zone == from_zone or ...)`. `projection.zones` is the authoritative membership dict (adjacency is derived from it). Handles all four known/unknown × same/adjacent combos.
- **[G] totality flood is phantom-adjacency-safe for free** because the flood and the adjacency pass use the SAME 8-connected `neighbors()`: a cell left unassigned by the multi-source BFS cannot be `neighbors`-adjacent to any seed-assigned cell (it'd have been claimed), so a disconnected all-choke component is `neighbors`-isolated and the adjacency pass can't mint a cross-wall edge. The whole-mask all-choke corridor still degenerates to a single `z0` (zid starts at 0 with no seeds), losslessly subsuming the deleted degenerate special-case.
- **⚠️ Pre-existing STASH CONFLICT in this sidecar file (`.pennyfarthing/sidecars/dev-gotchas.md`) — resolved this round by keeping BOTH sides.** The file arrived with `<<<<<<< Updated upstream` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> Stashed changes` markers straddling the 165-4-GREEN block (upstream) and the 164-3 + 163-7 blocks (stashed). Both are additive per-story gotchas, so the correct resolution removes only the 3 marker lines and keeps all content. If you see this pattern again, keep both — sidecars are append-only knowledge, never pick a side.

## 2026-07-13 — Green Room big-bang (ADR-156, SDD run)

- **Orphaned readiness polls are a self-DoS.** A background `until curl ...; do sleep 1; done` loop survived its sibling's TaskStop and hammered `/api/debug/state` for 23h, wedging every fresh server at boot (that endpoint sync-scans all 1,849 saves ON the event loop — load+migrate+deepcopy+span each). Diagnosis cost 3 server kills + 2 sudo py-spy dumps. Rules: (1) when killing a poll loop, `ps aux | grep curl` for its siblings; (2) never poll `/api/debug/state` or `/api/debug/saves` for readiness — use `/health` (trivial handler); (3) `sample`/py-spy the LISTEN pid (`lsof -sTCP:LISTEN -t`), not the first pid on the port (you'll sample your own curl).
- **When a plan replaces a merge/upsert, audit what the OLD merge overwrote.** ADR-156's "fill absent fields only" spec never inventoried `_merge_npc_patch`'s overwrites — the dropped per-turn location/region refresh froze every re-injected NPC's zone (caught only at whole-branch review, empirically). Same class: identity writes (origin/creature_id stamps) onto persisted records need a trusted-id gate or they cache-poison identity keys forever (derive-don't-cache).
- **Plan test-vs-implementation contradictions surface in pre-flight, not mid-task.** The Task-1 two-tier arbitration test could not pass against the plan's own admit() sample (different identity keys never group). One batched pre-flight question ("which governs?") saved a blown implementer round.
- **Live verification finds content gaps engine tests can't.** flickering_reach authored no bestiary `generics:` section, so the target-first seat loud-failed exactly per contract — correct engine, unplayable scene. Worlds that want named-person brawls need generics rows (content#530 pattern).

### 166-10 green: renaming a string that is BOTH an entity id and a player-facing label (2026-07-13)

- **When a field is simultaneously an id and a label, don't choose — make it resolve two ways.** `EncounterActor.name` is what the panel renders AND what `find_actor` exact-matches (the UI hands it back as a tag target). Promoting a coal Other to its prose name looked like a fork: rename the payload only → the UI targets a name the engine can't resolve; rename in place → every reference stamped in an earlier turn dangles. The escape is an **alias ledger on the actor** + a **two-pass `find_actor`** (whole-list canonical pass, THEN whole-list alias pass — so one actor's canonical always outranks another's alias, mirroring `origin.resolve_roster_npc`). Rename in place, park the old string in `aliases`, and both names land on the same seat. Applies to any "rename the thing the UI keys on" story.
- **A rename is only safe once you've inventoried what holds the old string by EXACT match and outlives the rename.** In `StructuredEncounter` that is: `initiative[].token_id` (yes — the WN round walk keys on the actor name), `wn_commits[].actor/.target`, `fate_commits[].actor/.target`, `pending_defenses[].attacker/.defender`, `pending_compels[].target`, `tags[].created_by/.target`, `taunt.active_actor` (set from `actor.name` at `beat_kinds.py:1016`). `TableState` is NOT in scope — its seats live in a separate `seat_id` id space. Anything reached through `find_actor` is free once the alias leg exists; anything comparing strings directly must be repointed. One `_repoint_actor_name` sweep, returning a count that rides the OTEL span, so a dangling reference shows up as a `references_rewritten: 0` that should not be.
- **Verify a load-bearing claim in the handoff brief against the model, not the UI comment.** TEA's brief (quoting `ConfrontationOverlay.tsx:649-656`) warned that `last_beat_impacts` keys reference `actor.name`. The server model says otherwise — `last_beat_impacts: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]` is keyed by **actor side** ("player"/"opponent"), rebuilt each beat. The warning was harmless here, but a brief's landmine list is a hypothesis, not a finding; grep the field before you design around it.
- **The GENERIC-origin identity_key trap is real and quiet.** `identity_key` keys a GENERIC origin on the *normalized display name* (a `generics:` row is a stat donor, not an identity — `origin.py:101-118`), so "promoting" by rewriting `Npc.core.name` silently **forks** the identity that ADR-156 §6 exists to keep whole. The promotion has to be display-layer: `core.name` stays coal, the alias carries the diamond, and the *seat* is what gets renamed.
- **How to earn the words "pre-existing failure."** A subagent reporting "the 4 remaining failures are pre-existing on main" is a claim, not evidence. `git stash push` → run the **full suite** → compare failure NAMES → `git stash pop`. Baseline was 7 failures (my 3 RED + 4 others); post-change was exactly those same 4. That's a regression proof; "passes in isolation" is not (it can't see an ordering interaction your change introduced). Cost: ~2 min. The 4 known-noise tests are the ones already logged under "Known suite noise as of 2026-07-10" — that entry held up exactly.

### 166-10 REWORK: when the implementation needs a defensive sweep to survive a test, suspect the test (2026-07-13)

Round 1 I built exactly what the RED suite asked for — repoint `EncounterActor.name` to the narrator's prose name — and shipped an enemy the player **could not hit**. `find_creature_core` (`session.py`) exact-matches `Npc.core.name`, which the design deliberately keeps as coal to hold `identity_key` stable, so `apply_damage` / `wn_tools` / `apply_status` / `query_encounter` and the player's own dice throw all returned `not_found` for the name I put on the panel. The HP bar vanished with it. Rejected.

- **The tell I had and ignored.** I wrote in my own assessment: *"`EncounterActor.name` is simultaneously the label the panel renders and a load-bearing entity id."* That sentence is the bug report. The correct conclusion is **stop overloading the field** — add a label. Instead I concluded "make the field resolve two ways," and then wrote `_repoint_actor_name` (a 7-structure reference sweep) plus an alias ledger plus a two-pass `find_actor` to hold the overload together. **All of that defensive machinery was the smell.** ~50 lines of careful, correct code whose only job was to survive a bad premise. The rework **deleted every line of it** and came out 41 lines *smaller* than the thing it replaced.
- **The rule:** if making a change safe requires you to sweep N places, ask whether the change should be made at all. Real fixes usually delete code. A fix that needs a sweep is usually a fix in the wrong place.
- **Renaming an identifier: grep its CONSUMERS before you touch it, not after.** `grep -rn "== actor.name\|core.name ==\|find_.*(actor.name)" sidequest/` takes two minutes and would have handed me the answer. I swept the references *inside* `StructuredEncounter` and never asked what resolved the actor *outside* it. Inside-the-diff thinking. The consumers are where renames die.
- **The codebase had already fought this and left notes.** `encounter_lifecycle.py:438-446` (from a *prior* review's HIGH): *"a seat left under the prose alias is an unreachable opponent."* `ConfrontationOverlay.tsx`: *"a load-bearing entity id ... we humanize for DISPLAY only and never rewrite the id."* **Grep comments for the field name before designing against it.** Two greps, both free, both decisive.
- **Passing tests are not evidence of a working feature.** 8/8 green, ruff clean, pyright clean, preflight clean — on a feature that made the enemy invulnerable. The suite was green *because* it never left the blast radius (it drove only the two functions that changed). When a story touches a mechanical seam, drive the mechanical seam: promote the enemy, then **attack** it.
- **The corrected shape, for next time:** id vs label is a *type* distinction, not a naming convention. `name` (id, exact-match, never rewritten) + `display_name` (label, `display_name ?? name`, resolves nothing), and one `actorDisplayName()` helper at every render site so the roster and the panel can't drift apart. `find_creature_core` gets an alias-aware roster leg (`resolve_roster_npc`) so the narrator can target by the prose name — with the *character* leg checked first, so a PC can never be shadowed by an NPC alias.
